- **2026-10-04 · S0 is drawn online.** At a candidate's first check, S0 draws one of the window's check slots from the engine's seeded rng and enters at the first eligible check from there, with the same gates, risk and exits and no setup. The draw reads only the seed, so it cannot depend on outcomes. One engine run per seed.
- **2026-10-04 · The backtest judges each entry against a fresh trial account** (RISK-1 in backtest mode). Per-trade controls (R2, R4–R6, R12–R14) apply; portfolio controls (R7–R10) and R11 do not, because §14 takes every eligible candidate. Sizing counts the token-account rent as recoverable (the bot closes the account with its full exit, §9). Trade P&L is scored by the fill scenario: since fills-2 the conservative scenario returns the rent only when the final sell lands (see below). CORE-1 allows one entry in flight and no new entry while any intent is unresolved, so a candidate that meets a busy book waits for its next check.
- **2026-10-04 · The window and the holdout are fixed by configuration, never by the data downloaded.** The window is 2026-08-03 to 2026-10-01; practice days are its days before the holdout (to 2026-09-21), and the holdout runs from 2026-09-22 to the fixed end E (below). That lies entirely after B4 with the 2 h embargo; the plan refuses a holdout that starts before B4 or contains a market boundary (B2–B4). Walk-forward trades whose hold crosses a fold end or a regime boundary are purged; G1 is reported per universe and regime, with the pooled figure under its own label. n_power is simulated from the walk-forward of the holdout's regime (after B4). The study refuses to run unless every practice day is present, and every holdout day through the observation tail when it runs the holdout.
- **2026-10-04 · Holdout days are never traded before the sealed run.** The `day` command on a holdout day runs with entries off and reports engine validity only (crashes, illegal states, replays, leak test, ledger replay, fact counts), with no candidates, rejects or trades. `trial` runs only practice days that have their 14-day look-back, and refuses holdout days.
- **2026-10-04 · Every holdout run is logged before it starts and can happen once** (PR #24 review follow-up). The study registry (`StudyRegistry`: STATS-1's holdout registry, the run log and the trials) is written before the run. A run that finds any earlier run of the same holdout, sealed, failed or half-done after a crash, is refused and burns every holdout it names ('reconfigured'). The ledger and an outcomes file are made read-only; the seal is sha256 over both files' hashes, recorded with each universe's candidate and entry counts. S0's 200 holdout seeds run inside the sealed run only when a universe's counts already pass the size check.
- **2026-10-04 · G2 opens nothing when the sample is short.** When no universe's sealed counts reach max(300, n_power) entries on at least 10 days (or the walk-forward cannot size n_power), G2 is "not proven yet" from the counts alone and every seal stays closed. Otherwise the scoring stage reads the outcomes once, and gateG2 verifies the hash, configuration and counts before opening (Holm over the registry's family of 2: U1, U2).
- **2026-10-04 · G0 counts what BT-2 cannot measure as failing.** Second-source coverage (DATA-1 QA) and live/backtest parity (TEST-1) are inputs BT-2 does not produce, so G0 reports them as not passed until their evidence exists. BT-2 adds two checks: engine validity (0 crashes, illegal states and unreconciled intents) and `ledger:replay` on the walk-forward ledger.
- **2026-10-04 · H12's curve check is remembered per address** (`gates/holders.ts`). The ed25519 check took 84% of a study run, holder by holder at every check. It depends on the address alone, so a bounded memory changes no answer; a run is 8× faster.
- **2026-10-04 · Holdout boundary and sizing (supervisor ruling, fixed before any data was read; its end is superseded by the fixed end E below).** The holdout covers UTC days 2026-09-22 to 2026-10-01, with entries from 2026-09-22T02:00Z (after the embargo) to 2026-10-01T21:30Z. Practice days are 2026-08-03 to 2026-09-21; post-B4 practice starts at 2026-09-12T15:24Z. The boundary and the planned size are in `docs/evidence/bt2/registry.json` (`plan`). That size comes from estimates only: about 1,270 graduates a day, 24% passing H9, an assumed 30% passing the other hard rejects and an assumed 25% meeting a setup and risk. That gives about 23 entries a day per universe, about 230 in 10 days, with a range of 100 to 500. If the sealed counts fall short of max(300, n_power), G2 says "not proven yet" and the next window is after 2026-10-02.
- **2026-10-04 · Holdouts are registered only for frozen configurations.** `study-1`'s U1/U2 rules are placeholders written before any data, and RES-3 proposes the configurations. Until `frozen` is set, the study runs the walk-forward and the trial only: it registers no holdout and refuses `--run-holdout`. A configuration changed after registration would burn its holdout.
- **2026-10-04 · fills-2: the conservative scenario returns the token-account rent when the final sell lands** *(Superseded on 2026-10-04 by BT-1c's account-close outcome model, merged in the base: the conservative scenario keeps `rentRecovery: false` and models the sell-and-close outcome with `closeSuccessPpm`/`dustPpm`; the fills config, its test and RES-3's test follow the base.)* (supervisor ruling). The builders close the account in that sell transaction, so recovery is mechanical once it lands; a blocked or unlanded final exit keeps the rent lost. G1 and an opened G2 also report a "no rent recovery" sensitivity line, which never gates. RISK-1 still counts the rent in C, the worst case for sizing.
- **2026-10-04 · H5's trade-event tails (GATE-1c) come from each row's `extra_hex`.** The feed releases a pool's first trade event since migration, plus every curve or pool event that carries a tail. Those are the only events that can fail the check, and the check needs one pool event and accepts a curve with none, so its answer is the same as with every event, without millions of empty tails in memory.
- **2026-10-04 · Trial view: practice days only.** `trial` runs each assembled window whose days are all practice days and writes a part. `scripts/trial-report.ts` merges the parts into UI-2's report: G1 is "not run", labelled "Trial in progress" and "not a verdict". A sealed-window day, a trade reaching 2026-09-22T00:00Z, a day in two parts or a part from another commit is refused whole (tested). The `backtest-trial` workflow is manual. Its run job reads only, skips any window that is not all practice days, and never downloads one. Its publish job alone has `contents: write` and runs `publish-report.ts` (which refuses any report naming the holdout) in a clean environment.
- **2026-10-04 · Assembled windows have lead-in days without trades.** Rug coverage starts on the window's first day. A launch created on a lead-in day keeps its create (H9 needs it) but is `rug-unjudged`, so H14 is not covered for its deployer until RUG-1c's supplement covers it.
- **2026-10-04 · Deployment replay (external review, supervisor ruling).** Besides the broad study, the walk-forward days are replayed at the real size against one running account:
  - RISK-1 in live mode: R3 with one position, R11 with 3 entries a day and one per mint, cooldowns, the daily and weekly triggers and the kill switch;
  - trips latch for the rest of the replay (there is no owner to re-arm);
  - open positions are marked at executable liquidation value on every update.

  It reports the drawdown, the value left stranded at the end, kill-switch and weekly trips, and the opportunities a portfolio control refused. Signal priority: the earliest fully eligible signal wins; checks due at the same block break ties by a salted hash (below). R16 is reported as not applied until FACTS-1 produces its inputs. It never runs on the holdout.
- **2026-10-04 · Ablations of H9, H11 and H14 (supervisor rulings after external review).** Paper-only runs on the walk-forward days enter the candidates whose only failing gate is the ablated one, with the same size, costs, delays and exits. Their outcomes are reported per universe and regime: count, mean, losses avoided, profits excluded and the 5th percentile. For H14 this is the missed-opportunity record of rug rejections. A filter is removed only if out-of-sample evidence shows that removing it improves net results without an unacceptable tail. Pre-registered: the 5th-percentile trade and the maximum drawdown must be no worse at the one-sided 95% level. Until then H9, H11 and H14 stay on.
- **2026-10-04 · Completeness manifest.** Every report lists each gate input with how the dataset and supplements rebuild it as of the decision (rebuilt, supplement, missing, or live-only veto), and how often each gate rejected for missing evidence. A candidate whose safety evidence cannot be rebuilt is rejected, as live would reject it.
- **2026-10-04 · Costs are as of each trade's slot.** Every quote and fill uses the fee fields of the latest released trade of that pool, never today's configuration; a test changes a pool's fees mid-run and the trade pays the new rate. Further supervisor rulings after external review, recorded here for BT-2:
- **2026-10-04 · Fixed holdout end E = 2026-10-20 (consensus of the three reviews).** The holdout is [2026-09-22, 2026-10-20): entries from 2026-09-22T02:00Z (after the embargo) to the cutoff 2026-10-20T00:00Z, then one observation-only day (2026-10-20) so every hold finishes; one sealed ledger and one endpoint for both universes. Days 2026-10-02 to 2026-10-20 are holdout days and never practice days; `day` runs them with entries off, `trial` refuses them. B5 is a decoder boundary (`market: false`): allowed inside the holdout, not purged, and reported as its own line; B2–B4 stay hard. `docs/evidence/bt2/registry.json` (`plan`) was regenerated: 28 entry days, about 640 expected entries per universe (range 280–1,400), estimates only. Tests: `test/plan.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · One error budget across holdout attempts.** Attempt 1 runs at family α = 0.04, attempt k ≥ 2 at 0.01 / 2^(k−1) on a new later window (`attemptAlpha`). The registry records each run's attempt index and α, refuses (and burns) an attempt that skips or repeats an index, n_power is simulated at that α, and G2 is called with `familyAlpha` set to it (tightening only). Required size is max(300, n_power, closed form) at α/m on ≥ 10 Melbourne trade days. Tests: `test/registry.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · The seal opens only after a G1 pass.** A universe's sealed holdout is opened only when its G1 over the practice days (all regimes, pooled: the cross-regime evidence) passed, and must be opened once its counts are met; otherwise G2 says "not proven" with the reason "G1 did not pass". Running the sealed holdout itself is unchanged (once, counts only). The registry enforces it: each study run appends the G1 result for every registered holdout (`recordG1`, nothing replaced, so a fail stays on record), and `openSealed` refuses unless the latest record is a pass for the registered configuration (`g1Blocks`). Tests: `test/registry.test.ts`, `test/full-study.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · Simultaneous signals: salted-hash tie-break.** An earlier block's check always comes first, so the earliest fully eligible signal wins. Checks due at the same block are released in the order of sha256(salt | universe | mint) with the salt `study-1-ties-2026-10-04`, fixed in `STUDY_CONFIG.tieSalt` and the registry plan before any replay; the first eligible one takes the position slot. This replaces universe order and pool depth, which tilted the choice by market features. Test: `test/facts.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · Funnel first.** Every check inside the entry window is counted once, by universe, at the stage where it stopped: the first failed hard gate in H1–H16 order (a reason filed under H16 for another gate counts under that gate), then market data, setup, stop distance, a busy book, risk, or entry. Each gate stop is "adverse" (the evidence said no) or "not covered" (any evidence code: missing, stale, partial, a gap, a lead-in mint, no funding source); "not covered" is never shown as a reject. Mints are counted at their furthest stage. The study reports the research sample (each candidate alone) and the deployment replay (one account) apart; `day` and `trial` outputs carry the funnel. Tests: `test/funnel.test.ts`, `test/full-study.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · Coverage exclusions are reported, never counted as rejects.** The funnel gives, per universe, the mints whose furthest stage was a "not covered" stop (lead-in holders, unjudged deployers, no funding source, gaps) with their share of all mints, and G1's pooled line carries the same count and share. Ownership missing because lead-in days have no movements stays unresolved until a complete seed or reconstruction covers it; elapsed time never repairs it.
- **2026-10-04 · The registered procedure is written into `registry.json` (`plan.procedure`)** before any holdout count is known: configurations chosen from practice days only and frozen and registered before any U1/U2 configuration runs live; registering commits the attempt (at E it is spent whatever happens: halted, abandoned or short is a failed attempt); one sealed ledger, opened once after the tail, never for one universe while another is pending; the G1 rule; the size rule at α 0.04; B5 as a decoder boundary; and the attempt-2 rule (registered after attempt 1 is scored, its window starting on the first whole UTC day after that registration, 28 days, α 0.005, same procedure). Residual risk: the live dry run overlaps the holdout window. It is contained because selection reads practice days only, nobody selecting configurations reads live shakedown P&L before the freeze, and opening at E is mandatory once the counts are met, so seeing live P&L cannot change what is opened.
- **2026-10-04 · Exits per universe (CFG-2).** The study reads exits through `exitsFor(policy.exits, universe)`: ATR period and bar for the stop-distance check, and `EntryPlan.universe` for every exit decision (S0 uses the universe it controls for). The run's tail is the longest `tMaxMs` of any universe (120 min cap) plus S0's end margin.
- **2026-10-04 · Delegates in the holder read (GATE-1e).** The raw reader keeps Approve and ApproveChecked (delegate and amount) and Revoke and CloseAccount (cleared) on token accounts; an owner change (SetAuthority AccountOwner) moves the account and clears its delegate. A delegate's own transfers are not counted down from the approved amount, so delegated control is only ever overstated (the conservative side). An approval in a transaction the dataset did not keep, or on an account before it held the mint, is not seen. That is the permissive direction (live reads delegates directly), so by supervisor ruling every holder read is marked partial and H12 is "not covered" until DATA-1 keeps every Approve/ApproveChecked/Revoke/SetAuthority transaction on tracked mints' token accounts; then `delegatesComplete` (CLI `--delegates-complete`) is set for that dataset. The completeness manifest lists the gap as missing. Tests: `test/raw.test.ts`, `test/facts.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · H17's pool fields in the backtest (TX-1b).** Per ARCHITECTURE §16 the source is DATA-1's record of the pool account at creation. The projector takes it as a supplement (`poolAccounts`, CLI `--pool-accounts`: size, cashback flag, coin creator, known from `knownAtMs`) and adds the fields only as of that moment; the coin creator also comes from CreatePoolEvent when the row carries it. Without the record the fields stay absent and every candidate stops at H17 through H16 (missing): the safe failure, shown as "not covered" in the funnel and as `missing` in the completeness manifest. Nothing is inferred from slot or program version. Test: `test/facts.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · RES-3's still-pool round-trip test follows fills-2.** *(Superseded on 2026-10-04 by BT-1c's account-close outcome model, merged in the base: the conservative scenario keeps `rentRecovery: false` and models the sell-and-close outcome with `closeSuccessPpm`/`dustPpm`; the fills config, its test and RES-3's test follow the base.)* Its outcome stage reads the scenario's `rentRecovery`, which fills-2 turned on for the conservative scenario (rent back when the final sell lands). The exact 1e-9 check now adds the rent when the scenario returns it and the exit was not blocked; the old −0.14 to −0.10 bounds apply unchanged to the result without the rent.
- **2026-10-04 · S0 in the deployment replay and the SPA panel (supervisor rulings).** S0 also runs in deployment mode (`deployment-s0`, one run per walk-forward S0 seed) under the same account, capacity, timing and cost rules, so the paired comparison is like for like. The study emits, per variant (U1, U2, and S0-U1/S0-U2 per seed), the realised net per Melbourne day over one fixed capital base (the policy bankroll at the SOL/USD close of the walk-forward's first hour), on the walk-forward's common Melbourne calendar, with entries and active days. A variant active on fewer than MIN_DAYS days is marked ineligible from activity alone, before any outcome is read. Tests: `test/spa.test.ts`, `test/study.test.ts`, `test/full-study.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · A rule kind for RES-3 handoffs (`features`).** One or two conditions `feature >= t` / `<= t` over RES-3's tracker features, mapped one to one, with the thresholds kept as the exact decimal text handed over (the configuration hash never depends on float formatting). The tracker runs feed side on the same rows in chain order and its features are released with each check as of that moment (`features:<mint>`), so the engine stays blind; a feature that is unknown at the check fails its condition. The stop is a fixed distance below the entry spot (the barrier's stop loss), and S0 uses the same stop, so the pair differs only in selection. The barrier's take-profit and time limit are exit parameters, which are CFG-2's per-universe policy, not this rule. Tests: `test/study.test.ts`, `test/funnel.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · Deployment replay on RISK-1b and EXIT-1b (base merge).** The simulated wallet holds the trading cash plus the operations floor, so RISK-1b's economic NAV equals the trading equity and sizing is unchanged. A NAV mark, in the evaluator's own `economicNav` definition, is recorded at every account evaluation for R10's high-water mark. Day and week start marks are reporting-only in RISK-1 and are left unrecorded (null). For EXIT-1b, the token-account balance is the position quantity (nobody sends tokens in the simulation), and `closeFailed` is false, because the fill model has no separate close failure: a sell-and-close lands or fails whole.
- **2026-10-04 · FACTS-1 staging in the backtest.** One stage table in core (`HARD_STAGE`, `gatesOfStages`, and an `only` option on `evaluateHardRejects`) is shared by live and the backtest:
  - stage 1: the stream-derived gates H7, H9, H10, H11, H14;
  - stage 2: the account reads and cross-checks H1–H6, H8, H16, H17;
  - stage 3: the complete holder scan and funders H12, H13;
  - stage 4: H15 (live only).

  At a check the strategy evaluates stage 1 only. A reject there logs "not evaluated" for every later gate, and no read is asked. A candidate that passes asks for its reads (at most one read round per mint per minute; none while one is in flight).
  - The stage-2 answers land `accountsMs + feedReleaseMs` after the check, and the stage-3 answers `holderScanMs + feedReleaseMs` after that. Each lands as a `landed:<mint>` event, with the facts released again as of that moment.
  - Stage 2 is evaluated when its answers land. A candidate past it spends one complete holder scan from the live daily cap. With the cap spent, H12 and H13 are "not evaluated", never a pass.
  - Stage 3 (and 4) is evaluated when the scan lands, then setup, risk and entry at that moment's prices. An entry is only ever decided on a stage-3 landing.

  No optimistic defaults (supervisor ruling), in `src/study/reads.ts`:
  - `READ_LIMITS` is the worker's own configuration, imported: `HOLDER_SCANS_PER_DAY` (100 per UTC day) and one read round per mint per minute (`60_000 / ASSUMPTIONS.evaluationsPerMinute`).
  - `READ_LATENCY` holds conservative named figures until the dry run measures p95s: account and largest-holder reads 500 ms; the complete holder scan 5,000 ms (gpa-probe: 4.3 s for a 569k-account legacy scan; Token-2022 0.07–0.26 s); plus the live feed's longest release hold, `DEFAULT_LIVE_FEED.staleReleaseMs` (2 s, per FACTS-1: receipt to engine takes 1–2 s).
  - A test checks the defaults equal the live config.

  The live worker does not stage by gate yet (FACTS-1b #75 stages on the H16 evidence reasons, and reads the funders in the account round). Adopting `only` per stage is a WORKER-1 follow-up after #41.

  Under staging a dust pool is stopped by H11 in stage 1 before H8 is read, which is the live order. H8 is tested past stage 1 through an H11 ablation. Tests: `packages/core/test/gates/stages.test.ts`, `test/study.test.ts`, `test/facts.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · Wallet setup rent in the deployment replay (supervisor parity ruling).** Booked as live books it: one `wallet_setup` account cost at the walk-forward start, the worker's own `oneTimeRent(fills)` (imported, not copied), valued at that hour's SOL price and rounded up. Equity and cash start at bankroll minus the rent, and the rent counts toward day 1's loss. Test: `test/study.test.ts`; NAV is within one micro-dollar of bankroll − rent, because it values the wallet in lamports and back, flooring both ways.
- **2026-10-04 · The regime gate in the backtest (supervisor parity question).** Before this change the backtest never evaluated it: research runs passed `regime: 'unknown'` to RISK-1, where R16 is live only, and the deployment replay passed `'on'`. The projector produces no graduate-survival or curve-volume facts, so if the regime were evaluated it would be off ("unknown") on every check, independent of the 365-day volume window.

  Now `evaluateRegime` runs first at every check, as in the live worker. Off is its own funnel stage, "regime off": "not covered" when its inputs are unknown, adverse when its conditions fail. It is never skipped. A labelled diagnostic, `regime: 'assume-on'` (CLI `--regime-assumed-on`), lets the downstream logic run while the inputs do not exist. Every output of such a run says so: `regimeGate` in the study report and the CLI evidence, and a failed "Regime gate: assumed on" line in the trial report. The holdout refuses to run in that mode, and its counts never feed G1 (every G1 line reads "not proven", descriptive only), the trial registry, a G1 record or a holdout registration (supervisor ruling). Tests: `test/study.test.ts`, `test/full-study.test.ts`.

- **2026-10-04 · Graduate survival for the regime gate comes from FACTS-1's producer (supervisor ruling: one source, never a copy).** The projector gives each graduating mint its own `FactProducer` (options `producerOptions(policy)`), fed as fetched, confirmed transactions:
  - the mint's CompleteEvent, CompletePumpAmmMigrationEvent and CreatePoolEvent;
  - a `coverage:trades:<pool>:start` at pool creation, since the dataset keeps every canonical-pool trade from creation;
  - the pool's swaps up to the mark, mapped field for field from the dataset's Buy/Sell rows (pre-trade reserves, `base_amount`, `quote_amount_lp_adjusted`);
  - slot notices once the mark has passed.

  The producer dates survival by its own rule and is dropped once the mark resolves or its read window ends. The resolved items go through `graduatesFact` (core/facts), the one aggregation the live producer now calls too (supervisor ruling): keep items migrated within `graduatesKeepMs`, list them by migration time then mint. A test pins the live producer's whole graduates fact before and after that refactor. Per-mint producers rather than one: a single producer for the whole run would scan every pool's stream on every slot notice (about 94k pools over 16M slots in a 74-day run), while a per-mint producer holds one stream and lives about 31 minutes.

  Curve volume waits for FACTS-1d's core `parseVolumeHoursCsv` (#78) and DATA-1c's `volume-hours` assets. SOL/USD uses the existing hourly series. Until volume exists, the regime reads "unknown" and real-day counts run in the labelled assume-on mode. The synthetic CreatePoolEvent gained `pool_base_amount` and `pool_quote_amount`, as the real event carries them. Test: `test/facts.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-05 · External audit B1–B5 fixed in BT-2 (#41), each reproduced first in a failing test.**
  - B5, failed entries: a failed entry's landed fee was dropped by `scoreRun`. It is now charged to the next trade of its tag opened at or after it (else the tag's last trade before it), so the per-trade inputs of G1, G2, the power estimate and the trial view carry every lamport without adding a trade; `ScoredTrade.stray` lists what a trade carries. A fee of a tag with no trade at all is returned by `scoreRunAll` as uncarried. The SPA daily series books every failed-entry fee on its landing day (carried or not), and the deployment and control nets include them. Trial parts carry them by tag into the report.
  - B1, S0's gates: S0 passed universe `S0` into the hard gates, which skips U2's chase check and U1's liquidity floor. S0 now runs its universe's gates; only its entry check is random.
  - B3, holder growth: H3 counts distinct wallet owners with a positive summed balance (#115 `definitions.holderGrowth`), never token accounts; lockers, program-owned accounts, the curve, the mayhem vault, burns and the tape pool's base vault (named by the pool fact for that pool) are not holders. Coverage must be complete at the moment: `coverage 'all'`, no quality flag, accounts summing to the supply, and a pool fact for the tape's pool; otherwise the count is unknown and the condition fails.
  - B2, one decision time: every entry decision runs every gate stage from stage 1 in one call at `ctx.now` (`stagedHardRejects`, the same groups and stop rule as FACTS-1f's live path, #106), and needs GATE-2's complete-and-no-reasons rule. The stage-1 call at the check and stages 1–2 at the account landing only decide which reads to ask for, as live. Parity test against the live single evaluation on the core gate world, in both modes. When #106 merges, its `stagedHardRejects` and the study's must stay equal (the parity test is the place to check it).
  - B4, wording: U2's flow is "non-creator-user flow" (#115 `definitions.nonCreatorUserFlow`): swaps by anyone but the creator's exact address. A wallet the creator funded still counts, so it is never called independent buying.
- **2026-10-05 · 01FHfb's binding stats rulings built in BT-2 (#41).**
  - The SPA settings (SE floor 0.0005 of the capital base per day, 2,000 replicates, level 0.05) are a typed part of the registered holdout plan (`HoldoutPlan.spa`), written before any practice-day statistic is computed; the selection reads them from the stored plan. A changed floor is a different plan, which the registry refuses once one is set, so it can never be raised from practice-day ω. A plan without them gives no selection.
  - Calibration at that floor (`spa-floor-calibration.test.ts`): the real 64-day layout and regimes, k = 6 with per-universe S0, zero edge, H5 sparse (12 active days) and H2 all costs (a small daily cost only), daily SD 0.05 of the base, 300 seeded runs through the study's own selection: the global test rejected in 6 (2.0%) and a hypothesis was picked in 1 (0.33%), both below α = 0.05; pinned.
  - Pick rule: among the SPA-passing hypotheses of a universe, drop any whose practice entries per calendar day × 28 fall below the holdout requirement it would freeze (max(300, n_power, closed form) at the attempt's α over the Holm family of 2, the same number registration freezes, which is at least the ruled max(300, n_power)); rank the rest by min(zVsZero, zVsS0), ties by file order.
  - The Holm family is fixed at 2 (`HOLDOUT_FAMILY_SIZE`), whatever the configuration or the pick holds; G2 already counts a universe without a configuration at p = 1.
  - `s0Of` is required: H1, H2, H3, H6 against S0-U1 and H4, H5 against S0-U2; a universe without its S0 series stops the selection (before, it fell back to the first universe's S0).
- **2026-10-05 · Review follow-ups (012efQ on #41).** A worker test pins live freshness against the observed tip (a holder read more than `maxStateSlotLag` slots behind the clock's slot is stale; mutating the live tip to 0n or slot − 50 fails it). `study` refuses `--preregistration` (it silently ignored it); the early look's command line is tested refusing a non-practice day and an incomplete one.
- **2026-10-05 · S2: G3's bounded checks use a capped estimand (external audit; supervisor ruling with the stats reviewer's conditions C1–C6).** The study's exits have no ceiling on one trade's return, so `RETURN_CAP = 3` was not a bound on what a trade can make. The quantity is now r_c = min(rNet, 3), labelled "net, capped at +300%" (`CAPPED_ESTIMAND`); `RETURN_CAP` and core's `MAX_RETURN_CAP` are one constant. G1, G2 and the SPA test stay on the uncapped net return, the registered estimand.
  - Per use (C2):
    - retainedLower: the holdout's lower bound is computed on r_c in its own day-block bootstrap at 1 − α/4 (`holdoutSummary`), never G2's uncapped bound reused. Capping can only lower the tested mean, so this is conservative.
    - veto bias v⁺·|Δ|⁺: both arms capped. Capping can hide a difference in the tails, so the tail rule (C1) applies: cappedCount is reported per arm (kept, vetoed), and when any vetoed trade is capped while no kept trade is, or one-sided Fisher at α/4 says the vetoed arm is capped more often, the check "veto bias tail" is not proven, never a pass.
    - G3 agree checks (the mean against the holdout's predictive interval): both sides capped identically, each side's capped count shown; report only, no extra rule.
    - Reverse e-process (demotion): see C3.
  - Worst gap (C5): unmeasured, the veto gap is the estimand's range, 3 − RETURN_FLOOR = 4.1, not the configured take-profit minus the floor (a vetoed trade at +500% with a 0.3 take-profit could exceed that). G3 checks the holdout bound's estimand tag and refuses any other (C6), and any return below RETURN_FLOOR, dry run, vetoed or holdout, fails G3, as in G5's e-process path.
  - E-process validity (C3): per day Y_d = −mean_d(min(r, c))/c ∈ [−1, ∞); wealth Π(1 + λ_d·Y_d) with predictable λ_d ∈ [0, 1) is a nonnegative supermartingale under H0_c: E[min(r, c) | past] ≥ 0, so Ville's inequality gives P(max wealth ≥ 20) ≤ 1/20. Under the uncapped null E[r | past] ≥ 0 it is not valid in general: min(r, c) ≤ r, so a strategy whose mean comes from returns above c can show capped decay and be demoted. False demotion rises, which is the safe direction.
  - Demotion cap (C4): it stays the configured take-profit (0 < cap ≤ 3), never a fixed 3. At cap 3 a −10% decay went undetected for 50 days (eprocess.ts, review of PR #8); fixing it at 3 would loosen demotion and needs the owner. Nothing in S2 changes it.
  - Levels: with STATS-1g (#129) merged, all four parts of the retained-expectancy composite (holdout bound, veto rate, gap, fill error) are at α/4, so it holds at ≥ 95%. The capped holdout bound's level is that one constant (`HOLDOUT_LOWER_LEVEL = VETO_COMPOSITE_LEVEL`, M1), and G3 refuses any other. The gap bounds are #129's cluster-robust Welch bounds, on the capped returns of both arms with their creator clusters. The tail rule uses α/4 (`VETO_TAIL_ALPHA`).
- **2026-10-05 · H3's holder count and the backtest's delegate-only gap (BT review of #41, N1 and N4).**
  - N1: #115's `definitions.holderGrowth` counts owners holding a positive balance; a missing delegation never adds an owner or a balance, so the backtest's delegate-only 'partial' flag (delegations not rebuilt, `--delegates-complete` absent) leaves H3's count standing. The projector tags that fact `partialReason: 'delegates'`, and only that tag lifts that one flag; any other quality flag, or 'partial' for another reason, still makes the count unknown. H12 and H13 still treat the flag as before.
  - D1 (BT review of 0b134bb, ruling (b); supersedes "delegates count as control" for H3, before the pre-registration sha is pinned): H3 follows #115's `definitions.holderGrowth` exactly, distinct owners with a positive balance, H12's exclusions, no delegate grouping. Grouping owners with their delegates made the count depend on delegation data, so missing delegations raised it and incomplete evidence could pass an entry condition. Delegates count as control only in H12/H13's concentration. With delegations out of H3, the N1 lift is sound. Test: delegated owners each count and a delegate adds no holder.
  - N4: a closed and reopened non-ATA token account keeps its old owner in HolderBook unless a SetAuthority is seen; a new owner without one is not visible to the rebuild.
- **2026-10-05 · BT-2's n_power meets G2's pinned settings (STATS-1g M2).** `powerOf` simulates each simulated G2 with ceil(20 / (α/m)) bootstrap replicates (1,000 at the family of 2 and α 0.04), G2's own minimum, and the study hands G2 the same S0 control as `walkForwardControl`, so the inputs fingerprint matches. Test: a BT-2 power result passes every pinned setting and its fingerprint is the study's inputs; at the old default of 500 replicates it fails.
- **2026-10-05 · SOL/USD in the books (paper is real money; BT review S1 of b1b9fdb).** Closes and marks book at the latest SOL/USD close however old (a loss is never dropped), and the deployment report counts those priced at a close older than `SOL_USD_BOOKS_STALE_MS` (`staleSolUsdCloses`, `staleSolUsdMarks`). That bound is the entry bound (2 h, unchanged) plus the close's delivery (feed release and observation delay, under 2 minutes even with the stress profile's blackout), so a series that never stops flags nothing; it only counts, it changes no trade.
  - The projector releases the SOL/USD fact whenever a new close becomes usable, as live's price stream does, not only with a candidate's facts; before, a held position's marks read a close up to an hour older than the series allowed.
  - The market's observation release skips empty slots up to the next row's slot rather than arming a hook per empty slot (same release block, same order; driver-only, the engine never sees the next row). With the extra SOL facts, the synthetic lead-in (one block an hour) otherwise walked 9,000 slots an hour, 30 times slower under the runtime trap.
  - Tests (each failing before): a study run whose series stops after the entry's close books the same close and trades with byte-identical lamports and counts stale closes and marks, both 0 with the current series; with no candidate checked, every close usable in the run is released at its first block; a release over 100,000 empty slots arms at most 3 hooks. Hand mutants, all caught: no close counter, no mark counter, a stale close dropped, no per-close release, the books flagged at the entry bound, a hook per empty slot.

## External review of the promotion gates (STATS-1b)

- **2026-10-04 · RES-4's family through one SPA test, at most one pick per universe (supervisor ruling (b), (c); two choices for the stats review, 01FHfb).**
  - Inputs: on the practice days every pre-registered hypothesis runs on its own deployment replay (one account, its limits), and S0 runs per universe under the same rules. Each has a daily net P&L over the fixed capital base, on the walk-forward's Melbourne calendar.
  - The test: STATS-1c's joint SPA test runs once over all hypotheses (k = 6), each against zero and against its own universe's S0. Core `spaTest` gained an optional per-variant benchmark (`s0Of`): with every variant on one S0 the result is identical (test). S0's series is the mean of its seeds. Blocks stay within market regimes.
  - Pick rule (proposed): per universe, among the hypotheses that pass at every block length, the highest statistic against S0, with ties broken by file order. If none passes, that universe gets no holdout configuration.
  - Holdout set: only universes whose studied configuration is the pick register. The Holm family stays the universes (2), so α is unchanged. With no pick the holdout refuses to run.
  - Trials: every hypothesis's replay is a trial in the experiment registry.
  - SE floor (proposed): STATS-1c registered no SE floor, so BT-2 proposes 0.0005 of the capital base per day (`STUDY_CONFIG.spa`, with 2,000 replicates at α 0.05), to be confirmed by the stats review before any practice-day result is read.

  Tests:
  - `core/test/stats-math.test.ts` (per-variant S0);
  - `test/select.test.ts` (pick and too few days);
  - `test/full-study.test.ts` (family replays and trials; no pick on 2 days, so nothing registered and the holdout refused).
- **2026-10-04 · BT-2e early look (owner request via the supervisor).** `cli.ts early` (`src/study/early.ts`) runs the same engine, gates and as-of guards on one or two practice days from DATA-2's free reader, U2 only (U1 needs a 14-day lead-in).
  - Configurations: BT-2's U2 and RES-4's U2 hypotheses, each on its own run (one configuration per universe per run), with S0 pooled over its seeds. It uses whatever lead-in the dataset holds; checks without history abstain as not covered.
  - Report per day, labelled "early look, not proof":
    - the funnel gate by gate;
    - trades per day (no daily cap in backtest);
    - win rate with its Clopper-Pearson 95% interval;
    - mean net after costs with a 95% trade-bootstrap interval (stated as ignoring clustering, so the true interval is wider; one or two days allow no day-block interval);
    - median net, profit factor, worst trade and longest losing streak.
  - Limits: holdout days and days outside the window are refused, and so is an incomplete day. Nothing is written to the holdout registry: no plan, attempt, trial or G1 record. These days count again in the study.

  Tests: `test/early.test.ts`, covering the figures, two U2 configurations run on their own with S0, and the command on an on-disk dataset (report written, holdout day refused).
- **2026-10-04 · RES-4's family in BT-2: hypotheses by id, bound by hash, one experiment registry (supervisor ruling, from the RES-4 #115 review).**
  - (a) Configurations per hypothesis. A configuration may carry its hypothesis id (`H4-U2-reclaim`). The id is its tag on positions, decisions, the funnel, trials and its configuration id; without an id the universe is the tag, so existing ids are unchanged. Gates still run per universe and S0 stays one per universe. Checks come per universe, so a run holds one configuration per universe: hypotheses of one universe run one at a time, and two in one run are refused. Feature rules take any number of conditions, all required.
  - (d) Pre-registration. `src/strategy/preregistration.ts` reads `research/edge/preregistration.json` in BT-2's shape. It is strict (known features, exact decimal thresholds, integer amounts as strings, unique ids, a window of the hypothesis's own universe) and only with the sha256 pinned in the study configuration. Until #115 merges the pin is null and nothing is read.
    - The holdout plan records the hash and the ids. A changed file or an added hypothesis is a different plan, which the registry refuses once set, so a 7th trial needs a new window.
    - The study refuses any configuration that is not exactly one of the hypotheses.
  - (e) One experiment registry. The trial log is now a section of the holdout registry (`trials`, written by `recordTrials`): one log per registry, pushed with it, whatever `--out` says. `--trials` is gone. With a family in the plan, a trial tagged outside it is refused and the log is left unchanged.

  Tests: `test/preregistration.test.ts`, `test/registry.test.ts` (experiment registry), `test/study.test.ts` (a hypothesis under its id; two per universe refused) and `test/full-study.test.ts` (outside the family refused before anything runs; trials in the registry).
- **2026-10-04 · BT-2 on STATS-1c's registry (#62).** The study now takes the attempt's α from the core registry's schedule.
  - Sizing first: it computes the holdout's size requirement before registering. That is max(300, n_power, closed form) on MIN_DAYS days, with n_power's seed, all from the walk-forward of the holdout's regime.
  - Registration: attempt 1 is registered with that requirement frozen in, before any holdout count exists.
  - A walk-forward that cannot size it (too few trades, days or S0 trades) registers nothing and spends no α. G2 says why ("the size requirement cannot be frozen").
  - Later runs report the frozen requirement, never a recomputed one, and the size check reads its trades and days.
  - G2 receives each universe's G1 result. Opening also waits for the tail to mature (core).

  Tests: `test/full-study.test.ts` (no registration without sizing; then the frozen requirement reported) and `test/registry.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · G1's test comes from the stored registry (STATS-1f #109).** BT-2 passes `gateG1` the registry as read from the holdout store; the helper's parameter is the full `HoldoutRegistry`, so a literal `{ g1Test }` cannot be passed. A diagnostic run before any registry exists does not evaluate G1 ("not evaluated", never a pass). Test: `test/full-study.test.ts`, where G1's test check names the stored test and a stored test G1 does not know fails it.
- **2026-10-04 · Projector facts through the observation delay (supervisor ruling).** The delay is when the engine sees an event; an event's chain slot never changes. The projector's facts, checks and read landings now go through the same observation queue as the pool tape (`src/sim/market.ts`).
  - Release: each is released at the first block at or after its slot plus the delay slots. The release moment (and the provider time) only places it in the engine's order, which runs by moment, so a delayed event cannot keep its chain slot there. Its value goes as built and keeps every chain slot.
  - Observed tip: as a release batch goes out in chain order, `tip:observed` moves to the newest chain slot released so far. Each event is judged against what had been seen when it arrived, never against a later slot of the same batch, so sparse block rows cannot age a fact. During a blackout nothing is released and the tip stands still.
  - Staleness: `GateContext.observedTip` is required, and core `Evidence` always judges chain state and stream heads against it (one rule, live and backtest; supervisor ruling). A tip ahead of the clock is ignored.
    - Live passes the newest released chain slot. Its feed releases events at their own slots, so that is the clock's slot, and live decisions are unchanged.
    - The study strategy passes `tip:observed`, or the clock's slot with recorded receipt times.
  - Effect: a uniform delay alone makes nothing stale. A backlog after a blackout is judged against the tip as of its release.
  - Fills still pay for the delay: the entry is decided later and filled at chain state then (BT-1c).
  - Rows with recorded receipt times are not delayed again.

  Tests:
  - `test/replay.test.ts`: released after the delay, chain slots unchanged, tip first and equal to the newest chain slot;
  - `core/test/gates/hard.test.ts`: freshness against the tip, and a tip ahead of the clock ignored;
  - `worker/test/observed-tip.test.ts`: a recorded live session replays to byte-identical decisions, gate reasons included. The digest was taken on the code before the field existed;
  - `test/study.test.ts`: no stale reason under the adverse profile, the entry at least 8 slots later and opened later than with recorded times. With the tip removed, it rejects `H11:stale`.
- **2026-10-04 · G2's funder cluster is the dev's first funder (supervisor ruling).** The label comes from the dev's own read in the insider-funding supplement: `worker/scripts/funding-backfill.ts` writes `insider-funding.jsonl` with its sha256 manifest, and `readSupplement` reads it. It is the same point-in-time reader and the same supplement as H13's links (core `facts/funding.ts`), so there is no new source. The read counts only when it is complete and names a funder; otherwise the trade has no funder label.
  - A trade without either label fails G2 as missing evidence (the stricter outcome).
  - There is no shared "unknown" cluster, and an unknown never gets a singleton cluster (either would loosen the gate).
  - The creator label works the same way: the deployer named in the entry line, otherwise none.

  The CLI's `--insiders <dir>` now reads the supplement itself (`devFunderOf` in `src/study/score.ts`). No backfill runs before its Helius credit estimate is reported and cleared. Test: `test/study.test.ts` (labels).
- **2026-10-04 · BT-2 on GATE-2's stages.** BT-2's copies of `HARD_STAGE`, `gatesOfStages` and `only` are dropped for GATE-2's (#94). The study's two staged evaluations, merged, are `complete` only when together they evaluated every hard gate. An entry needs a complete evaluation with no reasons; anything less stops as "not evaluated" (not covered), never a pass. Test: `test/funnel.test.ts` (staged gates).
- **2026-10-04 · BT-2 writes through the one holdout registry (supervisor ruling).** BT-2's own registry file and `docs/evidence/bt2/registry.json` are gone. The study now:
  - sets its plan once with `setHoldoutPlan`: window, cutoff E, tail, tie salt, the ruled α schedule, B5 as a decoder boundary, and the procedure in words (`holdoutPlanOf` in `src/study/plan.ts`). The plan holds only what was fixed before any data was read, so the same study registers the same plan;
  - registers attempt 1 for U1 and U2 with `registerAttempt` when the configurations are frozen (registering commits the attempt and its α). A registry holding only some of an attempt's universes stops the study;
  - takes each attempt's α from the registry (`attemptAlpha` of `src/holdout.ts`; the study config's copy is removed) for n_power and G2's family α;
  - appends each G1 result with `recordHoldoutG1`;
  - runs the sealed holdout through `sealThroughStore`, the checks, start record, failure burn and seal of `runAndSealHoldout`, now shared. It takes each universe's own configuration id and the caller's run, and `runAndSealHoldout` uses it unchanged;
  - stores G2's registry with `recordHoldoutG2`. That write is refused unless only sealed holdouts changed, each to opened or burned, and every opening had a latest G1 pass.

  The registry's rules now apply to BT-2: a second run of a sealed window is refused and logged, and the seal stays untouched (BT-2 burned it before). The study's sealed run reads its 14-day lead-in from before the window, and a row after the window's last day stops it. Its entry window must end at the registered cutoff. Trials go to their own experiment registry (`--trials`, default `docs/evidence/bt2/trials.json`). The CLI's `--registry` is refused, and a tree with uncommitted changes may not write the registry. The leak test's planted token is now a valid address (the holder rebuild decodes every owner), and its create carries the initial real reserves. Tests: `test/registry.test.ts` and `test/full-study.test.ts` (plan, attempt, G1 records, start and seal records, the second run refused).
- **2026-10-04 · Holders come from BT-1d's HolderBook (agreed with BT-1d: one rebuild, no copy).** The projector feeds the book in chain order:
  - the dataset's token movements (fed before each row they precede), the lifecycle events, and every curve and pool trade row (crediting `user_token_owner`, never `user` or the signer);
  - the account operations from raw records: approve, revoke, close and owner change, each mint taken from the record's balances for that account.

  Each check reads `holdersAsOf(mint)`. A resolved read becomes the holder fact (supply, every account with its owner program and delegate), still marked partial until the dataset keeps every approval-changing transaction. An unresolved read gives no fact, so H12 and H13 are "not covered". Its reason (`sum_mismatch`, `no_create`, `owner_program_unknown`, `swap_owner_unknown`, …) is counted per check and per distinct mint in the run's evidence (`holderAbstentions`), never imputed.

  The CLI loads the movement coverage notes, each run's movements and the owner-program supplement (`--owner-programs <dir>`). BT-2's raw-balance rebuild is removed. Raw records still give mint authorities, extensions, supply and LP. The synthetic world became schema-3 consistent:
  - the create's initial real reserves;
  - curve reserves that track every buy;
  - the dev buy as a curve trade;
  - the pool creation's `base_amount_in`;
  - an owner-program map for every holder.

  Tests: `test/facts.test.ts` (a swap with no owner leaves the mint unresolved with its reason counted; holders no longer depend on raw records), `test/raw.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · Regime volume in the backtest, through FACTS-1's producer.** Volume hours come from the dataset's `days/DAY/volume_hours-NNN.csv.zst` files (`loadVolumeHours`), plus DATA-1c's `volume-hours-DAY.csv` release assets (CLI `--volume-hours <dir>`). Both are parsed by core's `parseVolumeHoursCsv`, the live reader's parser; a malformed day is refused whole. Each hour reaches a FACTS-1 producer as the live reader's `read:chain-volume-hour` answer once the hour has ended, and the producer's curve-volume fact is released. Complete covered days only; an uncovered hour leaves its day unknown. The regime's window rule (expanding from 2026-07-20, at least 28 days, day D−3) is FACTS-1d's, in regime.ts. Tests: `test/dataset.test.ts`, `test/facts.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · G3's holdout summary** (`src/study/summary.ts`; shape from the G3 builder's `HoldoutSummary`). When a universe's seal is opened, the scoring stage writes `holdout-summary-<U>.json` next to the sealed result:
  - n, mean and sd of net return;
  - the severe share (blocked, or net at or below −50%);
  - the day-block one-sided lower bound at `VETO_COMPOSITE_LEVEL`;
  - candidates and the holdout's entry hours;
  - the reject mix;
  - `returnCap`.

  The reject mix is sealed with the outcomes: one count per never-entered candidate, keyed by the typed `gate:code` of its last abstention (H-gates and R-controls as logged, `regime:<code>`, `stop:<reason>`, `setup:no-setup`, `worker:market-data|book-busy|no-quote|not-evaluated`). `returnCap` is 3, G3's upper bound, because the study's exits put no structural ceiling on one trade (a trailing stop rides a winner). The widest cap only widens G3's bias bound. Test: `test/summary.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-04 · Base merge with BT-1d, BT-1c and STATS-1b.**
  - Schema 3 is the only dataset schema. Raw records ride in it (the writer and reader keep them), and trade rows carry `lp_fee`, `quote_amount_lp_adjusted` and `extra_hex` beside BT-1d's `user_token_account`/`user_token_owner`.
  - Rent follows BT-1c's account-close outcome model: the conservative scenario keeps `rentRecovery: false`, which supersedes fills-2.
  - G2 resamples by creator and funder cluster (STATS-1b). The creator cluster is the deployer, written in each entry's log line and read at scoring. The funder cluster is one shared "unknown" for every trade because the dataset records no funding. That is the conservative side: G2 cannot pass the funder variant until a funder source exists. Raised with the supervisor.
  - The projector's facts, checks and read landings are released at chain time, not through BT-1c's observation delay. Delayed events are re-stamped at their release slot, so under the "adverse" profile (2 + 6 slots) every chain-state fact would sit past `maxStateSlotLag` (2) and every check would read "stale". In live the engine's clock follows each event's own slot. Until ruled, the gate facts are kinder than live by the observation delay, while the strategy's pool tape is delayed as BT-1c models. Raised with the supervisor. *(Superseded: "Projector facts through the observation delay" below.)*
  - Synthetic test seeds changed where BT-1c's fill draws now drop or fail the one entry a mechanic needs: study runs use 'e2', the deployment busy-book test 'd3', the full study 'study3'. The assertions are unchanged. The S0 one-position test now proves the rule binds by its busy-book refusals rather than by a research-S0 overlap.
  - A feature rule's fixed stop is rounded up, so a barrier at the policy's widest stop (B1, −20%) is never refused for being a rounding unit too wide (test).

- **2026-10-04 · G3 measures the live-only veto bias.** The fixed 50-point gap and the skip below 30 paper trades let a run with 20 paper trades at −1% against a +5% holdout and 10% vetoes pass (reproduced on the base: `pass`). Now every vetoed candidate is scored as if entered by the scoring stage (`scoreVetoCounterfactuals`), Δ and v carry their 95% bounds, and the holdout's lower bound minus the selection and execution allowances must stay above 0. Missing or censored counterfactuals, too few to measure, or a bound that fails while its point estimate passes give "not proven: extend the dry run". Options considered: keep the 50-point assumption (rejected: an assumption, not evidence), or fail on any missing counterfactual (rejected: missing evidence is not a failure, and "not proven" already blocks funding).
- **2026-10-04 · G2 passes only under every resampling unit.** 300 trades are not 300 independent observations: day blocks of 1, 2 and 3 days and creator and funder clusters are all applied, and the universe's p is the largest. The S0 difference uses the day blocks only, because S0 trades carry no cluster. n_power stays simulated on the 1-day rule (a lower bound for the stricter rule); the 300 floor and the closed form still apply.
- **2026-10-04 · B5 needs post-change evidence before funding.** B5 (2026-10-02 15:47 UTC) follows the holdout (ends 2026-10-01), so the holdout's proof predates the market being traded. Ruling: funding also needs `evaluateRevalidation` to pass: a qualifying dry run started after B5 that passes G3 against the holdout; and, if UPG-1's "economics unchanged" finding is ever contradicted (set `economicsUnchanged: false` in `config/platform.ts`), a backtest re-run on ≥ 200 candidates after B5 whose gates pass. An unreviewed change counts as contradicted.
- **2026-10-04 · What "agrees" means for the post-B5 dry run** (supervisor ruling after external review, not the owner's). Fixed and registered before the run, as part of G3: candidate and rejection rates per hour and per reason; executable amounts within TEST-2's bounds (median ≤ 0.5 points, each ≤ 2 points); transaction behaviour (≥ 95% simulation success, only registered error codes); paper outcomes against the holdout's expected distribution (mean and severe share); parity. Each has a pre-set tolerance and a minimum sample; too little evidence is inconclusive (extend the run), never agreement. Below 30 paper trades G3 used to pass on rates alone; it is now inconclusive, so `minPaperTradesForMean` is a floor (raising it is stricter).
- **2026-10-04 · Review of 437e60d and the final G3 rule** (supervisor rulings after three reviews).
  - The veto-bias composite takes each of its three bounds at α/3: the holdout's one-sided lower bound (G3 checks the input's level), the veto-rate upper bound, and the gap (one-sided for the selection allowance, two-sided for |Δ|). Together they hold with at least 95%. Before this, |Δ| took the larger of two one-sided 95% bounds, which is a 90% bound.
  - Tests kill two mutants: S3 (the |Δ| bound replaced by its point estimate) and S9 (the execution allowance set to 0).
  - The consistency checks are agree tests and keep their levels: 95% per reason, the mean inside an explicit 90% predictive interval, plus a joint 95% G-test of the reject mix. Power at 48 h in the fixture setting: rate halved 1.0, one reason doubled 1.0, fill gap 0.75 points 1.0, mean −5 points 0.276. A short power is answered with a longer run, never a looser bound.
  - n_power simulates the full G2 rule with cluster sensitivity, drawing walk-forward days in runs of 3 with their creator and funder labels; this only raises n.
  - A platform change inside the holdout window fails revalidation.
- **2026-10-04 · The #52 review round (holdout-regime, mutants, cluster labels).** Fail the holdout only on a platform change inside the window whose economics changed or were not reviewed; an inside change marked unchanged (B5) needs its before/after report, not a failure (the fixed-window ruling put E at 10-20, so B5 is inside). Tests kill the `done('pass')` early-return mutant (R2) and the walk-forward-cluster-label mutant (U2). In n_power's simulation a creator or funder seen on several walk-forward days keeps its id across repeat draws (a prolific deployer makes most coins); only a single-day creator gets a fresh id. MIN_DAYS stays 10: at α 0.02, 0.005 and 0.0025 every per-tail false-positive rate at D = 10 (ρ 0.05 and 0.1, 8k–24k runs) is at or below nominal within Monte Carlo error. The G3 registration should be hashed into the worker's run journal at start so "fixed before the run" can be checked later — a WORKER follow-up, because the stats module is pure (no crypto) and the worker does not yet call G3.
- **2026-10-04 · G4's blocked-exit evidence is a bound.** 0 blocked exits in 30 trades still allows about 9.5% (one-sided 95%); G4 reports `blockedExitUpper95` and says so in its notes.

## STATS-1c implementation (2026-10-04)

Built to the supervisor's revised spec and the consensus rulings that followed it; these entries replace the first STATS-1c draft (day-level DSR as the gate, count-driven extension), which those rulings withdrew.

- **2026-10-04 · G1's gate is unchanged: the per-trade DSR over every registered trial, with clamped moments.** Skewness is clamped to min(sample, 0) and kurtosis to max(sample, 3), the normal floor, registered in code before any data (`DSR_KURTOSIS_FLOOR`). For a positive Sharpe above the benchmark this only widens the PSR denominator, so it is tighten-only. The paper's worked example is pinned: 0.9004 at N = 100, 0.9505 at N = 46, and 0.9505 at N = 88 with normal moments. Simulated pass rate of this gate at a true +5% edge on 50 days (best of N): 3 trades a day 0.5–2%, 10 a day 9.5–14% (N = 10, 72, 200). That is the evidence for the owner's SPA sign-off.
- **2026-10-04 · Reported only, never gating:**
  - the day-level DSR under raw N, configuration-de-duplicated N (repeated runs of one config id count once; different configurations with identical or scaled returns stay distinct), and effective N (`trials.ts` clustering);
  - the block-bootstrap Sharpe of the selected trial's days, with the full statistic recomputed per replicate. Its null p-value is calibrated (5.0% at zero Sharpe, D = 50). Its percentile interval covered only 90–92%, so the interval is not reported as calibrated.
- **2026-10-04 · The SPA test to the ruled spec** (`spa.ts`):
  - both benchmarks in one test: 2K statistics, one critical value, and a variant passes only if both of its statistics exceed it;
  - a step-down for the selected configuration;
  - stationary bootstrap at expected block lengths 3, 5 and 7, with the largest p promoting;
  - resampling only within registered regimes;
  - variants active on fewer than 10 days, or all zero, left out before outcomes are read;
  - a registered SE floor;
  - p = (k + 1)/(B + 1), with B ≥ 20/α.

  Both studentisations were calibrated at T = 50 across 12 scenarios (300 runs each, zero edge). Hansen's fixed ω̂ rejected globally in 12–42% of runs and passed a variant in up to 9.3%. Re-studentising every replicate rejected globally in at most 3.0% and passed a variant in at most 0.33%. The re-studentised form is frozen (`SPA_STUDENTISATION`). Power is in `stats-simulation.test.ts`; the headline is a +10%-a-trade rule among 8 × 9 variants: SPA passes it 60%, the day-level DSR 29.5%. G1 reports SPA every time and gated on it only when `RESEARCH_CONFIG.g1EdgeTest` was `spa`; the owner chose SPA on 2026-10-04 (STATS-1f below).
- **2026-10-04 · Holdout registry** (`holdout.ts`):
  - **Attempts:** an attempt is spent when its configuration is registered (attempt 1 at 0.04, attempt k at 0.01/2^(k−1)). A halted, abandoned or short window is a failed attempt, and the next registration takes level k + 1.
  - **Windows:** every window has a fixed entry cutoff E, at most 28 entry days, and a registered observation tail. Attempt 1 of every universe shares one window. Attempt k ≥ 2 is registered only after the previous attempt is spent, starts the first whole UTC day after its registration and runs exactly 28 days.
  - **Family size** counts every universe ever registered, so it cannot shrink.
  - **Requirement:** max(300, n_power, closed form) and the n_power seed are frozen before any count is read.
  - **Opening:** the seal opens only after the tail and a G1 pass. There is no count-driven extension: `extendHoldout` always refuses.
  - **Storage:** a fresh registry would reset the attempt count. That is a storage rule (one append-only registry, BT-1c's `holdout-registry` branch) that this pure module cannot enforce.
- **2026-10-04 · G2 takes its level from the registry** and refuses a bootstrap with fewer than 20/level replicates. Its false-pass rate for a positive-edge claim is α/2, because the test is two-sided. The error budget is at most 0.05 family error across attempts; the bound requires valid testing under each attempt's registered selection, stopping and dependence assumptions.
- **2026-10-04 · n_power per attempt is reported with the chance of reaching n by E** (`holdoutPlan`: practice-day entry counts in 3-day runs) and the overall pass probability (P(reach) × power given n).
- **2026-10-04 · G3 is judged at an end registered with the strategy** (`G3Registration.evaluateAtMs`: at least 48 h after the start, and never earlier). Power at 48 h / 7 days / 10 days: candidate rate halved, one reason's share doubled and a 0.75-point median fill gap are always caught; a 5-point mean shift is caught 27.6% / 60% / 74.2%.
- **2026-10-04 · Demotion power, per universe at its own cap** (−10% decay, 20 trades a day, 30 days, ρ 0 / 0.05 / 0.1):
  - U2 (cap +30%): 0.987 / 0.983 / 0.927.
  - **U1 (cap +40%): 0.713 / 0.613 / 0.523, below the 80% target.** U1 would need 40 trading days (1.0 / 0.99 / 0.93) or a −12.5% detectable decay at 30 days (0.997 / 0.987 / 0.91). Supervisor ruling (2026-10-04): the shortfall is recorded as is and nothing changes in STATS-1c. Follow-up STATS-1d: U1 runs the current window plus a 40-day window and demotes on either (more demotion is the safe direction, so the owner is not needed), reporting the combined false-demotion rate and power. Until then the risk layer's hard limits are the backstop.
- **2026-10-04 · STATS-1g: external audit of core stats (S1, S3, S4); each item was reproduced before it was fixed.**
  - **S1, the G3 retained-expectancy budget.**
    - The bound subtracted four uncertain parts: the holdout lower bound, v⁺ and Δ⁺ at α/3 each, plus a 95% fill-error bound. The union bound is 3·(0.05/3) + 0.05 = 0.10, so only 90% joint coverage was established.
    - All four parts are now taken at α/4 (`VETO_COMPOSITE_PARTS` = 4, `VETO_COMPOSITE_ALPHA` = 0.0125, the holdout bound at 0.9875, the two-sided |Δ|⁺ at α/8 a side). Together they hold with at least 95%.
    - The consistency ("agree") intervals keep their own levels.
  - **S3, the n_power fingerprint.**
    - G2 compared only n, days, mean and SD, so the same returns under independent creators and under one creator matched (the audit's counterexample, now a test).
    - The fingerprint is now the exact canonical inputs (`g2PowerInputs`): the ordered, labelled walk-forward trades, the walk-forward S0 control and every simulation setting. It is the inputs themselves rather than a hash, because the stats module imports no crypto, and equal fingerprints mean equal inputs. G2 rebuilds it from the universe's own walk-forward and control (`walkForwardControl`, new).
    - The chosen n is validated independently. `simulateG2Power` re-simulates the full rule at nPower on a seed stream no search step used, and reports the Monte Carlo standard error √(p(1 − p)/simulations) of both powers.
    - G2 fails when the validation sits more than 1.645 standard errors below the target power, or validates another n.
    - The validation criterion is biased toward failing (safe direction): the search stops at the first n whose power clears the target, so the search's power at that n is selected high (winner's curse), while the independent draws are not. The validation is therefore expected to sit a little below the search value.
    - The settings are pinned, not read from the result (review B3, at 89df086 a result declaring power 0.5, target mean 0.2 or 100 simulations passed): power ≥ 0.8, target mean ≤ +5 points, ≥ 400 simulations, bootstrap replicates ≥ the gate's 20/level, the registry's family size, the attempt's α and every resampling unit. Each is a test, and so are the validation stream (re-simulated on seed·STRIDE + STRIDE − 1 − n), both standard errors and the settings' seed.
  - **S4, independence in G3.**
    - The veto-gap bounds are now cluster-robust by creator (`clusterWelchBounds`): CR2 variance (Bell–McCaffrey: each creator's residual sum scaled by (1 − n_g/n)^−½) with the Bell–McCaffrey/Imbens–Kolesár df per side, combined by Satterthwaite (supervisor ruling on review B1).
    - Why not CR1 with G − 1 df (the first version): it under-covered when one creator made half the trades, which is common in meme launches. One-sided misses at α/4 = 0.0125, 4,000 runs, ICC 0.5, true gap 0: 10 creators of 50 trades, CR1 5.35%, CR2 1.23%; 20 creators of 100 trades, CR1 8.38%, CR2 1.43%; equal sizes, both 1.02% and 1.07% (nominal 1.25%, Monte Carlo bound 1.25% + 2·SE = 1.60%). At α/8 (a side of the two-sided bound) CR2 misses 0.50% and 0.63%. With a dominant creator CR2 still over-rejects at 0.05 (6.4% and 7.2%); G3 uses the bound only at α/4 and α/8, where it holds. Tests: the coverage simulation at α/4 and α/8 (dominant and equal sizes) and a hand-computed 3-creator case pinning the variance and df. Kept trades and scored vetoed candidates carry their creator clusters (`dryRunClusters`, `VetoCounterfactuals.clusters`).
    - Missing or misaligned labels fail. Fewer than two creators a side leaves the gap unmeasured, so the worst case applies.
    - With every trade its own creator, the bound equals the classic Welch bound.
    - Cost of the old assumption, measured over 2,000 runs (creator-shared shocks with ICC 0.5, 5 trades per creator, 20 creators a side, true gap 0): the classic one-sided 95% bound missed in 17.5% of runs, the cluster-robust bound in 4.45% (test bound 0.05 + 2·SE ≈ 0.0597, and α/4 checked on the same runs).
    - The agree checks still assume independence. That narrows their intervals, so it can only add disagreements.
- **2026-10-04 · STATS-1f: G1 gates on the SPA test (owner decision, 14:33 Melbourne: "Spa").**
  - **The rule:** G1's significance test is the SPA rule exactly as registered and calibrated in STATS-1e: the global test plus the step-down promotion of the selected rule, both benchmarks (zero and S0), expected blocks 3, 5 and 7 with the worst p, and short regimes merged (`mergeShortRegimes`). α is unchanged at 0.05.
  - **The DSR:** the clamped per-trade DSR stays in the report as a descriptive number and no longer gates.
  - **Unchanged:** every other gate (G2's n ≥ max(300, n_power), CI above zero and cluster sensitivity; G3; the holdout registry rules).
  - **Registration (review of #109, B1):** the test is a closed choice, `g1Test: 'spa' | 'dsr'`, stored in the holdout registry when it is created. That happens together with the plan, before any G1 evaluation it governs: G1 runs on the walk-forward before an attempt is registered, so an attempt entry would come too late.
    - It is required (`createHoldoutRegistry` refuses a registry without it) and never changed.
    - `gateG1` takes the test from the stored registry. A caller who asks for a different test fails G1, so it cannot pick the one that passes.
    - A registry stored before this change has no test and fails G1 closed. Neither test gates without a valid stored one.
    - The backtest stamps `RESEARCH_CONFIG.g1Test` (`spa`, research-4) when `setHoldoutPlan` creates the registry. Only the owner changes that setting.
  - **Both paths are kept and tested** (the owner may want a switch later; no UI or config switch is built now). SPA runs in its own try, so a diagnostics failure cannot skip it. Without its inputs it fails closed.
  - **Sign-off pack:** on the real 64-day layout with 300 runs per scenario across 12 scenarios, the promotion rule passed a variant in at most 0.67% of runs (the gating number) and the global test rejected in at most 4.3%. Two independent seed sets were run, and the reviewer reproduced them on two more. The full run is repeatable by hand with `.github/workflows/spa-calibration.yml`.
- **2026-10-04 · STATS-1e: SPA resamples short regimes merged, calibrated on the real layout** (supervisor ruling after the #62 review).
  - **Merge rule, registered in advance (`mergeShortRegimes`):** a regime shorter than the longest expected block (7 days) merges, for resampling only, into its preceding neighbour, or into the following one when it is first. No day is dropped.
  - **On the real practice layout:** 07-20 to 09-21, 64 days, with B2 on day 1, B3 on day 51 and B4 on day 54. The registered regimes [0,1) [1,51) [51,54) [54,64) resample as [0,54) [54,64). `spaTest` reports the regimes it used (`resampleRegimes`).
  - **Calibration:** 300 runs per scenario at zero edge on independent seeds, all 12 scenarios. The promotion rule, which gates, passed a variant in at most 0.67% of runs (duplicates). The global test rejected in at most 4.3% (duplicates, autocorrelated), so both stay under 5%.
  - **Before the merge:** the reviewer measured up to 7.0% global (common shock) and 1.3% for the promotion rule. Per-scenario numbers are in `stats-simulation.test.ts`, and CI repeats the first 40 runs.
  - **Review of #96:**
    - A deterministic test shows the bootstrap resamples within the merged regimes: with the same seeds, the registered layout gives exactly the merged layout's p-values and passing set, and not the unmerged one's (this kills mutant S1).
    - CI pins the exact counts of the 40 seeded runs.
    - The full 300-run calibration runs by hand in `.github/workflows/spa-calibration.yml`, which uploads its counts as an artifact for the sign-off pack.
  - **G1:** stays on the clamped DSR until the owner signs off.
  - **G3 wording:** the dry-run inputs are cut at the registered end for decisions, and outcomes of trades entered by then are read to the end plus the outcome tail.
- **2026-10-04 · STATS-1d: a 40-day trailing reverse e-process beside the full-history one, for every universe** (supervisor ruling for U1; applying it to U2 too is safe, because it only adds a trigger). Measured with daily evaluation, 100 runs per cell:
  - **Decay from the start:** the window adds nothing within 30 days, because the two detectors see the same days. U1 stays at 0.71 at ρ 0, as STATS-1c measured. This part of the shortfall remains, and the risk layer's hard limits stay its backstop.
  - **Decay after a good stretch** (60 days at +5%, then −10%): caught within 40 days of the decay by U1 in 1.0 / 0.95 of runs, and by U2 in 1.0 / 1.0 (ρ 0 / 0.1).
  - **Full history alone** catches that late decay in at most 0.17 of runs. After a good stretch it has spent weeks betting on a decay that did not come, and it has lost the wealth it needs. This was a hole for every universe, and the window closes it.
  - **No false demotion** occurred during the good stretch.
  - **False demotion at zero edge over 120 days:** U1 0 / 0.01, U2 0 / 0.21 (ρ 0 / 0.1). U2's 0.21 at ρ 0.1 comes from its +30% cap clipping the day shocks, the intended safe side: full history alone gives 0.18. The window adds at most 5 points.
  - `DEMOTION_TRAILING_DAYS` is fixed at 40 and is not an override.
- **2026-10-04 · STATS-1c review fixes (STATS reviewer, at 7c13f54).**
  - **G2 opens at the frozen day requirement.** It used MIN_DAYS, so a holdout frozen at any other day count could never be scored. A frozen day count under MIN_DAYS fails G2, and freezeRequirement refuses one outside a test rule.
  - **The DSR moment clamp is pinned by a gate test:** a two-point return with skewness 0.87 and kurtosis 1.76 gives 0.9509 unclamped and 0.9388 clamped, so G1 fails.
  - **G3 is judged exactly at its registered end:** the judged data must end within 1 minute of `evaluateAtMs`. The inputs are cut at that moment by the G3 report tool, so a run that kept going past its end, uncut, fails.
  - **M6:** a dedicated core test shows that the seal stays closed and unburned after a G1 fail.
  - **SPA sign-off pack:** an independent-seed calibration was run at the registry's real T, the 64 practice days from 07-20 to 09-21, with 300 runs per scenario at zero edge. The global test rejected in at most 3.0% of runs and the promotion rule in at most 0.33%, both under 5%. The worst cases were the common-shock and rule-grid scenarios (3.0%, 0) and the autocorrelated one (2.0%, 0.33%). CI repeats the first 40 runs.
- **2026-10-04 · The backtest's holdout layer (BT-1c, BT-1d) runs on the STATS-1c registry** (supervisor rulings: the PR that changes an interface updates its callers; α, the registration day, the attempt budget and the end rule live in the core registry). #73 merged first, so #62 adapted it. Only the call sites changed:
  - **Registration:** `registerAttempt` / `authoriseHoldout` register each holdout's entry days [fromDay, cutoff) with today's UTC day. In the same write they freeze its requirement from the walk-forward: required trades and days, n_power and its seed (`holdout-register --required-trades --required-days --n-power --n-power-seed`).
  - **Attempt and α:** the attempt index is the core registry's next round (`nextAttemptIndex`). Core `registerHoldout` refuses a holdout whose universe is on another attempt. α and the tail end come from the core registry (`ATTEMPT_ALPHA`, `attemptAlpha`); `RULED_ALPHA` is the core constant.
  - **End rule:** the eligibility rule moved into core as `spendHoldout` (g1-failed, short and never-run, each proven against the registry, only after the tail). 'short' reads the frozen trades and days, never a caller's numbers. `endAttempt` takes no size argument any more.
  - **Opening:** `openSealedHoldout` passes the core registry its G1 result and the UTC day. A seal opens only after the tail and only at the frozen trades and days. G2's sample check also needs the frozen days (never fewer than MIN_DAYS).
  - **Windows:** runs, overlap checks and research-day guards use the run window [fromDay, tailEnd). A holdout with no frozen requirement is refused before anything runs. Attempt k ≥ 2 starts exactly on the day after registration.
  - **Floor (BT review):** `freezeRequirement` refuses anything below the owner's 300 trades on 10 entry days (`REQUIREMENT_FLOOR`, kept in the registry's attempt rule). Only a test rule on synthetic data sits lower (`setHoldoutPlan`'s test-only floor, never passed by the CLI), and G2 still refuses any requirement below max(300, n_power, closed form).
  - **Tests:** every BT-1c and BT-1d test is kept (R3, R4, E1, attempt k ≥ 2, typed sections and the rest). Their setup adds the frozen requirement where callers used to pass sizes, and opens after the synthetic tail. No assertion was loosened. The cutoff test now puts its one entry day before the data, because an empty window is refused.

## Worker (WORKER-1, `packages/worker/src/run`, `packages/worker/src/engine`, `packages/worker/src/main.ts`)

- **2026-10-04 · The worker is the shared engine on FEED-1's live Feed with a paper world.** `main.ts` implements the §12.4 process contract (config, `--reconcile`, health and drills on loopback, journal, signals, exit codes 0/1/2/3). The engine, strategy, gates, risk and exits are the same code BT-1/BT-2 run; only the feed (live), the clock (release clock) and the effect runner (`PaperWorld`) are live parts. Paper only: no signing key, and no module in `src/run`, `src/engine` or `main.ts` names a send method (guard test).
- **2026-10-04 · The environment is read in one file outside `src/`.** FEED-1's guard forbids environment reads in `src/`; `boot/environment.ts` reads the §12.4 names, the provider keys (`$CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY/<lower-case name>` as OPS-1 stores them, else the upper-case variables in GitHub Actions) and the host credentials (`telegram_chat_id`, `heartbeat_hmac_key`), and hands keys on only as `Secrets`. A new guard test keeps every other `boot/` file from reading the environment and this one from printing or writing. Any environment name that looks like key material (the runner's `KEY_ENV`) exits 2.
- **2026-10-04 · Reservations: written first, then handed to the engine.** The strategy stops an entry at `approve_risk` and puts RISK-1's `ReservationRequest` (amount q + C, limits, the snapshot's account version) in a machine-read reason. The worker writes `reserve_exposure` with LEDGER-1's `recordBookEvent(…, { accountVersion })`, so the version check, the limit check and the insert are one BEGIN IMMEDIATE. Stored: the same event goes to the engine as a world frame and is not written twice. Refused (`stale_snapshot`, over a limit): a world `reject` ends the intent, and the strategy decides again on the next snapshot under a new intent id; the old request is never retried. The account snapshot (`worker:account`) is built from the ledger's held reservations and version, read in one transaction, and published as a fact after every ledger change, so a replay sees the same snapshot.
- **2026-10-04 · Restart: the book comes back through the feed.** At start the stored book events (rebuilt from the ledger as the replay check reads them) are put on the feed as world frames, then `worker:restore` (exit plans, trackers and price bars, saved after every step in `exits.json`), then `restart`. The recorder keeps all of it, so a replay rebuilds the same book. Intents that never reached the paper network are cancelled (an entry's reservation is released; an exit's position re-triggers). A paper attempt in flight at a kill never lands: paper attempts die with the process that made them, so the paper chain reports it past its last valid height at once and the lifecycle settles it before any entry. The reconcile line is journaled only once every restored frame was applied and nothing is open; a reconcile that cannot settle within its time exits 3.
- **2026-10-04 · EXIT-1 restore acceptance: save per step.** `peak`, `trail`, `flatMet`, `partials`/`partialSeq`, the entry plan (stop, 1R, entry reserve) and the position's price bars are written after every engine step (atomic file) and restored before any market event. The attempt budget and retries still come from the book. Test: a kill with a runner open restores the same plan, trail, peak, flatMet and partials.
- **2026-10-04 · Paper fills.** `PaperWorld` draws each attempt's latency and fate from BT-1's fill model (conservative scenario) with a seed per signature, executes it at its landing slot on the latest gate pool fact with CORE-2 math and the scenario's slippage, and reports send, status and balances as world frames.
