The paper block height is the latest accepted slot notice. Before an attempt can land, TEST-2's `dryRunTrade` simulates the real build and its record is journaled as the `simulation` line (bigints as strings) that precedes the `entry` or `exit` line.
- **2026-10-04 · Live dry-run inputs** (`run/live-sim.ts`): the pool, the mint, PumpSwap's GlobalConfig and the Rent sysvar are read with TEST-2's read-only client at or after the leg's slot. The bot wallet's public address comes from `ZEROED_WALLET` (the signer makes the wallet on the host; OPS-1d passes its address); without it every leg is recorded as `build-refused`, never skipped. Compute limits use TX-1's provisional table (p99 × 1.1 of its 60 recorded mainnet swaps per kind) until TEST-2's own measurements replace it. Buy stand-ins come from `ZEROED_STANDINS` (public addresses).
- **2026-10-04 · The live strategy (`paper-u2-0`) is provisional until BT-2 registers U2.** Universe U2 (the research config's 60–240 min window after migration), candidates from FEED-1's migration events or GATE-1's migration fact; GATE-1's regime gate and hard rejects (live mode, `rugLabeller: 'RUG-1'`, always with our deployer index); a stop at the tighter of 3 × ATR(14, 1 min) and the policy's maximum distance, from the executable price after the buy; RISK-1's `evaluateEntry` at q_min; EXIT-1 for every open position, with exits never blocked (`evaluateExit` only logs and latches trips). The gross edge is configuration and **0 until research proves one**, so risk refuses every entry (owner rule: zeroed trades only when the data proves the setup). A reject is journaled when its reason changes, numbers aside, so a long wait does not fill the journal; a merge that adds no reason to the exit owner is not logged or stored.
- **2026-10-04 · Inputs the strategy reads that GATE-1 does not define:** `worker:fees:<mint>` (the pool's CORE-2 fee context, read with the pool), `worker:sol-price` (`{ value, atMs }`, a live SOL/USD read; risk needs one younger than `maxQuoteAgeMs`, which the hourly series never is), `worker:account`, `worker:restore`, `worker:halt`. Facts with their own provenance enter through a new FEED-1 frame type, `fact`, released unwrapped: `offchain` frames wrap their value with frame metadata, which the gates' parsers read as malformed.
- **2026-10-04 · Paper rent and balance inputs for risk.** A full exit closes the token account in its transaction (TX-1 `closeTokenAccount`), so its rent returns; one-time accounts are paid once per wallet, not per trade. With the conservative "no rent recovery" both counted in every trade, R5 refused even q_min on the $20 trial bankroll. The paper wallet changes only by our booked fills, which the account snapshot holds, so its balance is read as of the decision (R4); a live wallet keeps its read time.
- **2026-10-04 · Entries halt while a critical feed is down, stale (no frame for 10 s) or dropped by a drill, or the owner paused.** The state goes to the engine as the `worker:halt` fact (recorded; the strategy reads it) and to the journal as `halt`/`resume` lines; exits and monitoring go on. The watchdog's `paused` reply is applied both ways as the book's `owner` pause and kept in `control.json` across restarts. Helius is the critical feed; Alchemy and PumpPortal are second sources.
- **2026-10-04 · Live sources inside the free plans.** Slot notices and two narrow log streams on Helius and on Alchemy: creates by pump's mint authority (`TSLvdd…`, log lines kept, coverage `creates` on Helius) and migrations by pump's withdraw authority (`39azUY…`, fetched at confirmed); PumpPortal's free creates and migrations. A shortlisted mint's create transaction is fetched at confirmed (P2) when the creates stream saw it, for live H9 and H12–H14.
- **2026-10-04 · No `coverage:rugs:*` is claimed live.** RUG-1's rule makes rug coverage follow trade streams for every mint created in the look-back. Full pump and PumpSwap trade logs are far outside the free budgets (data.md: about 14M Helius credits a month for pump logs alone), so the worker starts no such stream and claims no rug coverage: H14 rejects as not covered (fail safe) until a paid stream or a backfill covers trades. The labeller is still fed every released event and its labels reach the deployer index.
- **2026-10-04 · The month's credit use** is saved within a second of each spend and at stop (`credits.json`, UTC month) and loaded into each scheduler at start, so a restart never resets the 70% halt; a new UTC month starts from zero at the next start.
- **2026-10-04 · The recorder writes DATA-1's layout, one dataset folder per boot** (`recorder/<boot>/manifest.json` schema 2 with `source: "live-recorder"`, and `days/<UTC day>/frames|raw|releases-NNN.jsonl.zst`): every frame from process start in lossless JSON (`{"$n":…}` bigints, `{"$b":…}` bytes), every fetched transaction as a schema-2 raw record (meta fields DEC-1 does not read are not fetched live and are null), and every release. Lines are flushed before the engine acts on them; files are sealed at rotation, at a clean stop, or at the next start after a kill (cut at the last whole line). DATA-1's QA reads the folder; replaying its frames in release order through the same engine and strategy reproduces the live decisions (test). RUN-1's runner now lists recorded files under subfolders by their path.
- **2026-10-04 · Off-chain event ids carry a 12-digit seq.** Events at the same moment are ordered by id; with `#9` and `#10` a restored book replayed out of order. Zero-padded, one key's events keep arrival order (FEED-1 format change before any data was recorded; its tests updated).
- **2026-10-04 · Heartbeat.** The health payload in the watchdog's `Heartbeat` shape (numeric `open_position` with the stop and the last exit attempt, `owner_chat_id` from the host credential), signed over `t\nPOST\n/heartbeat\nbody` with `heartbeat_hmac_key`; nothing is sent without the key. `/health` keeps the runner's string amounts. Both report the release commit.
- **2026-10-04 · Stored data (supervisor ruling, no personal data):** `exits.json`, `paper.json` (paper attempts), `account.json` (the paper wallet and the bot's own paper trades), `control.json` (pause and risk latches), `credits.json`, `deployers.jsonl` (the deployer index's creates, rug facts and creates/rugs coverage facts, kept for the look-back plus a day; supervisor ruling for SEED-1). Only public market data and the bot's own decisions and fills.
- **2026-10-04 · The FactSource hook (`packages/worker/src/run/facts.ts`) is where live fact producers plug in (FACTS-1, RUG-1c).** A producer gets a `FactContext` once, after the start reconcile, the live feeds' start and SEED-1's seed: a sink that puts a fact on the feed as a `fact` frame (unwrapped, as of its receipt, recorded), the worker's provider schedulers (calls at P2 or P3 only, never P0/P1, so exits and open positions keep their capacity), and `watched()` (the candidates in their window and every position not closed). Values are GATE-1's fact shapes with their own `obs`, or the worker keys `worker:fees:<mint>` and `worker:sol-price`; a producer decides nothing, the gates judge freshness and quality.
- **2026-10-04 · SEED-1 at start (supervisor rulings).** Order: reconcile, then the live sources start, then the seed, then fact producers and the loop (since changed: the loop no longer waits for the seed, see "Exits never wait for the seed"). The start waits up to 30 s for the live creates watch's `coverage:creates:start` and builds the seed up to its first slot: on a first start an RPC backfill over the H14 look-back (Helius, capped at 150,000 credits; day releases are not downloaded on the host yet, so what the cap does not reach is a bounded gap and H14 stays not covered there); on every restart only the downtime, with `buildSeed`'s fill mode from the first slot after `deployers.jsonl`, closing the saved watch. The `worker:seed` event (saved and seeded creates, the fill, saved rug facts) and the saved and seeded coverage facts go to the engine ahead of every live event, in a slot the reconcile reserves while the feed is drained, keeping their receipt times (what H14 judges coverage by). A restart whose fill fails or never ran marks the downtime an open gap on the saved watch, which its new start settles as lossy: downtime never reads as covered. Restart drill test: covered from the first seed across a kill and a 10-minute downtime, and not covered without the fill (both fail if the saved coverage or the store are removed).
- **2026-10-04 · Rehearsal 37142749019 fixes.**
  - An RPC stream reports `up` on its first open. Before, Helius streamed all run while health said it was down, so entries stayed halted.
  - A stop (a drill or a shutdown) is a disconnect: it opens the coverage gap, and the next start backfills it.
  - The worker opens no Alchemy socket. On mainnet it refused every subscription (-32601) and idled out every 30 s, costing a backfill each time.
  - Feed drills last at least two health samples.
- **2026-10-04 · Halts fail closed (review of f679188).**
  - Entries start halted (`starting`) and need a readable halt fact that says not halted.
  - A ledger refusal of an event the engine applied adds a halt reason for the rest of the process: entries off, exits on, shown in health.
- **2026-10-04 · No key in any output.** Every stdout, stderr, journal and recorder line is redacted for:
  - the credential values read at start;
  - key-shaped URL parts (Helius `api-key=`, Alchemy `/v2/<key>`, Telegram bot tokens).
- **2026-10-04 · One-time rent is paid at the paper wallet's setup.** Risk's `rent.oneTime` comes from the wallet state.
  - The venue's volume-accumulator rent (137 bytes at the configured rate) leaves the paper wallet once, when it is funded.
  - If the first trade carried it instead, R14's cost gate would refuse that trade at the trial size forever, and the account would never be made.
  - The live path needs the same setup step before its first trade (signer work, owner approval).
- **2026-10-04 · S0 shakedown mode (supervisor ruling).**
  - `ZEROED_STRATEGY=S0` draws each candidate's entry moment in its window from sha256(run id, mint), then applies the same gates, risk and exits.
  - It is journaled as non-qualifying.
  - `ZEROED_PAPER_EDGE_PPM` is refused in any mode other than paper, and without S0.
- **2026-10-04 · Live market inputs.**
  - SOL/USD comes from Coinbase Exchange's public SOL-USD ticker over WebSocket (no key, the exchange FACTS-1 reads bars from). Each price is dated at the trade's exchange time, so a stalled feed ages. This feed is critical.
  - Every candidate's and open position's pool gets a logs watch named `trades:<pool>` (P1 for a held pool, P3 otherwise).
  - The swaps from that watch give the pool's fee terms, a flat schedule at the observed rates as in BT-1. This applies when no fee-context fact exists.
  - The same swaps feed EXIT-1's deployer-sell trigger: sales by the create's creator and signer since the entry, as a share of the create's total supply. Linked wallets are not known, so the "cluster" is those two.
  - A position whose trigger cannot be judged says so once in the journal.
- **2026-10-04 · Commands.** The API serves no command.
  - Pause, close and session control are refused (403) and journaled with the auth level each needs.
  - Pause stays with the watchdog's signed `/pause`.
- **2026-10-04 · Measured delays (supervisor ruling).** Once a minute, the newest creates signature seen at processed is read at confirmed.
  - Both arrival times go to the recorder's `delays` table, with any PumpPortal sighting of the same signature.
  - The manifest lists each path's commitment.
  - Cost: one Helius credit a minute.
- **2026-10-04 · Restarts take partials from the book.** Each exit owner that sold is one partial, and the saved file is corrected when it disagrees.
- **2026-10-04 · Heartbeat units.** The heartbeat's and health's entry, stop and mark are one price unit (executable price, PRICE_SCALE). Unknown is null, and a mark older than 30 s is null.
- **2026-10-04 · RUN-1c contract (#49).**
  - Health fields:
    - `open_position` carries the trade (its position id) and the mark: the executable sale price of the holding with its slot and read time. With no mark yet it sends the entry price at time 0, which the runner reads as unmeasured.
    - `unresolved_intents.trades` lists each intent's trade.
    - `exit_capable` means reconciled, with every critical chain feed connected, fresh and not dropped.
    - `quota` comes from the Helius, Alchemy and Jupiter schedulers. Each lists credits since boot by class, rounded up so they sum (stream bytes count as P3), plus the plan's monthly credits.
    - `lookups` counts the TxFetcher's found transactions by latency bucket.
  - Journal lines:
    - `coverage_gap` comes from FEED-1's coverage facts: one line when a gap opens and one when it closes, under one `gap_id`. A bounded gap reported at once is one line, its span estimated at 400 ms a slot.
    - Rejects are `action: reject` with typed `gate_reasons` ({gate, code, detail}), where gate is H1–H16, `regime`, a risk control, `stop` or `worker`. An entry is `action: enter`.
  - The runner counts every other decision line (the lifecycle steps) as an untyped rejection. That inflates its rate; RUN-1 owns the fix.
  - Exposure: each trade open or in flight when a process stops is kept in `exposure.json` across a `--reconcile`. The next full start journals one `exposure` line per trade, in the background so exits never wait:
    - the window runs from the last journal line before the stop;
    - the worst move is the largest move of the pool price against the last spot saved with the exit plan, before and after every swap on the pool read from chain history (at most 200 transactions);
    - when the reading cannot be done, it is null with the reason.
- **2026-10-04 · Delay stamps use a monotonic clock.** `delay_ms` is measured on `performance.now()`; wall times are kept for display only (supervisor refinement).
- **2026-10-04 · Re-review of #48 (supervisor rulings).**
  - **FACTS-1 merges before the qualifying run.** FACTS-1 is #54 plus FACTS-1b. Until it merges, no live producer makes GATE-1's facts, so every candidate is rejected (H16 not covered, regime unknown) and nothing trades, S0 included.
  - **The seed runs after the live sources start.** The order is reconcile, sources start, the seed up to the live creates watch's first slot, then the fact producers and the loop. Recorded in the SEED-1 entry above, and repeated here because the original order was seed first.
  - **A divergence halt is cleared by a restart.** A ledger/book divergence halts entries for the rest of the process. A restart rebuilds the book from the ledger, so the halt clears then. This is paper only, and both the halt and the new boot are journaled.
  - **Qualifying is real, and it fails closed.**
    - When `packages/runner/qualifying-run.json` is in the release, a worker whose `ZEROED_RUN_ID` is unset, empty, or that file's name is the qualifying run. The host's worker unit sets no run id; rehearsals and the shakedown set theirs.
    - The qualifying run refuses to start in each of these cases: with S0, with a paper edge, without a registered strategy, or with an unreadable file.
    - The registered list is `REGISTERED_STRATEGIES` in the run contract. It is empty until BT-2 registers a strategy.
    - The report's `qualifying_start` check fails a named host run when any `start` line has an unregistered entry rule, a paper edge, or `qualifying` other than true.
  - **Exits per universe (CFG-2).** The entry intent key starts with the universe (`entry:<mint>:<universe>.<version>.<n>`). The exit plan stores it, and a restored plan keeps it. A plan saved before this takes it from its intent key, never from the worker's own universe.
  - **Setup rent is an account cost (risk review, RISK-1b).** It is one `AccountHistory.costs` entry (`wallet_setup`) valued at the setup SOL price and rounded up, so equity and the day and week losses include it. It is not a closed trade, so R8, R11, R15 and trade statistics never see it; a first real losing trade starts no cooldown because of it.
    - AccountHistory has no cost record, so it rides with the closed trades.
    - It carries the policy's largest notional, so R15 never caps a size by it.
    - It may count once toward R8 (the safe side).
    - A cost field in RISK-1b would be the cleaner shape.
  - **Redactions in recorded files are listed.** Each redaction is named, with its count, in the manifest's `coverage_gaps`, so a replay difference there is explained.
  - **Simulation lines carry `finalExit`, `simulatedSlot` and `standIn`** in full, null when unknown.
  - **The rehearsal workflow runs S0 with a paper-only edge of 400,000 ppm.** The supervisor approved this as a `.github` change. It lets trades open, so the restart drills can run mid-trade.
- **2026-10-04 · RUN-1d contract (WORKER-1b).**
  - `pending_exits`: every trade whose position is exit-requested, pending or blocked, or that has an exit intent not yet final.
  - `open_position.universe`: from the plan, else the entry key.
  - `recovered` is journaled once per full start, after the reconcile. Its source is `chain` when the state dir began with no ledger (marked by `cold_start` until a full start journals it), else `state`. It lists `pending_exits` and each open position with its universe. A genuine first boot therefore reports `recovered` with source `chain` and nothing kept. A position with no universe on record is listed as `none on record` (the runner counts it as missing, and the worker starts sell-only and flattens it). A boot whose ledger cannot be opened stops before its `start` line (the ledger opens first), so the journal has no line for that boot; the error is in the process log and the exit code.
  - `POST /drill/drop-rpc {ms}` (same token and codes as drop-feed) does three things:
    - drops every feed;
    - refuses every provider RPC through `RpcCut` (a network error, never a shed);
    - makes `exit_capable` false until the cut ends.
  - `--reconcile-only` does the following:
    - reconciles on its own state dir and journals `recovered`;
    - serves only `/health`: no API, drills, heartbeat, seed or fact producers;
    - drains the live feeds unread, so `exit_capable` is real while nothing is decided;
    - runs until SIGTERM.
  - The contract types arrive with #64. Until then the two health fields ride on top of `Health`, and `recovered` widens the journal kind.
- **2026-10-04 · A restored position whose universe the policy lacks (EXIT-1b review, supervisor ruling).** Exits are never blocked, so the worker still starts, as follows:
  - it runs sell-only for the whole process: an entry halt reason per such position, journaled and logged as an alert;
  - the reasons are listed in the start line's `sell_only`;
  - the strategy flattens the position through the global exit ladder: that universe's exit block is replaced by one with zero time stops and no take-profit, so the whole position goes at once;
  - the exit's decision line carries `universe missing: flatten`.
  Before this, `exitsFor` threw and the position got no exit decision.
- **2026-10-04 · Rehearsal 37148935094 (S0): the first start no longer reads 14 days of creates over RPC.** That read spent about 4,000 Helius credits in the first minutes; at roughly 64,000 creates a day, a full look-back is far beyond the free month. It also held the start, so the loop and every exit waited. A partial read leaves H14 not covered anyway.
  - A first start now seeds from day releases only. There are none on the host yet, so the look-back is a gap until the live watch has run through it.
  - Restarts still fill the downtime over RPC.
  - The start waits at most 90 s for the seed or the fill (`seedMaxMs`). After that the downtime reads as a gap.
- **2026-10-04 · Exits never wait for the seed (review of 9fdf837).** The loop starts right after the reconcile; until the seed is placed, entries halt with `deployer index seeding`, and exits and monitoring run.
  - The seed is a `worker:seed` fact on the live feed, so the recorder keeps it as a frame and a replay feeds it in the same place. Its coverage history is dated in the slot reserved at reconcile, ahead of every live event, and the strategy merges it into every history read, so H14 judges coverage in the same order as before.
  - While the halt names the seeding, the strategy holds the deployer index's live events back and observes them after the seed, in release order: the index is still seeded before it observes any live event. No entry runs while events wait, even on the halt that ends the seeding (it can be released just before the seed).
  - A seed that loses the 90 s race is aborted: the backfill checks the signal before every RPC call, so no page or transaction is fetched after it.
  - With #45 merged, the fill gets `liveStart`, the live watch's start moment as the feed placed it (its slot also bounds `asOf`). `close.at` is not passed: the worker dates all seed coverage in the reserved slot, below the live start, so the saved gap always comes before the start and the failover case it guards cannot arise.
  - Tests: an exit during a seed that never answers, before `seedMaxMs` (fails when the loop waits for the seed); a restart whose fill misses the cap is not covered across the downtime and stops its RPC; the restart drill's seed is taken in full, never refused (fails without the held-back events); no call after an abort.
- **2026-10-04 · A restart rebuilds the stored book before it decides, and a killed process writes no state file (root cause of the one-off `--reconcile` failure).** The worker's own start facts (`worker:halt`, `worker:restore`) are dated 1 ms after the stored book's world frames, so every restored event sorts before them whatever the clock's resolution.
  - The failure: all those frames are placed in the same off-chain slot, so a tie on receipt time was broken by id, and `worker:halt` sorts before `world#…` ("work" < "worl"). The strategy then saw an intent the ledger had already carried to sent, cancelled it ("no slot height yet"), and the engine refused the restored `prepare`, `sign` and `submit` that followed. The two books ended apart: `--reconcile` reported success from the engine's and wrote `open_intents` 1 from the ledger's. It reproduced about once in 50 runs of the test under full-suite load, never alone.
  - The saved exit plans come before the halt fact (1 ms apart). With both dated after the restored frames at one instant, the halt sorted first by id; the strategy manages positions on any market event, so on the halt it built a fresh plan and tracker from the fill and could exit on them (a tracker that had met its flat target got the flat time stop) before the saved plans arrived. Test: a restored tracker past its flat deadline with `flatMet` keeps its position (fails with the halt first).
  - A refused world event the ledger already holds is now a divergence: entries halt until a restart, with a journal line, instead of the books quietly differing.
  - A paper simulation can answer after the worker was killed or stopped. `PaperWorld.stop()` ends its state writes, so a dead process never writes `paper.json` or `open_intents` over what the next one (the host unit's ExecStartPre) wrote.
  - Tests: the restored events are released before the halt fact on a tying clock (8 of them came after it before the fix); a simulation answering after a kill leaves both files as the successor wrote them.
  - Most worker test failures that came and went between CI runs of the same commit were the pid, not timing: the boot id was `<time>-<pid>` and seeds the paper world's fill draws (`paper:<boot>`), so the test process's pid decided whether an attempt drew a landing tail or a drop. With pid 16431, 13 of 27 tests in `worker-flow` and `run1c` failed every time; 16432 to 16434 passed. The harness now pins the boot (`boot-<n>`, the n-th worker on a state folder). RUN-1d's test settings stay (LANDS with no landing tail; the "lands failed" test never drops), so those tests say what they test. Production keeps the per-boot seed; the boot names the recorder folder and is in its manifest and the journal's start line (with `seed`), so a parity replay draws the same. The recorder parity tests that failed with it failed on their precondition (an exit happened), not on parity.
  - Test files run in parallel processes, and several bound the same address: the harness default health port 18790 and the API's live default 8788 (every test that set only a health port), 18796/18797 in both `worker-api` and `worker-contract`, 18860/18861 in both `facts-source` and `market-inputs`. A clash fails one start. The harness now gives each worker its own pair (21000 and up, by Vitest pool and count), and the fixed ones were moved apart (`worker-api` to 18792/18793, `facts-source` to 18900 and up, FACTS-1e's change).
  - The harness's virtual clock moves only when a timer callback runs, so a test that asserts on a paper effect inside a fixed window can starve when the container runs several suites at once. `until(m, maxMs, ready)` waits for the effect instead, bounded; the abandon test uses it. Draining the queued callbacks per step was tried and is worse: they each advance the clock, so a run reaches its end in fewer steps.
- **2026-10-04 · The app's read API (`run/api.ts`) implements UI-2's contract exactly and is checked by the app's own strict schemas** (`checkEnvelope` with `schemaFor`, test against a worker that made paper trades). Paper only: other modes' paths answer 404, the backtest report is `null` (the app loads reports from the release). Loopback only, `ZEROED_API_ADDR` (default `127.0.0.1:8788`, refused unless loopback and not the health port); on the host OPS publishes it to the owner's tailnet with `tailscale serve`, so the worker never binds anything else (supervisor ruling). GET only: no command is served yet, because a command needs its own auth level and loopback (where tailscale's proxied reads arrive) is no proof of anything; pause stays with the watchdog's `/pause`. Paper costs: venue and creator fees and the scenario's extra slippage come from each paper fill; priority, tip and base fee from every attempt's outcome, failed ones included; rent per the account-close outcome (PAPER-1 below replaced "rent shows 0"). Planned and realized R, MFE and MAE are null until the exit plan is kept with the trade record.
- **REC-1: rejected candidates' pools are watched until window end + tMax (supervisor ruling, 2026-10-04).** G3's counterfactual scorer needs every swap a hypothetical entry at any moment of the window would see until its exit, and a candidate's pool watch used to stop at its window end. A candidate that was evaluated and rejected (its last reason is not null) keeps its pool in `watchedPools()` (not held, so P3) until its window end plus its universe's `tMaxMs` (120 min), through that moment itself (an exit at exactly the maximum hold needs it), pruned at the first event after; a candidate never evaluated gets no tail. Contract for G3: swaps of such a pool are recorded until windowEnd + tMax; a gap (a refused or halted watch, a disconnect) is a recorded `coverage:trades:<pool>:gap`, which the scorer treats as unknown and censors. At the 70% budget halt the stream drops P2–P3 watches first, the tail included, and records the open gap, while an open position's pool (P1) stays (test). **Cost:** the pool watches run on the Helius socket, metered at 2 credits per 0.1 MB: at the budget's planning figure (60 swaps a minute at 1.84 KB each) a tail pool costs about 2.2 credits a minute, up to about 265 credits over its 120 minutes, against about 16.2 credits a minute for everything below the halt. Seven busy tails at once would take the whole allowance; real rejected coins are usually far quieter hours after migration, but that is not measured. The halt sheds tails first, so the cost can never starve an open position, only cut G3's sample (recorded as gaps). **Cap (supervisor ruling):** at most `maxTails` tails at once (a strategy setting: `StrategyConfig.maxTails`, set in `run/settings.ts`, default 3, about 6.6 credits a minute at the planning figure); a rejected candidate leaving its window while the cap is full is not watched and is logged as a decision `no tail` with `tail cap <n>`, which G3 censors with that reason, never imputes. A slot frees when a tail ends. Candidates leave at their window end on every event, also while entries are halted (a feed down, a pause, seeding, sell-only, a divergence), so a tail always starts at the window end (review of 4730074; test). **Known issue found (not REC-1's scope):** when the halt drops a P3 watch inside the stream, `PoolWatch` still lists it as watched, so after the halt lifts that pool is not watched again until it leaves the list and comes back. `#entries` keeps its own `now >= to` backstop, so an ended window is never an entry whichever pass runs first; a window ending on the very event where the gates first pass gives no entry (`rec-same-event.test.ts`, which fails with the backstop removed and the passes swapped).
- **POOL-1: a pool watch the stream drops by itself is watched again (supervisor ruling, 2026-10-04).** The stream drops a watch without its owner asking in two ways: at the 70% budget halt it removes every P2–P3 watch (an open position's P0–P1 watches stay), and a server may refuse a subscription (for example a subscription limit), at any priority, held pools (P1) included; a refused subscription used to stay dead until the next reconnect. `PoolWatch` kept listing both as watched, so a candidate pool dropped at the halt was never watched again, and a refused held pool, the exit-critical case, delivered no swaps until a reconnect. `RpcStream.onDropped` now tells listeners of both (`halted`, `refused`). `PoolWatch` forgets the watch; after a halt it watches again at the first sync once the stream takes P3 watches (a new month resets the budget; while halted a new watch is refused inside the stream with no request sent); after a refusal it unwatches it (so a reconnect does not subscribe it twice) and asks again after a wait of 2 s, doubling per refusal up to 60 s, forgotten when the pool leaves the list. Coverage: the halted or refused range is the open gap the stream already records, and the new watch's `coverage:trades:<pool>:start` marks where coverage resumes, as the stream's existing semantics for open-ended gaps say. Other owners of refused watches (creates, migrations, slots) are unchanged: their subscription stays and the next reconnect re-subscribes it. Review of 813c7fd: a watch the server accepts again (`RpcStream.onServed`) forgets its wait, so a refusal hours later waits 2 s again, not an old doubled wait. POS-1's `setPriority` drops a watch lowered above P1 while halted; it now reports that drop too, so a held pool that becomes a candidate's again during a halt is forgotten and watched again once the halt lifts (test).

## Worker follow-ups (WORKER-1c, `packages/worker/src/run`, `packages/core/src/risk/evaluate.ts`, `packages/runner/src/runner.ts`)

- **2026-10-04 · PERSIST-1 in the worker (item 1).**
  - **Load.** The worker loads `deployer-state.json` from its state dir at construction, right after the stored book's world frames and before the start facts. A file that does not hold up is discarded, the reason is logged, and the start is a fresh one (not covered).
  - **Restore.** The restored index and labeller travel inside the recorded `worker:seed` fact (`state`), and the strategy restores them from that fact. So a replay of the recording rebuilds the same state; the strategy never reads the file itself. Live events wait for the seed as before, so the index is whole before any decision.
  - **Downtime fill.** The fill starts at the saved moment, and its `close` is the restart gap `loadState` opened on the live creates watch, so one fill closes it. The rug half stays not covered after a restart until the deployer checks (item 4) cover each creator.
  - **Saves.** A save holds the index (entries older than the look-back plus a day left out), the labeller's tables and every coverage fact, the restored history included, as of the latest released moment. It is written every 5 minutes and at a clean stop. It is refused until the seed (or the restore) is applied, so an unseeded index never replaces a good file; a crash keeps the last periodic save.
  - **Fill budget.** The fill's credits come from PERSIST-1's `DailyBudget`: 20,000 a UTC day in `fill-budget.json` (since S0-ZERO derived from the Helius plan, 3,870 a day; see "Candidate trade coverage from the migration"). A restart's fill reserves its cap first and gives back the unused part after, so a reboot loop stops there.
  - **Fix in `DailyBudget`.** The day now rolls only forward, and a file dated later than today keeps its spend. Before, a clock stepped back gave a fresh budget, the same flaw the review found in the deployer checks.
  - **Rename.** `strategy.markOf` is renamed `displayQuoteOf` (review N3), so it is never read as risk's mark (RISK-MARK's executable mark). It is a display value only (/health and the heartbeat): the whole holding's sale quote at the pool's last read, venue fees and price impact included, network fees, the exit slippage allowance and landing not. Not called a spot price, because it is not one (spot is the pool's marginal price before impact).
- **2026-10-04 · Every open position in `/health`.** `open_positions` lists each position that is not closed and not still opening, oldest first (by the account's open time, then id), each in `open_position`'s shape (`run/open-positions.ts`). `open_position` stays as the first entry, so the watchdog and older readers keep working. The runner reads the whole list where it counts positions: the kept positions at a restart, the exposed trades, trade-id completeness and the kill reply's universe check. Moving the watchdog's heartbeat onto the list is a follow-up.
- **2026-10-04 · Day and week loss: the stricter of two measures (supervisor ruling).** `dayLoss`/`weekLoss` (R7, R9 and the room each entry has left) are now the larger of the loss from realized equity at the Melbourne boundary, as before, and the loss from the marked equity recorded at that boundary (`markedAtDayStart`/`markedAtWeekStart`, net of flows since). A boundary with no recorded mark uses the realized loss only, never zero. This only tightens. Moving the limits onto the marked figure alone stays the owner's call. Note: a mark taken exactly at the boundary is never above realized equity at it (marked equity counts open losses, never gains), so the marked measure binds mainly when the mark was taken late, after a realized gain.
- **2026-10-04 · The worker records the marks.** After the reconcile, each engine step asks risk for the figures it would use now (`riskSnapshot`, read-only). It then records, in `account.json`: the first equity seen at or after each Melbourne day and week start, with the time it was taken (later than the boundary when the worker was down then; the process log says so); and the highest economic NAV seen since the last kill-switch re-arm (none while NAV cannot be valued, for example with a position open, since positions still carry no mark: the RISK-MARK card). A non-positive NAV is never recorded. The account fact hands risk this day's and this week's marks only, and `navMarks` as that one peak. With no deposits or withdrawals in paper, the peak alone gives R10 the same NAV high-water mark as keeping every observation, and the file stays small. A change puts the account fact again, so the next evaluation sees it.
- **2026-10-04 · The marks come from the marked account (RISK-MARK follow-up, risk review spec).** `#markAccount` asks risk for its figures on the account each open position marked at its executable mark (marks.ts `riskAccount`, the path the strategy's exits use, `fallback: true`), from the position's newest market under rule M (`poolOf`) at the live SOL price; never the raw account fact, whose marks are null (a total loss).
  - A day or week boundary is recorded only when every open position has a fresh, non-null mark (none open counts); otherwise the next look takes it. A null mark would freeze a total-loss equity as the day start and later read as a gain.
  - The NAV peak comes from risk's `nav` only, which is non-null only when every mark is fresh, so it now also rises while a position is open.
  - Note on the two measures: risk counts no unrealized gain (a mark is capped at its notional), so with no flows the realized measure (the whole open loss since entry) is never below the marked one, and risk takes the larger. A fall in the mark after the boundary shows in the marked measure exactly; the `daily_loss` trip at trial sizes comes from both.
  - Tests (`boundary-marks.test.ts`, each failing before): a boundary with an open position records equity at the executable mark, not a total loss; a boundary seen with a stale mark waits for a fresh one; after an 85% fall the marked measure equals the capped fall in the executable value since the boundary and `daily_loss` trips; the NAV peak rises with a position open. The boundary tests reach a boundary through a restart with yesterday's marks (the hold is at most 2 hours, the harness's market is anchored at one moment). Mutants killed: boundary gating dropped in the account, the raw account fact used, `marked` forced true (3/3).
- **2026-10-04 · The deployer check runs live (RUG-1c wiring).** When a candidate's last reasons include H14's rug half not covered with its deployer check missing or not accepted (H16 `not-covered`, input `coverage`, needed by H14, detail starting `rug labels unavailable …: `), `LiveFacts` asks for a check of that candidate's creator. That is at most once per `minReadGapMs` (a minute) per candidate, and only once the creator is known (the strategy records it from the released create) and the feed has a tip. The request carries the deployer index's mints by that creator from `rugCheckFromMs(now − look-back)` on (the candidate excepted) and is as of the tip. `FactReaders.readDeployerCheck` runs the check through the per-creator cache below, over Helius RPC at P2 under the per-candidate credit cap (`rug-check-1`, 500 credits), and ingests what it found: a new `rug:<mint>` label per rug, then `coverage:rugs:deployer:<creator>`. A capped or failed check is ingested too, so H14 sees why it stays not covered. The creates half of H14 and a missing labeller ask for nothing, since a deployer check cannot cover them. - **2026-10-04 · Deployer checks are cached per creator, under a daily cap (review of #99, supervisor ruling).** Without a cache, H14's 300-slot freshness would bring a new check every minute for each candidate's whole window: up to 1.8M credits an hour in the worst case, against about 23,000 a day under the Helius halt share. `DeployerChecks` keeps each creator's prior mints. A `rug`, `clear` or `unjudged` answer is final and never read again (a rug's label is released once), but it counts only for checks at or after the slot it was read at. A mint still `open`, or not read in full, is read again at most once a minute per creator, whichever candidate asks, timed from that creator's last read. A prior mint the index lists that the cache does not hold is always read, so a new launch is never missed. The coverage fact is issued at the asking slot from the cache. A mint counts only when its answer is final or was read within `maxLagSlots` (300); otherwise it is listed `unfetched`, so H14 stays not covered (fail closed). Every read also spends from `DEPLOYER_CHECK_CREDITS_PER_DAY`, 5,000 Helius credits a UTC day (a trial value: about 34 checks at the measured 146 credits, or 10 at the 500 cap). Past it nothing is read and those mints stay unfetched. In `budget.ts` the first check of a candidate counts up to the per-candidate cap (`deployerCheckCreditsOnce`), and the daily cap comes off the top like the holder scans, so the plan carries 2 candidates instead of 3. `plannedHeliusPerDay` shows the planned worst case (both daily caps spent and the planned candidates all day) under the halt share. The cache lives in memory, so a restart reads a creator's mints again. The day's spend is kept in `deployer-check-spend.json` in the state dir, so a restart cannot spend the cap again; an unreadable file counts the day as spent. Review fixes (2026-10-04): the day only rolls forward, so a clock stepped back (NTP, a VM restore) keeps today's spend; each read reserves its cap in the file before it starts and gives back the unused part after, so a crash mid-read never loses spend. One creator's checks run one at a time (single flight per creator), so a pending mint is read once. No answer, final or not, is used for a check whose slot is earlier than the read that gave it, so a check never claims a label observed after its own moment.

- **2026-10-04 · A fill is journaled before the ledger lets the position go (WORKER-ORDER, ARCHITECTURE §12.4).** The desk wrote the `entry`/`exit` line after the ledger commit. A kill between the two left the position closed in the ledger with no `exit` line, and the runner (which forgives a vanished position only by its `exit` line after the last reply) reported "not recovered: position, exit": the same false failure CI-1b found in the stub. Now the desk computes the step, writes the line, then commits.
  - **The other way round** (line written, ledger not): the paper world saves a landing before it reports it, so the restart's reconcile books the same fill again. The desk reads the journal's fill lines at start (`journaledFillKeys`: intent and tokens) and does not write that line a second time.
  - **account.json behind the ledger.** A kill after the commit and before account.json lost the trade's close for good (no close, P&L or wallet credit). At start the worker lists fills the ledger holds and the account does not (`PaperAccount.behind`) and records them at the first SOL price, dated and explained by the fill's own journal line, which is now always there.
  - **Tests** (`write-order.test.ts`): a crash image of the state dir is taken right after the first of the exit fill's two writes (a `crashPoint` test seam on the desk), and a new worker starts on it. The runner's `closedSince` and `recoveredState` pass; the reverse case closes the position with one fill and one line; an image after the commit gets the account's close back. Mutants: the ledger written first (the runner check fails with "not recovered: exit, position", the CI-1b symptom), the dedupe dropped (two exit lines), the catch-up dropped (the trade stays open): 3/3 killed.
  - A fill booked at a start reconcile before any SOL price is valued as before (no price: a loss of the notional, the safe side).
  - **Review fix (012QdD, FAIL at b849d9f): each caught-up fill at its own rate (PAPER-1).** The catch-up valued a missed fill at the SOL/USD price after the restart. Every `entry`/`exit` line now carries `sol_usd`, the price the desk books it at (micro-dollars, null before the first price), written before the ledger commit. The catch-up uses it: the trade's `openSolPrice`, or the close's `netPnl` and `closeSolPrice`. A null there is valued as the live path did (null: a loss of the notional). Only a line without the field (written before this change) falls back to the price now, flagged `fill sol_usd unknown: valued at the price after restart` in the fill's reasons and the log line. Tests (each failing before): an exit crash at `fill-committed` with SOL doubled before the restart books `netPnl` at the fill-time price; a kill after the entry commit opens the trade with the line's time, rate, notional and wallet debit; the null and missing-field variants; equal partial fills give distinct keys. Mutants killed: catch-up at the price now, no `sol_usd` on the line, `behind()` entry branch, `behind()` exit branch, dedupe skip, catch-up call, key without tokens, null rate valued at the price now, flag dropped from the log (9). Equivalent: the flag dropped from the reasons, since the account keeps no free-form reasons (the log carries it).
  - **Ruling (supervisor, 2026-10-04): entries are line-first too.** The write-ahead order holds for entries as for exits: the journal leads, and a restart reconciles from the paper world. §12.4 states the real order for both. A fill booked again after a kill between its line and the ledger commit is recorded with that line's time, reasons and `sol_usd`, not the booking moment's (before this, a re-booked entry at a start reconcile had no SOL price yet and lost its notional reason). Tests (each failing before where behaviour changed): a kill between the entry line and the ledger commit books the entry once from the paper world, at the line's rate, time and notional, with one line and one wallet debit; a kill after both entry writes does not book it twice. Mutants killed: the held fill not recorded, the held line ignored (second line), the held rate, reasons and time each ignored, the worker ignoring the fill's rate (6). Equivalent: the desk's already-held check (`was.fills.length === s.fills.length`) removed, because a reconcile is legal only from `confirmed_fill`, `failed` or `expired_unfilled` (`intent.ts`), and fills are set only by a reconcile, so an intent that already holds its fill is never booked again.
  - **Review fix after merge (01NGXu, FAIL on 16555b5): the journal's fill lines are streamed.** `readJournalFills` ran at every boot with `readFileSync` over the whole journal; a long shakedown journal under `MemoryMax=800M` could OOM-kill the process that owns exits on each restart. It now reads through `journalLines` (1 MB chunks, EXIT-1g N7) and parses only lines holding `"kind":"entry"` or `"kind":"exit"`. Test (`journal-fills.test.ts`, failing before): with the whole-file read made to throw, chunk sizes 1, 3, 17, 64, 1 MB and the default all find the fill lines on both sides of every boundary and skip other and torn lines. Mutants killed: the kind filter dropped, exit lines dropped, stopping after the first fill, a missing file not handled (4).

## Fact producers (FACTS-1, `packages/core/src/facts`, `packages/worker/src/facts`)

- **One producer, fed released events only.** `FactProducer.observe` takes every market event in release order and returns the gate facts it changes; `FactFeed` wraps any Feed and releases each fact at its source's moment with id `<source id>~<key>` (sorts right after the source: ids use base58, digits and ASCII punctuation, all below `~`). The engine stores facts like any event, so GATE-1 reads them unchanged and the decision log names each fact's source. Live (WORKER-1) and the backtest (BT-2) wrap their Feed with the same class: same events, same facts (parity test over LiveFeed, `replayRecorded`, `frameEvents` + `createReplay` and DATA-1 raw records).
- **Raw reads are events too.** Live readers never make a fact: they ingest the provider's answer, trimmed, under a raw key (`read:accounts|holders|sim|rugcheck|goplus|jupiter-audit|funder:*`, `sol-usd`, `read:defillama:pump-curve-volume`, `read:exec-health`), and the producer maps it. Recorded answers replay to the same facts.
- **Processed never makes a fact.** Fetched transactions are confirmed; log lines are processed unless the watch was opened at `confirmed` (new FEED-1 `WatchOptions.commitment`, carried in the log frame and its events; its ids and dedup key name the commitment so both copies stay apart). Account reads go through `getMultipleAccounts` at confirmed (TEST-2's reads are processed and stay its own).
- **Nothing proves a negative by absence.** The curve fact exists only from a CompleteEvent (`complete: true`); no trade or silence is read as "not complete". The migration fact needs the CompleteEvent, the migration event and its CreatePoolEvent, all confirmed. A missing vault, a vault that is not the pool's own token account of the right mint, or an undecodable LP mint makes no pool or LP fact.
- **Coverage, not hope, keeps event-built state fresh.** Candles depend on every swap since the pool opened, so their `obs.slot` is the pool's first slot and they carry `stream: trades:<pool>`; `gates/stream:<name>` facts follow FEED-1's `coverage:<name>:start|gap|resume` and `chain:slot` (open gap: flagged `partial`, rejects; bounded gap or a cut/undecodable log on the stream's watch moves `gapFreeSince` past it; resume closes an open gap with no loss). A gap after migration rejects H11 for the rest of the coin's life: strict, and safe.
- **Candle prices** use effective quote (vault + signed virtual) with reserves before the trade from the event and after it by the base amount and the lp-adjusted quote amount (checked on real consecutive swaps: each trade's after equals the next trade's before). Open is the price before a minute's first trade, high the max over before and after prices, close the price after its last trade. A trade whose price cannot be formed, or stamped before the newest candle, flags the candles `partial`. Only the first `chaseCheckAfterMs + 1 min` and the last `candleWindowMs + 1 min` are kept.
- **Insiders (H13), operational definition (hypothesis, calibrate):** creation-slot buyers (buys in slots s0..s0+2) plus `devCluster`: first buyers funded by the dev, or by the dev's own first funder (`core/src/facts/funding.ts`, one rule for live and the backtest supplement). On the recorded coin three of six first buyers share the dev's funder, a bundle; a shared funder can also be an exchange hot wallet, which over-links, so the rule only adds rejects (accepted by the supervisor, 2026-10-03). First buyers are every buyer whose first buy is in a slot up to the slot where the 20th distinct buyer appears (a boundary slot counts whole: order inside a slot is unknown for fetched transactions), or all curve buyers once the curve completed. `complete` only when `mint-txs:<mint>` coverage is gap-free from s0 through that slot and the dev and every first buyer have a complete funder read that is point in time (below). The first funder is read by one decoder (`core/src/chain/system.ts`: System Transfer, TransferWithSeed, CreateAccount, CreateAccountWithSeed, top level and inner).
- **Cross-checks** keep only reads younger than `maxQuoteAgeMs` at the newest read; the fact is as old as its oldest kept read. RugCheck uses the full report's `mintAuthority`/`freezeAuthority` (the summary has no authorities); GoPlus `mintable.status`/`freezable.status`; Jupiter `audit.*Disabled`. GoPlus has no verified rate limit: 1 request per 5 s until one is measured.
- **Graduate survival** (regime): a migration is dated at +30 min by the last reserve before the mark when the pool's trades are covered since migration, else by a confirmed account read landing within a minute after the mark; otherwise the graduate is left out (unknown), never guessed.
- **Funding is point in time.** `readFunder`/`funderOf(wallet, { asOfSlot, beforeSlot, maxPages, maxTransactions })` skip every signature after the decision slot (RPC answers with today's history) and read the wallet's successful transactions oldest first, up to its first buy of the mint (the create, for the dev), until one credits it with SOL from another account. A first transaction that funds nothing (a third party creating its token account, a close refund) is passed over (review finding). No credit before the first buy, a history past the page cap, or more transactions than the budget: incomplete, never "no funder". The producer and `insiderLinks` count only found funders whose slot is at or before now. For the backtest, `scripts/funding-backfill.ts` writes `insider-funding.jsonl` with a sha256 manifest in BT-2's shape (mint, knownAtMs, funded, devCluster, plus the reads); `readSupplement` refuses a file whose hash, row count or reads do not check out.
- **Migration `obs.slot`** is the CompletePumpAmmMigrationEvent's own slot, never a later read: GATE-1c's H5 filters the pool's trade tape from it (test pins it).
- **Completeness stamp:** every fact carries `completeness`: `bounded` for a largest-accounts holder list, `unresolved` for a flagged fact or an incomplete insider list, `complete` otherwise.
- **Complete holder set (supervisor rulings, 4f4cdfb and after the second review):** `readHoldersAll` reads the mint at confirmed first (slot A: supply, authority), then one getProgramAccounts with `minContextSlot` A on the mint's own token program with memcmp on the mint at offset 0, refusing an answer older than A (slot B ≥ A) (no dataSize filter: Token-2022 accounts are 170 bytes or more), then the programs of off-curve owners. The producer (`completeHolders`) proves completeness or makes no fact: every account owned by that program, decoded, of this mint, no address twice, mint authority none, the mint read at or before the scan, all raw balances summing to the supply read at A exactly (with no mint authority the supply can only fall, so equality proves no positive balance was left out and no burn happened in between); coverage `all` at slot B, each account carrying its `mint`. Freshness is judged when the fact is used, by the gate. Assumption: both reads are on the same fork (confirmed); a finalized mint anchor would remove it and can be added where cheap. The largest-accounts path (`readHolders`) reads the holder set and the amounts in two calls and reports the older slot: it serves definite rejects only (bounded). A daily scan cap (`holderScansPerDay`, trial default `HOLDER_SCANS_PER_DAY` = 100 by supervisor ruling, about 1,200 Helius credits a day at the published price; revisit once gpa-probe measures) stops scans; at the cap nothing is read and H12/H13 abstain. Filters (supervisor ruling, so the node uses its mint index): the one-shot call (never cursor-paginated V2); legacy SPL `dataSize 165` + mint memcmp; Token-2022 mint memcmp + memcmp(165, AccountType 2). Measured on the recorded coin: 4 of 62 Token-2022 accounts are exactly 165 bytes (no extensions) and drop out under that filter; all four were empty, so the sum still matched. One that holds tokens makes the sum fail and no holder fact form (never a pass; tested). The Token-2022 filter is configuration (`token2022Filter`). **gpa-probe run 37149567929 (2026-10-03, on 1faac04, Helius):** Token-2022 DRnMY… mint only served 60 accounts in 262 ms, indexed 56 in 68 ms (the 4 extension-less accounts drop out); legacy SPL Fartcoin mint only refused (-32600 too many accounts), indexed (dataSize 165 + mint) served 569,389 accounts in 4.3 s; 10 credits a call (published). Alchemy answered 429 (compute units per second) to all four back-to-back calls, so it is unmeasured; the probe now spaces Alchemy calls 1.5 s apart. **FACTS-1c (supervisor ruling):** the Token-2022 default is `mintOnly` (every account, extension-less ones included, so their balances count); legacy SPL keeps dataSize 165 + mint (every legacy token account is exactly 165 bytes, so that set is complete). A mint-only Token-2022 scan refused with the Helius too-many-accounts code (-32600, `GPA_TOO_MANY_ACCOUNTS`) falls back once to `indexed`; every other failure (a 429, a timeout, any other JSON-RPC error such as "minimum context slot not reached", a refused legacy scan or a scan already on `indexed`) propagates with no retry and uses one scan (supervisor ruling on review of bf75952). The fallback runs as a second scan off the daily cap (no retry past the cap); the fallback is counted per day as `mint-only-refused`, and its result stays fail-safe (an extension-less account holding tokens breaks the sum and no fact forms). Every holder set that does not form is counted per UTC day and reason (`facts/abstentions:holders`, e.g. `sum-mismatch`) for the coverage report, so a silent abstention shows; that key never feeds a gate. Each account carries `delegate` and `delegatedAmount` for GATE-1e. The scan slot B is judged against the decision moment by the gate (freshness at use). The public mainnet RPC served it on 2026-10-03 for Token-2022 (DRnMY…, 62 accounts, 92 ms) and legacy SPL (Fartcoin, 569,455 accounts, 6.6 s); Helius and Alchemy availability and credits wait on `scripts/gpa-probe.ts` being run with the keys.
- **Soft values are events.** `gates/soft` carries values fixed once their window is covered (creation-slot buyers, same-transaction dev buy, funding classes), so `soft.ts` reads it as an event: an hour after migration the values are still the ones logged (review finding: as state they went stale two slots after creation).
- **Soft owner classes:** among first buyers, `knownLinkedOwners` (funded by the dev or the dev's funder, or sharing a first funder with another first buyer), `supportedIndependentOwners` (a known funder nobody else shares), `unresolvedOwners` (no point-in-time read). Nothing is called independent without that evidence.
- **H17 inputs:** the pool fact carries `accountBytes` and the pool's `isCashbackCoin` and `coinCreator` from the same account read (mirroring TX-1b #51).
- **Regime volume from the chain (supervisor ruling after external review, 2026-10-03; DefiLlama dropped):** `read:chain-volume-hour` rows (pump curve and canonical PumpSwap buys and sells, quote side in lamports, one per completed UTC hour, `covered` false when any slot was not scanned) are summed by `dailyChainVolume` into complete UTC days only; a day with an uncovered, missing or contradicting hour is absent, so the volume condition is not covered and the regime is off. An hour row received before its hour ends is ignored. `CurveVolumeFact` now holds `volumeLamports`. Backtest: hour rows from DATA-1's hourly census. Live: the same rows from DATA-1c's release assets ("Regime volume window").
- **Regime volume window (FACTS-1d, supervisor ruling 2026-10-03):** the old rule needed 365 full days of volume, but our chain series starts 2026-07-20, so the condition was unknown every day and the regime was off for good, live and in the backtest. That was a defect, not a filter. Now the check on UTC day D reads day L = D−3 and compares it with the nearest-rank 25th percentile of the days from max(2026-07-20, L−364) to L. Fewer than 28 days (`volumeMinDays`), or any day in the window missing or uncovered, is unknown and the regime is off. 365 (`volumeWindowDays`) stays as the cap. Why D−3 (`volumeLagDays`): the archive makes a day complete 0.4 to 1.9 days after it ends (30 epochs measured), plus 2 to 3 h to scan, so D−2 can still be missing; reading D−3 keeps a normal publish delay from turning the regime off. Why 28 days: four whole weeks, so each weekday counts equally, and the 25th percentile rests on at least 7 days. Both new values are locked in `tighten.ts` like the window; `VOLUME_SERIES_START_DAY` is a constant in `config/time.ts`. One function (`volumeCondition`) for live and backtest. Asset format (owned by DATA-1c, PR #77): release `data-volume-DAY` holds `volume-hours-DAY.csv` (header `hour_start_ms,lamports,covered`, then exactly 24 rows in hour order; lamports of SOL-quoted curve and canonical WSOL PumpSwap buys and sells; covered `1` or `0`, an uncovered hour still listed) and `volume-check-DAY.json` (the cross-check, passed only when `mismatches` and `problems` are both empty). One parser, `parseVolumeHoursCsv` in `core/src/facts/volume.ts`, serves the live reader and the backtest (BT-2); anything off the format refuses the whole day. Live source (supervisor rulings on review of #78, 2026-10-04): `FactReaders.readChainVolume` lists releases through the GitHub API (`releases?per_page=100`, paged, 1 or 2 calls a pass) and checks each `data-volume-DAY` release object: the exact tag, published by GitHub Actions (`github-actions[bot]`, id 41898282), not a draft and a prerelease (as `publish-volume.sh` makes it), and exactly one `volume-hours-DAY.csv` and one `volume-check-DAY.json`, each fully uploaded by GitHub Actions, with a `sha256:` digest and a `browser_download_url` that is exactly this repository's download path. A forged series could turn the regime on, so anything else leaves the day unknown. Both assets are downloaded from github.com (outside the REST quota) and each must match its digest; the check must be for that day, cover 24 hours, and have no mismatches or problems; the CSV's 24 rows are then ingested as `read:chain-volume-hour` (an uncovered hour as `covered: false`, so its day is unknown), recorded like every other read. A day not published or refused ingests nothing (unknown, never zero) and is tried again at most once an hour. Budget: `GITHUB_RELEASES` (REST, 60 an hour without a token) allows 50 an hour and the reader spends 1 or 2 a pass; `GITHUB_DOWNLOADS` allows one download per 2 s. A rate limit (403 or 429, or a request the scheduler refuses) ends the pass. Persistence: each verified day (tag, asset ids, sha256 and both texts) is kept as `chain-volume/data-volume-DAY.json` in the worker's state dir (public market data; shape approved by the supervisor). On start every kept day is checked again (text against its sha256, the cross-check, the CSV) and ingested, so a restart downloads only new days; a record that fails is ignored and its day downloaded again. A kept day whose listed asset ids or digests later differ is tampered: releases are never edited, so this is not a publish delay. Its hours are ingested again as uncovered (the producer drops the day), the record is marked, a `worker:chain-volume-alert` fact is put on the feed, and the day is never used again, after a restart too. Producer cost: `ChainVolumeDays` keeps the daily sums one row at a time (each row touches only its own day, older hours are dropped once per boundary day) and the curve-volume fact is released only when the complete days change. Loading a 365-day window after a restart went from about 9 s (every kept hour re-summed on each row) to about 0.3 s. The live fact source (`LiveFacts`, FACTS-1b) calls it at start and at each new hour, next to SOL/USD; `main.ts` wires the GitHub scheduler.
- **Evaluation staging (supervisor ruling):** stream-derived hard rejects first; RPC reads (accounts, holders top 20, cross-checks, mint history and funders) only for candidates that survive them; re-read only when the as-of state changed or freshness expired, never on a fixed loop; the complete holder scan last. When the budget is spent the result is not evaluated, never a pass. The backtest follows the same order.
- **Execution health** is never green without owner-set limits (`ExecHealthLimits`): live risk limits are the owner's.
- **Live-only vetoes:** sim (H15), xcheck (H16), exec-health (regime); the gates already skip them in backtest mode. **Live reads with a historical source still to build:** mint, pool, LP (BT-2 must release DATA-1's mint and pool state and vault balances as `read:accounts` answers) and holders (rebuild from token balances; until then H12/H13 have no backtest input).
- **FACTS-1b: the worker's facts come from core's producer (2026-10-04).** The engine reads the live Feed through `FactFeed` (`packages/worker/src/run/engine-feed.ts`), and a replay of the recording uses the same helper, so facts are derived from recorded inputs and never recorded on their own (test: a raw account read on the live Feed gives the pool fact live, and the recording replays to the same facts and count). The FactSource hook gains `ingest` (the live Feed, for raw `offchain` reads), `candidates()` (each candidate's migration time and the typed reasons of its last evaluation, inputs included; the journal's `gate_reasons` keeps its shape) and `tip()`. `LiveFacts` (`packages/worker/src/facts/source.ts`) stages reads on those reasons: nothing before the first evaluation or after a pass; nothing while any reason is not H16 evidence (missing, malformed, stale, degraded, gap, not-covered, inconsistent), so the regime, a stream-built reject or a disagreement spends no read; otherwise only the named inputs (mint, pool, LP → one account read; xcheck → the three cross-checks; insiders → the mint history as of the feed tip, once it succeeds; holders → the largest accounts, and the complete scan only when the holder set is the last input missing; the worker's `no-market` → the account read). Each kind is read at most once a minute per mint (the budget's one evaluation a minute) and never twice at once. Outside the reasons: hourly SOL/USD bars (the producer's kept window at start, then 3 hours) and one pool read 5 s after each shortlisted graduate's survival mark. Every read is counted per UTC day by kind and outcome under `worker:fact-reads` (coverage report only; no gate reads it). Mint-history caps are configuration (`MINT_HISTORY_CAPS`: 20 signature pages, 3 pages and 10 transactions a funder). Not wired, by design: H15's simulation (SIM-1), execution health (needs the owner's limits) and regime volume (no free live source, so the regime stays off and, by the staging rule, no candidate read is made live until it has one). **Re-evaluation on a landed read (supervisor ruling, 2026-10-04):** facts must be at most 2 slots (chain) or 2 s (third party) old at the decision and the strategy evaluates every 2 s, so a read landing between two evaluations could age out before the next. A raw read (`read:<kind>:<mint>`) released for a candidate marks it, and the next event (the facts that read made, released right after it at its moment) evaluates it once outside the cadence; only that candidate, once per read, and only while the read is still fresh by evidence.ts's rules, judged when the mark is used: a chain read at most the state lag behind (exactly the lag marks, one slot more does not), an off-chain read with no slot (RugCheck, GoPlus, Jupiter) at most the quote age old (a quiet feed can deliver the next event later than that; it matters when the cadence is set slower than the quote age). FACTS-1e review: at the read's own event its age is always 0, so the check is made at use, not at the mark; a worker-harness test shows the decision itself changes (the coin is entered at once with the read, at the next cadence evaluation without it). The read's own recorded frame is the trigger, so the replay re-evaluates at the same position (test: event-by-event evaluation trace equal live and replay). The live Feed releases a slot two slots past the tip (about 0.8 s) or after 2 s without tip progress, so a read reaches the engine about 1 to 2 s after it arrives. **Regime staging stays (supervisor ruling):** reads for a regime-blocked candidate would be wasted credits; a live volume source (FACTS-1d) turns the regime on and the reads follow (test).

- **FACTS-1f: the live worker evaluates the hard rejects in BT-2's stages (supervisor ruling, 2026-10-04).** G3 compares the reject mix of live and the backtest, so both must name the same reasons for the same facts. `stagedHardRejects` (`engine/strategy.ts`) evaluates GATE-2's stages in BT-2's three groups, stage 1 (stream-derived), then stage 2 (account reads, cross-checks), then stages 3 and 4 together (holder scan and funders, simulation), each with every gate evaluated (`stopAtFirst: false`, as BT-2's calibration log), and stops at the first group with a reject; the gates after it are named in the reject as `not evaluated: H…` (BT-2's words) and are never passed or failed. Before, live stopped at the first failing gate of a single full evaluation, so its reasons were a subset of the backtest's. Each group's reasons equal BT-2's call for that stage on the same facts (test), and a recorded session replays to the same typed reasons at every reject (test). A side effect on FACTS-1b's staging: a stage-1 reject now hides stage-2 evidence, so no account read is made for a candidate until its stream-built gates pass, which is the staging rule. The entry path requires GATE-2's rule, `hardAllowsEntry`: `complete` (every gate of core's `HARD_GATES` evaluated, not only the groups') and no reasons; a pass that left a gate out is logged as `hard rejects incomplete`, never an entry (test).
## H15 round-trip simulation (SIM-1, `packages/worker/src/sim`)

- **2026-10-05 · H15 compares the simulation with a model of the same sequence (AUDIT-RM4 F2).** The simulation buys and then sells the bought tokens in one transaction, so the sell meets the reserves the buy left. H15 compared that loss with `paid − proceeds`, where PumpSwap's `proceeds` prices the planned exit on the reserves before entry and so pays both price impacts. The model's allowance was therefore larger than the simulated sequence could ever lose, by `entryImpact + exitImpact`, and a charge the model does not know of, up to that size, passed unseen: about 3 bps at $2, and percent-scale at $500 on the passing pool (it grows with size; "costs stay exact at any size"). `RoundTrip` now also carries `immediateProceeds`: the same tokens sold at once on the buy's `after` pool (v2 fee offsets included), the simulated sequence. H15 compares `sim paid − sim proceeds` with `paid − immediateProceeds + 16` (the rounding bound, unchanged). The pump curve already priced its sell on the curve the buy left, so there both figures are the same. `proceeds` keeps its meaning (the planned exit for sizing, the cost gate and the stop) and the simulation's own build is unchanged. A quote whose immediate sell cannot be priced is no quote (fail closed). The change only tightens: an honest simulation of the sequence passes to the lamport, as before. Evidence: `packages/core/test/gates/h15-sequence.test.ts`. At $2 and at $500, a simulation short by exactly the gap to the planned exit passes on the base code and is now `sim-loss`. The 16-lamport bound holds exactly. The passing worlds' simulations (core world, worker harness, WORKER-1e) now report the sequence's honest proceeds.
- **2026-10-04 · One unsigned transaction: the buy at the entry size, then a sell of exactly the quoted tokens,** built with the TX-1 builders for a funded stand-in (TEST-2's approach) after TX-1b's `checkShape`. It is simulated as TEST-2 does: `sigVerify: false`, `replaceRecentBlockhash: true`, `minContextSlot` at the feed head. It is never signed or sent; the no-send import-graph test covers this module too.
  - The sell keeps its token account open. If the buy got more tokens than quoted, the leftover stays and counts as loss; if it got fewer, the sell fails. Both are conservative.
  - The tip transfer is left out of the round trip. It does not touch the venue, so it cannot change the loss. Without it (and its jitodontfront marker), a PumpSwap round trip fits one packet with no lookup table: 1,241 bytes with it, over the 1,232 limit.
- **2026-10-04 · The loss is measured, not modelled.**
  - Loss = −(change in the stand-in's wallet, wSOL and base-account lamports) − the `fee` the node reports − the rent paid into other accounts. The balances are the node's own `preBalances`/`postBalances`, taken inside the simulation.
  - Rent paid is counted narrowly (review of PR #59). A charge must not be booked as rent and drop out of the loss:
    - a new account counts at most the rent-exempt minimum for its size; for wSOL, the rent reserve, capped the same way;
    - the creator vault counts what it gained beyond the creator fees in the stand-in's own TradeEvents (buy and sell), at most its shortfall to rent-exempt;
    - curve growth counts only against a measured length. When the curve is longer after the simulation than at the decision, it is read again (1 credit). A curve that grew in between makes the counted growth smaller, so the loss errs high. The decision-time length is never used to measure.
  - `paid` is the buy leg's own figure: PumpSwap `BuyEvent.userQuoteAmountIn`, or pump `TradeEvent` solAmount + fee + creatorFee. Both are the model's terms, verified by the golden vectors. `proceeds = paid − loss`, so a charge the model misses lowers proceeds and H15 rejects on it.
- **2026-10-04 · Fail-safe outcomes. No fact (H15 rejects the missing evidence):**
  - a payer error (`InsufficientFundsForFee`, `AccountNotFound`, `InsufficientFundsForRent` on account 0). This is the stand-in's funding, not the coin, so it gives "stand-in-unfunded" and drops the funding cache. It never becomes an H15 veto or enters G3's veto-bias count (review of PR #59);
  - unsupported shape;
  - a refused build;
  - an unfunded stand-in;
  - a scheduler refusal ("not evaluated");
  - a timeout, HTTP or RPC error;
  - a malformed answer.
- **2026-10-04 · Fail-safe outcomes. A failed fact (H15 rejects `sim-failed`):**
  - a program error or a stale blockhash;
  - no `fee`;
  - balances missing, or disagreeing with the read-back;
  - no buy event, or a truncated log;
  - paid above the spend;
  - a loss above paid.

  Nothing short of a clean, fully measured simulation passes.
- **2026-10-04 · `checkShape` runs on the buy only.** The sell is built by the same builders for the same market and mint, so it has the same shape (review of PR #59).
- **2026-10-04 · Quota and cost.** Calls run at the shortlisted tier (P2; P0 and P1 are refused before any request).
  - A simulation costs 1 Helius credit. A funding check of the stand-in costs 1 more, at most once per `standInCheckMs`. Both are recorded per simulation, with the latency.
  - FACTS-1's budget counts 1 credit per evaluation for the simulation. The funding check adds 1 per check interval: at 60 s, about one more credit a minute in total, however many candidates there are.
- **2026-10-04 · H15 stays a live-only veto** (the backtest applies the exact local round trip, §16.3). Every simulation returns a `SimRecord` (outcome, reason, credits, latency, measured and modelled loss). WORKER-1 journals it for each shortlisted candidate, next to the H15 reason the gate gives. G3's veto-bias measure (STATS-1b) reads those reasons.

## Backtest review fixes (BT-1c)

- **2026-10-03 · Fill and delay assumptions are harsh and documented (supervisor ruling after external review).** Until measurements justify a change, the fill model keeps harsh values, ordered conservative ≥ base ≥ optimistic (a test enforces the order for every field), with correlated failures and long delays, especially on exits. Reports say "provisional values". The dry run measures feed delay, quote age and simulation behaviour, but it sends nothing, so landing delay and success stay as set through it. They are refined only from observed-chain evidence (landing times of comparable transactions in DATA-1) and later the owner-authorised canary. Each assumption's source and status is in the table at the top of `config/fills.ts`. Promotion reads conservative only.
- **2026-10-03 · Observation delay in three parts.** Event → processed, processed → confirmed (charged because the research config's decision path waits for confirmed), provider → worker. Profiles: `measured` (today's base values, status unmeasured until the worker recorder), `adverse` 2 + 6 slots + 1 s (the conservative scenario), `stress` 4 + 12 slots + 2 s with a 30 s and a 60 s blackout per UTC day. An observation is released at the end of the first block at or after its due slot, at that block's real time; the backlog follows a blackout in chain order; each value carries its chain moment so it never looks fresh; recorded receipt times are never delayed again. Discoveries keep their own drawn lag and go through the same queue, so a blackout holds them too and they arrive in chain order when it ends.
- **2026-10-03 · Exit failures.** Congestion is one network state shared by every open position and every provider: a two-state chain over 150-slot windows. Market activity (all pools' real volume in the previous, complete window, causal) may raise its entry probability up to a cap, but never defines congestion on its own (supervisor correction). With no activity the base state is congested about 9% of windows, not below the earlier 8% burst share. Provider failures per window and deterministic 10/30/60 s bursts drop attempts on top; a dropped attempt is replaced only after its last valid height. Reports give the blocked-exit rate when a whole ladder fell inside congestion (for y_severe) and, with `--burst-sweep`, expectancy and survival against burst frequency.
- **2026-10-03 · Rent follows the account-close outcome.** The final sell of a token account is an atomic sell-and-close. A failed close fails the transaction (sell rolled back, fee charged) and leaves the account sell-only; dust or an unsolicited token (per account) keeps it open. Rent comes back once, only for an entry whose account a landed sell closed. The no-recovery line stays as a reported sensitivity line (economics' all-in result with no rent back), never the promotion score (RENT-1 ruling, 2026-10-04: line 108's consensus text governs). `closeSuccessPpm` is an assumption with no refinement source yet (TEST-2's simulations omit the close in some cases and test mechanics, not landing). The close is drawn from a seed per attempt, so scenarios keep common random numbers.
- **2026-10-03 · Drops keep the existing direction.** A dropped attempt costs no fee, so the scenario test keeps conservative ≤ base ≤ optimistic for the drop share (0.2, 0.3, 0.4 of misses). Drops still hurt exits through expiry delay, which the tail, congestion, provider failures and bursts model.
- **2026-10-03 · Liquidity decay on repeated exits is a proceeds haircut.** Each earlier exit attempt on a position takes a share off the next fill's proceeds (counted as slippage) while the pool sees the same sell. A proxy for sellers ahead of us until exit data exist.
- **2026-10-03 · All-in expectancy is per entry decision.** Failed entries cost fees without a trade, so the all-in figure divides by entry decisions (filled or not) as well as by filled trades; the report's mean net stays per filled trade.
- **2026-10-03 · Operating costs at other bankrolls.** Hosting (US$6/month) and the break-even net per trade are also shown at US$20, 100 and 200 (research config), with the policy's sizes kept at their share of the bankroll and this run's trade count. A projection only; the policy's limits stay what the owner set.
- **2026-10-03 · AUD is not reported yet.** The dataset has hourly SOL/USD only; AUD needs an AUD/USD series (a DATA follow-up). Cash flows are USD at each flow's time.
- **2026-10-03 · A holdout's code id includes uncommitted and untracked code.** The CLI records the commit plus a hash of any uncommitted diff and of every untracked file under packages/, so a registered configuration cannot be run on different code under the same id.
- **2026-10-03 · Holdout cannot be bypassed (review H1–H3, D1–D3, supervisor rulings).** Research runs read practice days only: never a day at or after the research config's reserved start (2026-09-22, UTC data day) nor one inside a registered window; with no `--days` they keep practice days. A window overlapping one already started cannot be registered or run under any other id or universe, and a universe the strategy does not produce is refused. The registry lives on the remote `holdout-registry` branch so it survives fresh clones, with a local copy at a path fixed in the research config inside the code's own repository (never from the working directory; a symlink is refused). Every command fetches that branch first and refuses a local copy that differs (a fresh clone takes the remote's); every write (registered, started, refused, failed, sealed) is committed onto it and pushed with a plain push before the command goes on, so a failed push or a race means no run.
