- **2026-10-04 · Attempts end, α is pinned (BT-1d R3, R4).** `endAttempt` ends an attempt after its tail when it was not opened, only for a reason the store proves for each of its holdouts not yet burned or opened: `g1-failed` (its latest G1 is not a pass for the registered configuration), `short` (not ready for the required trades and days, passed in) or `never-run` (still registered); any other reason, or a ready attempt with a G1 pass, is refused, so a mandatory opening is never skipped (review E1). Those holdouts are burned `spent` and the next attempt can register; before the tail it is refused. When STATS-1c (#62) moves α into the core registry, this eligibility rule moves there with it. The plan's α must be the ruled budget (attempt 1 at 0.04, attempt k ≥ 2 at 0.01 / 2^(k-1)); any other plan is refused. G2 uses the attempt's α as its family α (Holm across the attempt's universes).
- **2026-10-04 · One holdout registry (BT-1d, supervisor ruling).** The registry on the `holdout-registry` branch has typed sections: `plan` (window, entry cutoff and tail end, which must equal the research config's; family size; tie salt; α schedule, attempt 1 at 0.04 and attempt k ≥ 2 at 0.01 / 2^(k-1); decoder boundaries; procedure; other fixed details), `windows` (STATS-1's registry), `attempts` (index, α, holdouts and config ids, registered, started and ended times), `g1` (appended results) and `runs`. BT-2 writes through this API instead of its own file. The plan is fixed once set. Registering an attempt commits it; a skipped or repeated index is refused and burns the holdouts it names (and, when repeated, the earlier attempt's). A run must feed every holdout of its attempt. `openSealedHoldout` refuses unless the latest G1 record is a pass for the registered configuration. Every change is one pushed commit; a call that changes nothing writes nothing. Attempt k ≥ 2 is registered only after every earlier holdout is scored or burned; its window starts exactly on the first whole UTC day after registration (STATS-1c core rule; a requested start on any other day is refused), has 28 entry days and the plan's tail length, is written into the attempt at registration, and may not overlap any earlier attempt.
- **2026-10-04 · One holder rebuild (BT-1d, agreed with BT-2).** `dataset/holders.ts` (`HolderBook`) is the only holder rebuild; BT-2 consumes it. It keeps every token account of a mint (owner, amount, delegate, delegated amount, GATE-1e's `HolderAccount` shape), fed in chain order (refused otherwise), and answers as of the last input. A swap credits or debits its `user_token_owner`'s `user_token_account`, never `user` or the signer; a boost buy-and-burn credits nobody; a movement moves between accounts, and a row with an empty owner is left out whole. The mint's holders read `unresolved` (holder facts abstain) from: an empty swap owner, a coverage `unresolved` note once the replay reaches it, activity during the lead-in without movements, a mint searched only in its pump transactions, an owner change or mismatch, or a balance below zero at the end of a transaction. Delegates come from raw account operations through `applyAccountOps`; the handling is stricter than a balance-only rebuild: an account owner change marks the mint unresolved rather than moving the balance silently, and BT-2 keeps holder reads `partial` until DATA-1 keeps every approval-changing transaction. The loader accepts schema 3 only.
- **2026-10-04 · Holder rebuild gives live's account set (BT-1d R1, supervisor ruling).** `holdersAsOf` returns the supply and every account with a balance, the venue accounts included, so the gate sees what live's complete holder read sees. The curve's token account is the curve's real token reserves after each trade plus the tokens it holds back for migration (total supply minus the initial real reserves, from the create), owner the bonding-curve PDA; a migration moves `mint_amount` out of it. A pool's base vault (its associated token account) holds the pool's base reserve after each swap, or its base amount at creation, owner the pool. Supply is the create's total minus burns (boost buy-and-burn included) plus mints. It reads unresolved when no create was seen or the balances do not sum to the supply, mirroring live's `checkHolders`.
- **2026-10-04 · Owner programs from a supplement (BT-1d R2, supervisor ruling).** GATE-1e needs each owner's program to tell a locker from an unknown program. Off-curve owners' programs come from a hash-checked supplement, fetched once with getMultipleAccounts (`scripts/owner-programs.ts`; manifest with its sha256, rows, calls and accounts; the RPC URL is never written). As-of risk: a PDA's owning program is fixed when the account is created and practically never changes, so a later read stands for the decision time. An off-curve owner missing from the supplement leaves its mint unresolved.
- **2026-10-04 · Owner-program supplement workflow (BT-1e).** `.github/workflows/owner-programs.yml`, manual dispatch only, dry run by default. Input: dataset releases `data-FROM-TO`. A `data-day-*` release holds scanner units, not rows, and the assembled release is built only from day releases. Each must be published by github-actions[bot] and match its SHA256SUMS; releases are read one at a time and deleted before the next. The fetch job has contents: read, and HELIUS_API_KEY reaches only its fetch step, as env. The script scrubs the URL, the key and each URL part that can carry a key from every message, and the supplement manifest records the datasets it covers (tag and manifest sha256). The publish job has contents: write, never sees the key, and publishes release `owner-programs-DAY` (fetch day, UTC) under env -i. An existing release is never edited. Builder decision: the input is assembled dataset releases, not raw day releases, because only those hold owner-bearing rows.
- **2026-10-04 · Owner-program supplement: pool creators and the download (BT-1f).** `owners` now also lists the off-curve `creator` of each CreatePoolEvent, which the holder book credits with the base it pays in. It skips pump's pool authority, the same exception the holder book makes. Consumers fetch a release with `packages/backtest/ci/fetch-owner-programs.sh TAG DIR`, using read-only REST calls. The release must be by github-actions[bot], not a draft, a prerelease, and hold exactly the three files with sha256 digests. Each file must match its asset digest, SHA256SUMS must list exactly the two data files and verify, and the manifest must match the JSON lines and the tag's day. DIR appears only when all of this passes, and readOwnerPrograms then reads it.
- **2026-10-03 · Holdout final form.** The window is [2026-09-22, 2026-10-21) in UTC data days: entries only before the 2026-10-20 cutoff, then a one-day tail (it covers the longest hold any universe may have, plus S0's blocked retries and margin; a test checks it) so every position can finish. `holdout-register` records the window, the cutoff and the tail end before any count is known. One sealed ledger serves every registered universe. Opening (once, after the tail, only with a recorded G1 pass) belongs to the scoring stage; the backtester has no open path.
- **2026-10-04 · Pre-funding evidence, items 1 and 2 (BT-3).** `scripts/evidence.ts` replays every assembled window of a range 10 times through the engine; a window passes only with identical decision-log hashes, no crash, illegal state or unreconciled intent, the mirror book equal to the engine's and LEDGER-REPLAY passing on its ledger (each term pinned by its own test). Gate mode needs the window's 14 lead-in days and every file the replay reads (the manifest and each day file loadDay opens) listed in and matching the release's SHA256SUMS. The labelled no-lead-in mode is a determinism check only: it writes `gate: false` to a folder ending `-no-lead-in`. Any reader that counts BT-3 output as gate evidence must require `gate === true && mode === 'gate'` (and `pass === true`); nothing else qualifies.
- **2026-10-04 · RENT-1: the scored scenario refunds rent per the modelled close outcome (supervisor ruling).** Conservative, the promotion scenario, counted every token account's rent as lost (`rentRecovery: false`), about 9% of a $2 trade: a flat trade scored about −10.9% instead of about −2.6%. Line 108's consensus text governs: the rent comes back exactly when the final sell-and-close lands. In the backtest that is the fill model's per-attempt draw (`closeSuccessPpm`, dust making the account sell-only, sell-only fallback, partials; common random numbers); in the research outcome stage it is one draw per candidate (dust at entry, then the close), with a failed close costing one failed attempt. The "no rent recovery" line stays as a reported sensitivity (economics `allInNoRentRecoveryMicro`), never the score; worst-case reservations keep full rent; no statistical threshold changes. The `rentRecovery` flag decided nothing after this and was removed (`fills-3`).

## Trade-stream gap fills (FILL-2, `packages/worker/src/seed/fill.ts`, `WatchOptions.fill`)

- **2026-10-04 · Why.** H5 reads a pool's trade events since migration, and H11 reads its candles. One gap in `coverage:trades:<pool>` (a restart, a feed drop) rejects that coin for life. Without a fill, every restart drill would permanently reject every pool alive across it, and live would diverge from the backtest, which has every pool row.
- **2026-10-04 · How.** `fillTradeGaps({ rpc, timers, provider, creditCap, gaps, asOf })` takes one `TradeGap` per gap: pool, stream, kind (`position` | `candidate`), `fromSlot`/`fromMs`, `untilSlot` (the live watch's first slot after the gap), the open gap to close (`via`, `fromSlot`), and after a restart `liveStart`. For each gap it calls `getSignaturesForAddress` on the pool over `fromSlot`..`untilSlot − 1` (live covers `untilSlot` onward), then `getTransaction` at `confirmed`. It uses SEED-1's backfill (`backfillAddress`) with the same paging, `minContextSlot`, retry, history-end and as-of rules. Transactions are decoded only through FEED-1's `eventsOfFrame` for a chain-placed `tx` frame, so a filled event has the id, key, position and decoded value the stream gives (tested against the stream path on real transactions). Ranks within a slot follow block order, oldest first.
- **2026-10-04 · After a restart.** The fill returns the events with their chain moments in compareEvents order, to release ahead of the buffered live events. It also returns the close of the saved open gap, dated from the restarted watch's start fact: same slot and position, at `min(fromMs, liveStart.receivedAt − 1)`. That is SEED-1's liveStart rule, tested with the start at `untilSlot`, below it, just after the saved state, and with a clock-skewed receipt time. A complete fill closes with a `resume`, anything less with a bounded lossy gap. The fill reads from the older of `fromSlot` and `close.fromSlot`, so the close never restores a slot it did not read. It is empty and complete (no RPC call) only when that start is after `untilSlot − 1`. `close.at` (when the saved gap was reported) moves the close to just after a saved gap reported at or after the restarted start. Without it, such a gap stays open (fail safe). Both rules are SEED-1's, carried over and tested here. A gap whose `liveStart` or `close.at` is after `asOf` is refused before any call (SEED-1's as-of guard). A close that would still land after `asOf` is not made, so the gap stays open. Events are bounded by `asOf` (slot and receipt time), so nothing after the process start is released. An event held back makes the fill partial. WORKER-1 drops events whose id it already holds from the saved history: ids are deterministic and equal the stream's. Events of other pools in the same transactions come too, as `eventsOfFrame` gives them, without coverage facts of their own. That is harmless under the gap rules: a reader judges a pool only by that pool's own coverage facts, so an extra event can only add to what is known, never close a gap.
- **2026-10-04 · In a run.** FEED-1's socket watch takes an optional `fill` hook. Once a reconnect gap is ready to close (resubscribed, page backfill done, a live slot seen), it asks `fill({ address, fromSlot, toSlot })` for exactly that range and keeps the gap open meanwhile. True closes it with a `resume` (even if the single page alone looked lossy). False or a throw closes it as a bounded lossy gap. A drop while the fill runs discards its answer, and the next connection asks again. `ingestingFill` is the hook body WORKER-1 wires: it fills `fromSlot`..`toSlot` and ingests each transaction into the live feed as a backfilled lookup `tx` frame. The feed places what is already released after it and drops duplicates by signature. An unknown gap start answers false. Without the hook, FEED-1 behaves as before.
- **2026-10-04 · Fail safe and budget.** Until a fill completes, the gap stands and H5/H11 reject. A partial, skipped or failed fill is lossy, never covered. One credit cap per restart (config, passed by WORKER-1) is shared: open positions first at P2, then candidates at P3, oldest gap first. Positions fill at P2, below live open-position monitoring at P1, because exits never wait (supervisor ruling on the #70 review). A test shows a P1 call admitted at once while a long position fill holds its P2 share. Each gap reads at most `maxPagesPerFill` signature pages (default 10, i.e. 10,000 signatures); a busier gap stops as `page-cap`, partial, and stays a gap. A gap reached after the cap is spent is skipped and closed as lossy; a cap that runs out inside a gap stops it there (partial). Each fill reports credits, calls, retries, latency, futures dropped and why it stopped.
- **2026-10-04 · Evidence.** Real PumpSwap transactions from DEC-1's fixtures. Tests: a complete fill makes coverage continuous, a partial fill stays a gap, filled events equal the stream's, nothing is dated after the process start, over budget leaves the gap, the empty fill, and the in-run hook (complete, false, throw, a drop mid-fill, `ingestingFill` into a live feed). 13 of 14 mutations fail the tests. The close's 1 ms margin is tested with the feed's real start id (`coverage:<stream>:start#<seq>`, which sorts before the fill's ids on a tie), here and in SEED-1's tests, which had used an id that hid it (#70 review). The survivor is the hook's "asked once" guard, unreachable today: the socket checks a gap's close once per connection. It is kept as a guard against a double fill.

## Fault injection (TEST-3, `packages/worker/test/fault-injection.test.ts`)

- **Scripted faults on the real worker.** Each §18 case runs on the worker harness with the virtual clock. Waits are "until the effect, within a virtual-time bound", never a fixed wall-clock window. The only seam added to production code is `WorkerDeps.worldFault`. Every paper-world answer passes through it: null loses the answer, and another event replaces it. Tests set it; `main.ts` never does.
- **Cases and their guards.** Mutation evidence: each guard was removed in turn, and the case fails without it.
  - Timeout after a send. The send times out and the landing notice is lost, so the fill is found by the status read after expiry. It is booked once, with no second buy. Guards: `send_timeout` leads to unknown plus a status read (`lifecycle/intent.ts`), and so does a tick past the last valid height.
  - 429s. The first burst stays inside the window above the exit floor. After a 429 the provider gets nothing for one window. An exit-class read then goes before the queued reads, a refused read makes no fact, and the open position still exits on its stop. Guards: `penalize()` on a 429 and the scheduler floors. Class order is masked by the floor here; `scheduler.test.ts` covers it.
    - Entries under rate limits (supervisor ruling on the #83 review). A rate limit stops entries through the gates failing closed on the fact it withholds. It is not a global halt, so one limited provider never stops entries that do not need it. Halts stay for critical feeds, the owner pause, the seed and a ledger divergence.
    - Test: every read answers 429, and a candidate passes every gate but the third-party cross-check, which only a read can bring. Its reads are refused (counted failed, no fact), and each reject from then on is "H16 missing no xcheck". It is never entered, and no halt reason is raised. If the gate is made to pass on a missing cross-check, the candidate enters and the test fails.
  - Kill mid-trade, with the stop's exit in flight. The restart settles the old attempt unfilled before any entry. The stop then exits on the first slot, in full, once. Guards: a paper attempt in flight at a restart is lost, and the `#sendExit` fix below.
  - A restart with a signed, unsent transaction. The ledger holds the sign but not the submit, and no paper attempt exists. The intent becomes unknown, is never sent, settles as expired with no fill, and is released before any entry. Guards: restart turns `signed` into unknown with no rebroadcast, and an unseen signature is reported past its last valid height.
    - The reconcile's `status === 'signed'` skip is not reachable here. The restart event always turns `signed` into unknown before the cancel pass, so removing the skip changes nothing (an equivalent mutant).
  - Feed gap. A critical feed goes silent past `staleFeedMs`, while the chain feed keeps its slots. No entry is proposed while it is out, exits stay able, and the candidate enters only after it is back. When the chain feed goes silent, `exit_capable` turns false. Guards: the stale clause in `#checkHalt`, the strategy failing closed on the halt fact, and the freshness check in `#exitCapable`.
- **Found and fixed: an exit before the first slot was booked blocked.** A restart's reconcile runs before the feeds start, so it has no slot height yet. `#sendExit` booked such an exit blocked ("no slot height yet"), so the stop waited out `blockedRetryMs` (60 s) and went as a single last-rung retry. The owner now waits for the first slot, said once in the log. The kill-mid-trade case fails without the fix (exit not sent within 1 s of the first slot).
- **Found, not fixed here (EXIT-1 follow-up).** An exit decided during the start reconcile without a fresh quote is booked blocked by `exitBookEvents` ("no executable quote") and waits 60 s. Examples: the time stop after downtime longer than T_max, or the deployer trigger. That is timing, not a dead pool, the same reasoning as EXIT-1b's `pendingFull`.
- **Stale feed with an open position (§18 case 1).** Built by WATCH-1; see "Position watch (WATCH-1)".
- **Ordering note for SIGN-1.** In the paper worker the engine runs effects (the paper broadcast) inside `drain`, before the desk writes the ledger. Live signing must store the signed attempt before the first broadcast, as the `attempt` table says.

## Position watch (WATCH-1, `packages/worker/src/run/watch.ts`, `run/snapshot.ts`)

- **2026-10-04 · What.** An independent timer (`ZEROED_WATCH_EVERY_MS`, default 1 s) looks at every open position. It runs on the worker's timers, outside the engine step, and needs no feed event.
  - When the market the position is priced at is older than `ZEROED_WATCH_STALE_MS` (default 3 s), it reads a coherent snapshot through the second path.
  - The snapshot is the pool, both vaults, the mint, PumpSwap's GlobalConfig and its pump-fees FeeConfig, read in one `getMultipleAccounts` at confirmed: one bank, one context slot.
  - The read goes through Alchemy over HTTP (not the Helius socket the feed runs on), through Alchemy's quota scheduler at P1, above fills and seeds. It costs 20 CU (Alchemy's compute-unit page, checked 2026-10-04).
  - The vault addresses are learned from one read of the pool account and kept. A snapshot that fails the vault or mint checks drops them, so the next read learns them again.
- **The snapshot is the whole market.** `decodeSnapshot` builds the pool state and the full fee context from that read alone:
  - the FeeConfig tiers;
  - canonical, from pump's pool-authority PDA;
  - the SOL quote;
  - the live mint supply for the tier's market cap;
  - the coin creator and the pool's own creator fee;
  - mayhem mode;
  - active Token-2022 transfer fee or hook;
  - GlobalConfig's buyback share.
  It goes on the feed as `worker:snapshot:<mint>` (recorded, so a replay makes the same decisions). The strategy's `#market` and the paper world's market take it whole when it is newer than the pool fact. Its reserves are never mixed with the feed's fee terms.
  - Evidence: on a real mainnet read (`fixtures/watch-snapshot.json`), the pool's next swap, quoted from the snapshot alone, matches the chain to the lamport: LP, protocol, creator and buyback fees, and the amount paid. Edited real accounts prove each field's source.
- **Failure is never "fine".** A read that fails, or that cannot be trusted, raises the critical alert once per episode:
  - a journal `alert` line;
  - Health `critical`, which the heartbeat carries;
  - the watchdog's `worker_critical` alert to the owner.
  The position is read again every period, and nothing is sent without a price. The alert clears on the first good snapshot.
- **Acceptance (§18 case 1), on the real worker and virtual time.** The feed dies with a position open and the pool falls 40%.
  - The watch reads the snapshot.
  - The stop exit is submitted within the stale limit + one period + the feed's stale release + a step (6.5 s), its quote equal to `poolSell` on the snapshot.
  - The price stays fresh for the 5 minutes.
  - The position closes when the feed returns.
  - A failing second path alerts once, retries every period, sends nothing, then clears and exits when the path recovers.
  - Mutation evidence: 19 of 19 mutants fail the tests (7 on the watch and the strategy, 12 on the snapshot decoder).
- **Limits.** A paper exit lands only on new slots, so with the slot feed dead it is submitted but lands when the feed returns. Exits are not halted by the feed state, but `exit_capable` still reports the chain feed.
- **Found while building: live positions had no fresh pool state.** After the entry nothing refreshed a held position's pool fact (only candidate reads and the survival read make one). Until POS-1 prices held positions from their swap events, the watch is their only live price source: one 20 CU read per open position every stale period. With positions open around the clock that is about 0.58 M CU a day, against Alchemy's 30 M a month.
- **2026-10-04 · Review of #87 (risk reviewer): no gap between snapshots.**
  - Exits treat a market older than the policy's `maxQuoteAgeMs` (2 s) as no quote. The watch is now tied to it: the oldest a watched market can be when an exit is judged is staleMs + everyMs + latencyMs + the feed's release (2 slots × 400 ms, while slots arrive).
  - `watchTimingProblem` refuses any setting where that sum reaches the quote age. The entry exits 2, and the Worker refuses to build.
  - New defaults: period 200 ms, stale 500 ms, latency 400 ms (`ZEROED_WATCH_LATENCY_MS`). An answer later than the latency is not used: it raises the alert and is read again.
  - Found by the new entry test: `boot/environment.ts`'s allowlist had not passed the three settings at all. Fixed.
  - Test: slots arrive, no pool fact does, and every read answers 300 ms late. Over 20 s the position's market age stays below 2 s at every 100 ms step, and the market moves with each snapshot.
- **Paper fills and fees.**
  - A paper exit that lands after a snapshot is filled on the snapshot's reserves and fee context. The test computes the expected fill from its own decode of the read, not through the worker.
  - The 40%-fall case asserts that the feed's own fee terms would have priced the exit differently.
- **Newest by slot.** A snapshot is newer than the pool fact by slot when both carry one; the receipt time only breaks a tie or stands in for a slotless fact (`snapshotWins`).
- **FeeConfig owner.** The FeeConfig must be owned by pump-fees.
- **Cost, at the new period.** A held position with no fresh pool fact is read about every 0.7–1.1 s.
  - About 2.2 M CU a day if held around the clock, or about 0.18 M CU per 120-minute hold, against Alchemy's 30 M a month.
  - The watch reads at **P0** (review of #87, risk reviewer). It prices an exit, only for open positions, only while their market is stale, at most T_max each, so the monthly budget's halt (every class but P0, at 70%) never removes the price an exit needs. Its worst case is about 0.18 M CU per position per full-outage hold. Tested: a P0 read goes through at the halt.
  - A trading pool costs the watch no reads, a quiet one does (WATCH-1b, "Released-fact freshness"). Before WATCH-1b this was not so at the defaults: a feed fact is released two slots after receipt (650–950 ms old by design), past `staleMs` 500, so the watch read a held pool every 0.4–0.8 s on a healthy feed.
- **2026-10-04 · Released-fact freshness (WATCH-1b, supervisor ruling (a1'): judged by release, the 0.7 s transition window accepted).** Why: reading on receipt age (a3) would spend Alchemy's free tier, whose budget halt stops entries and voids the dry run; releasing the snapshot outside the feed's horizon (a2) changes the feed's total-order release, too wide for the live dry run's start (a later card if the dry run shows the window matters). Nothing is ever priced past `maxQuoteAgeMs`. The watch judges a feed pool fact by when it was released to the engine, not by its receipt time; its own snapshot by its read time, as before; a flagged fact is no market and is read on the next look.
  - A pool that trades (a fact released at least every `staleMs`) gets no read: on the harness's live feed, a held minute costs nothing after the one read at the open (20 CU; the vault layout once more on a pool's first read).
  - A quiet pool, or a dead feed, is still read. Kept on snapshots, a quiet pool reads about every 0.4 s at the defaults (the snapshot is already `release` 800 ms old when the feed releases it, past `staleMs`): about 50 CU a second, 0.36 M CU per 120-minute hold if it stays quiet throughout. That cost stands; only trading pools became free.
  - The start-up guard (`watchTimingProblem`) checks both bounds, steady below the quote age and transition within the quote age plus one release, and refuses to start when either breaks (with integer settings the steady bound implies the transition one).
  - Quote-age arithmetic at the defaults (stale 500, every 200, latency 400, release = 2 slots × 400 = 800, maxQuoteAge 2000). Steady on snapshots: stale + every + latency + release = 1900 < 2000, the start-up guard as it was. At the moment a pool's facts stop (feed death, or a held pool going quiet after its last swap), the last feed fact may already be one release old: the first snapshot lands by release + stale + every + latency + release = 2700 ms after that fact's receipt. For at most 0.7 s per such transition the market is past the quote age; an exit then waits for a fresh market (EXIT-1d) and is never sent stale or blind. Releasing the snapshot without the feed's horizon would close it (1900), but needs a new release rule in the feed and its replay; not done.
  - Tests that failed before: a healthy held minute and the swaps before the feed dies make no read (the risk review's pin; 298 reads before); the quiet pool is read before its last fact passes the quote age, peaks within the transition bound and stays under the quote age once snapshots arrive; a flagged fact is read within one period. The dead-feed bound holds. The harness's read now answers at a slot behind the tip, as a confirmed read does (it answered 5 slots ahead, so a snapshot outranked every later pool fact).
  - Mutants: receipt age instead of release (killed), the release time set once per mint (killed), a flagged fact's release counted as fresh (killed). Judging a winning snapshot by the pool fact's release instead of its read is equivalent at the defaults (both always stale once released) and is left documented.
- **On POS-1's merge (agreed with its builder):**
  - poolOf null, or a pool fact flagged `partial`, reads at once.
  - The flag is checked only when the pool fact is the newest whole market, so a newer snapshot still wins.
- **Mutation evidence:** 27 of 27 mutants fail the tests. That is the 19 above plus the eight review mutants: timing not tied to the policy, a late answer used, snapshot reserves with the feed's fee fact, the paper world on the pool fact, newest by receipt time only, FeeConfig owner unchecked, settings not passed from the environment, and the paper market mixing the feed's fees.
- **2026-10-04 · Entries stop when the second path cannot serve** (review of #87). The halt reason "second price path unavailable" is raised when no second path is configured, or when its provider's budget is halted. A position entered then could lose its price with no read to recover it; exits go on. Both cases are tested.
- **The feed's observed fee terms.** In the 40%-fall case the held mint also has fee terms observed on its swaps, and the exit is still priced with the snapshot's FeeConfig. The reviewer's mutant (`ctx: this.observedFees(mint) ?? snap.ctx`) fails it. In all, 4 more of 4 mutants fail (that one, the two halt cases, and P1 instead of P0).

- **2026-10-04 · No gap at the open (risk review B1).** Under POS-1 a quiet pool's last fact was read before the entry was sent, so at the open it was already past the quote age (4.85 s against 2 s), and no price stop could fire for up to one watch cycle. Two fixes:
  - The watch also covers an entry in flight (reserved through pending, unknown or confirmed, and on through its fill until the position shows its quantity), on its decision's pool, with the same read rule, so a fresh snapshot already exists when it lands and an alert is not cleared and raised again across the open. An entry that settles unfilled (failed, expired, rejected, cancelled, abandoned) drops out, as does one whose position closed, and the watch stops reading for it.
  - A position that opens is read once at once (`PositionWatch.opened`, from the worker's step), whatever its market's age, for an entry that lands faster than one cycle.
  - Tests: the held market's age, measured from the step the position opens with no skipped window, stays under `maxQuoteAgeMs` (failed before at 4,850 ms); an unlanded entry's market stays fresh while it is in flight and gets no read after it settles; `opened` reads at once, never twice at a time, and not once stopped. The open is read in the step that books it; a closed position gets no read after. A read already in flight for the mint stands in for the open read. Mutants killed: 8 of 8 (no in-flight entries, settled entries kept, confirmed fills left out, closed positions' entries kept, no `opened` call, `opened` a no-op or ignoring the in-flight or stopped guard).
- **2026-10-04 · With POS-1 (merge rule M).** The strategy's `#market` and the worker's `poolOf` (which the paper world and the watch's `marketAt` use) take the newest whole market first: the snapshot when it is newer than the pool fact, by slot then receipt time. Only when the pool fact wins is POS-1's flag checked, and a flagged fact is no market at all, so the watch sees it as stale and reads. Test: a held position whose swap stream gaps, so its pool fact is flagged, with no swap after; a 30% drop seen only by the snapshot fires the stop within `staleMs + everyMs` and one step (measured 600 ms of the 800 ms bound). Mutants killed: the flag check dropped, or run before the snapshot, in the worker or the strategy (3/3). TEST-3's fault cases that are not about the price path use POS-1's `heldPoolFacts` test switch.

## Position market from swaps (POS-1, `packages/core/src/facts/producer.ts`, `packages/core/src/fills/pool.ts`)

- **2026-10-04 · Why.** Live, the pool fact came only from account reads, which are made for candidate evidence and the survival mark. Nothing refreshed a held position's pool after entry, so about 2 s later (`maxQuoteAgeMs`) its state was stale: no price stop, trail or take-profit could fire and exits were booked blocked "pool state is stale". Only T_max and the deployer trigger worked. The harness hid it by publishing a pool fact every step.
- **2026-10-04 · One implementation.** The pool after a swap is the backtest's own replay: `replaySwap` (what `ShiftedPool` runs) on the Buy/SellEvent's pre-trade reserves. `realSwap` in core fills is the single mapping from the logged fields to its input; DATA-1's `readAmm` and the live `swapEventState` both use it. `swapEventState` accepts the result only when it reproduces every amount the event logged (base, LP, protocol, creator and buyback fees, quote with LP fee, or quote out); otherwise there is no state. Evidence: on all 353 golden mainnet PumpSwap swaps the event path and the dataset-row path give the same pool and trade to the unit; the state equals the next swap's pre-trade reserves; base and vault match the measured vault balances (`backtest/test/pool-state.test.ts`).
- **2026-10-04 · Sells and the v2 split.** SellEvent does not log its instruction, so a sell replays as v1 (as the dataset rows already do). The price-setting state (base and vault + virtual) is exact either way; after a `sell_v2` the vault is the v1 lower bound (the retained fees sit in the vault and are offset in virtual). The chain check therefore compares base and effective reserve exactly, and the next swap's event reports the real split. A lower vault only makes the sell-exceeds-vault refusal more conservative.
- **2026-10-04 · The chain.** The producer keeps one chain per pool, based on the last coherent account read (its static fields: index, vaults, LP mint, flags, `accountBytes`). Each confirmed swap after the read's slot moves it and releases the pool fact with the swap's provider, slot and receipt time. Swaps at or before the read's slot are already in the read. Processed swaps never move it. A read answered for an older slot than a swap already seen is not released over the newer state.
- **2026-10-04 · Fail closed.** The state goes stale, and the producer releases the last state flagged `partial` with a `stale` reason, when: the pool's `trades:<pool>` stream has not covered every slot since the base (not started by then, an open gap, a bounded gap, or a cut or undecodable log on its watch); a swap's pre-trade base or effective reserve differs from the chain (a swap or a liquidity change not seen: `reserves mismatch`); or a swap does not reproduce its event. Gates reject a flagged fact (`degraded`); the strategy's market refuses it whatever its age ("pool state flagged partial (...)"), so no exit is priced from it; the paper fill and the dry-run build treat it as unknown. Reserves are never guessed. Recovery: after a gap, the first swap the stream carries with no gap from its own slot on re-bases the chain on its pre-trade reserves (the program's own report). After a mismatch only a new coherent read re-bases it (WATCH-1's second-path read is the fallback).
- **2026-10-04 · Pool watches at confirmed.** `PoolWatch` now opens each pool's logs watch at `confirmed` (FACTS-1's `STREAMS.trades` contract; it was processed, which the producer refuses). The deployer-sell trigger dedups by signature, so a processed copy from another watch and the confirmed one count once. Cost: a swap reaches the strategy when its slot is confirmed rather than processed, typically one to a few slots (~0.4–2 s) later; the backtest already charges this processed → confirmed delay on every observation (BT-1c observation delay: adverse 2 + 6 slots + 1 s), so live and backtest decide on the same commitment.
- **2026-10-04 · No gap at entry (review N1).** A pool that becomes held keeps its watch: `PoolWatch` raises it to P1 in place (`RpcStream.setPriority`) instead of re-subscribing, so the trade stream has no gap at entry and the position is priced from the first swap after it. A watch the stream cannot re-prioritise falls back to unwatch and re-watch (a gap, fail closed). Lowered above P1 while the budget halt is on, a watch is dropped as at the halt.
- **2026-10-04 · Base slot and pruning (review N4, N5).** A read's base slot is its pool fact's `obs.slot` (the oldest account it was built from), so a swap after a vault's older read is never taken as already in it. A pool unwatched with reason `not watched` (it left the strategy's list) drops its chain, after releasing its last state flagged. The per-chain dedup set keeps only the newest swap slot's ids; an older swap is refused anyway.
- **2026-10-04 · Known limits.** A quiet pool: the swap-derived fact ages past `maxQuoteAgeMs` like a read, so an exit sent long after the last swap is booked blocked "pool state is stale" until the next swap or WATCH-1's read. Static fields such as `lpSupply` stay those of the last read. Pump curve: TradeEvents already carry post-trade reserves (golden test), and the strategy enters PumpSwap only (curve recorded, not traded), so no curve position needs a market fact; none is built.
- **2026-10-04 · Tests.** `core/test/facts/pool-chain.test.ts` (each swap releases the next state; processed and in-read swaps ignored; gap, hole, late stream, no stream, mismatch, non-reproducing swap; older read), `worker/test/position-market.test.ts` (on the real worker, with the harness no longer publishing pool facts while a position is held, `Market.heldPoolFacts` being the test-only switch for older tests: with no swap the state ages and nothing fires; a 30% sell fires the price stop with no account read after entry; a 15% buy fires take-profit, then a sell fires the trail; a gap makes the state stale and a later sell prices nothing; a flagged fact is never priced from), `worker/test/facts-parity.test.ts` (encoded log lines through FEED-1 and DEC-1: live, the recorded replay and the backtest re-sort give the same pool facts event by event and the same decision log). Each fails on the base. Guard mutations (after review B1, which found three untested guards): the coverage, gap, mismatch (both base-only and effective), mismatch-sticky, older-read, in-read, older-swap re-base (a late swap after a gap), older-swap on a clean chain, dedup, reproduce, prune, strategy flag, paper-market flag, harness switch, in-place priority and halt-on-lower mutations each fail a test (18 of 18); the earlier survivor (a duplicate coverage check on re-base) was removed. Added tests: a late older swap after a bounded gap does not re-base; a delivery twice stays clean, also when the second arrives after newer swaps; a base-only or effective-only mismatch goes stale; a pool leaving the watch list; the base slot from the fact; an exit decided ~2 s after entry is priced and filled, never booked blocked; the watch raised in place keeps coverage through the halt.

## Risk marks (RISK-MARK, `packages/worker/src/engine/marks.ts`, `packages/core/src/exits/value.ts`)

- **2026-10-04 · Why.** Risk counts an open position with no mark as a total loss and refuses entries while one is unknown or stale; R10's NAV needs every mark. The strategy marked positions for entries only, from the raw full-size sell, and judged exits on the unmarked account: each exit computed the day, week and kill figures as if the position were worth nothing (a $4.50 position would trip the weekly limit on its own exit and latch it).
- **2026-10-04 · The mark (review ruling N1).** `executableMark` (core exits): the full-size sell quote after the pool's fees (`liquidationValue`, exact at the real size), less the worst slippage the exit ladder accepts (the last rung's `minOutBelowTriggerBps`, 2,500 bps in the trial policy: the most an exit is allowed to give up, so the conservative value now) and that rung's exit transaction network cost (signatures × base fee + its priority fee + tip), never below zero. `markSettings(policy, network)` derives these; `markedHistory` values the mark at the SOL price (floor) and dates it at the market's own time. The market is the strategy's `#market`: POS-1's swap-derived pool fact (or a read), and WATCH-1's snapshot once #87 merges.
- **2026-10-04 · Fail closed (review B1).** The mark stays null with no market, a flagged one, a market or SOL price that fails risk's freshness rule (stamped after now, or older than `maxQuoteAgeMs`), or a non-positive SOL price. Risk then keeps refusing entries and counting a total loss, as before marks existed.
- **2026-10-04 · One helper, exits never blocked (review B2, N2).** `riskAccount` is the one path for entries and exits. On an exit, any failure while marking gives back the unmarked account, so marking can never block an exit. On an entry, a failure while marking refuses that candidate (`risk mark failed: <reason>`, logged as a reject) and the worker keeps stepping (review B3: uncaught, it would have stopped the engine and left an open position unmanaged). Exit decisions log the mark risk judged the position with (`risk mark <micro-dollars|unknown>`) and the entry controls tripped then (`risk tripped <codes>`); neither blocks the exit.
- **2026-10-04 · Limits.** None changed. `maxOpen` applies as configured (trial: 1, so a second entry is refused while a position is open whatever the mark); code cannot raise it (`startSession` refuses). A fresh mark only removes `mark_unknown`/`mark_stale` and makes NAV and R10's NAV high-water mark known; a real fall in the mark still trips the daily limit and the kill line.
- **2026-10-04 · Two marks, kept apart (review N3).** This is the executable mark, the only one risk's controls read. WORKER-1c (#99) records day, week and NAV-peak boundary marks from the account; whichever of #99 and #103 merges second switches those to the marked account, and #99's `strategy.markOf` is named a spot value so the two are never confused.
- **2026-10-04 · Known limit.** The account snapshot risk reads is published after each ledger change and released with the next slot: until the fill's snapshot is released (about one slot), a just-filled position is counted as an unresolved entry, so NAV is null and the position counts against `maxOpen` either way.
- **2026-10-04 · Tests.** `worker/test/marks.test.ts`: the mark's arithmetic and bounds; the settings (last rung, its cost); every null case (no market, stale, future, stale or future SOL, no or zero SOL price, nothing held); the exit fallback; and through core risk unchanged, NAV and its high-water mark, the weekly trip an unmarked $4.50 position causes on its exit, a real fall tripping the daily limit, the kill line, entries under maxOpen 1 and an owner-approved 2. `worker/test/position-market.test.ts` on the real worker: an exit priced from swaps logs exactly `executableMark` of the holding on that swap's pool at the ruled settings, with no unknown or stale mark; with the SOL price older than `maxQuoteAgeMs` (swaps keeping the pool fresh) the stop's exit is judged with `risk mark unknown` and `mark_unknown`. Since EXIT-1c a due exit waits for a fresh quote, so no exit is judged on a stale market; market staleness is covered by the unit tests. Mutations, each failing a test: slippage 0, exit cost 0, the priority fee or tip dropped, signatures dropped, the first rung instead of the last, freshness 1e12, SOL freshness dropped, the exit on the unmarked account, the entry's catch removed or the exit wired without the fallback (a worker-level test injects a marking fault through the `markedHistory` seam: the entry is refused and logged, the worker keeps stepping, enters once marking is healthy, and the stop still fires on the unmarked account), the seam ignored, the strategy's settings overridden, and (core) the zero floor, slippage and cost arithmetic. A redundant quantity guard was removed.

## Partial sales in risk (RISK-PARTIAL, `packages/core/src/risk/evaluate.ts`, `packages/worker/src/run/account.ts`)

- **2026-10-04 · Why (audit M3).** After a partial sale the open position kept its full cost, and the sale's result was nowhere in equity. Example: $20, buy $5, sell half for $3, rest marked $3. Cash was $18 and NAV $21, but risk saw $18 equity and a $2 daily loss.
- **2026-10-04 · Allocation.** The basis is the entry SOL plus entry fees, shared by tokens bought. A sale that leaves tokens held realizes its proceeds after its own fees, less the basis share of the tokens sold since the last part. What is still held keeps its share rounded up, so it is never under-costed. Tokens gone without a booked sale keep their basis, so their loss shows in the mark. Each part is valued at the sale's SOL price, or the open's if there is none. With no price at all, a gain counts as nothing and a loss as the part's whole dollar share. Exit fees are no longer added to the held position's basis.
- **2026-10-04 · Risk.** `RealizedPart` is new. `OpenPosition.partials` and `ClosedTrade.partials` are optional, and absent means none. Equity, the day and week loss, the high-water mark and the week base count each part at its own time, and the rest of a closed trade's whole `netPnl` at its close. So a part realized before a boundary stays in that day. `ClosedTrade.netPnl` stays the whole trade's result, so R8, the loss streak and win rate count whole trades. A part dated after now is refused as an invalid history. NAV waits for a balance read at or after the latest part.
- **2026-10-04 · Lifecycle unchanged.** `Position.cost` stays the entry cost and `bought` the tokens bought. The allocation is derived from them in the account, so the book and ledger shapes do not change.
- **2026-10-04 · Saved data.** `account.json` trade records gain `partials` (time, lamports, micro-dollars), `partialSold` and `partialNet`. These are the bot's own trades only, no personal data; supervisor ruling applies. Files from before read as no partials.
- **2026-10-04 · Review fix 1 (golden rule): no SOL price.** With no SOL price at the fill or the open, a part sold at a loss now counts as its share of the notional plus the entry fees, rounded up. The entry fees are converted at the entry's own rate (notional over entry SOL). Before, the share left out the fees, so a partial loss was under-counted until the close.
- **2026-10-04 · Review fix 2 (golden rule): a late sell.** A late-landing sell is booked by `orphan_fill`, as PAPER-1 (#133, 761e1b2) does. The desk's `#fill` now routes an exit's `orphan_fill` into `filled()`, so the part is booked when it lands. Before, the tokens kept their basis until the next booked part, which could move a day's or week's loss across a boundary. Only an exit's orphan is routed: a late buy opens its own position and is not a paper trade. Whichever of #132 and #133 lands second carries this change.
- **2026-10-04 · Limits.** None changed.
- **2026-10-04 · Tests.** `core/test/risk/partials.test.ts` and `worker/test/partial-sale.test.ts` cover the audit example, a losing part counted in the day loss at once, a part before the day boundary, whole-trade statistics, entry fees with two successive parts, rounding, re-booking, the no-price cases and NAV consistency. The take-profit test in `worker/test/position-market.test.ts` now also checks the partial is realized end to end. On the base (3f14e0f), the audit example and every equity test fail. Mutants: 22 tried, 22 killed. Review fixes, both failing on a244707: with no price at all, half sold at a loss counts $2.60 of a $5 notional plus $0.20 of fees (a third: $1.733334, rounded up); a late sell booked by `orphan_fill` on day D after a partial books its part at D, and the close on D+1 realizes only its own small loss, carrying no gain from D. Also: a late buy's `orphan_fill` books no paper trade. Mutants killed: rounding down, fees left out, the orphan not routed, any purpose routed.

## Risk latches from the account valuation (RISK-LATCH, `packages/worker/src/run/worker.ts` `#markAccount`, `#latch`)

- **2026-10-04 · Why (audit M1).** R10 (kill switch) and R9 (weekly loss) were latched only from the trips of an entry or exit evaluation. The routine account valuation (`#markAccount`, every step) computed the same figures and threw the trips away. A NAV breach with no candidate and no position (SOL falling, peak $20, NAV near $8.77) left `control.json` unlatched, and once NAV recovered the breach tripped nothing.
- **2026-10-04 · The fix.** Every valuation that gives a snapshot also runs `evaluateExit` on the same input (the marked account, the SOL price, the current latches). Any trip is latched at that moment through the one `#latch` path that entry and exit records also use. A latch already set keeps its first moment. The trip is logged with equity and NAV, and the account is put again so the next evaluation sees it. Only the owner re-arms or reviews; nothing in code clears a latch. No limit changed.
- **2026-10-04 · Review fix (latch only on a full, fresh valuation).** Risk counts an unknown mark as a total loss: a stand-in for refusing entries, not proof of a breach. A latch lasts the week (R9) or until the owner re-arms (R10), so one null mark (a stale market or SOL price, or the exit's fallback account) must not set it: at trial settings a $2.01 booked loss plus a $2 position with no mark crosses the $4 weekly line. `marks.ts` `latchable` is true only when the SOL price is known and fresh (at or before now, within `maxQuoteAgeMs`) and every open position has a mark and a fresh `markAtMs`. `#markAccount` latches from the valuation only when it holds. The same rule now applies to the exit path (`strategy.ts` `#exitDecision`, already present before this PR): on an account that fails it no `trip` line is emitted, so `#afterRecord` latches nothing; the `risk tripped` codes are still logged. A valuation that fails it changes nothing; the next fresh one decides.
- **2026-10-04 · Observation, no change.** The R10 NAV line is 70% of a high-water mark seeded with opening equity ($20), but NAV leaves out the operations floor (about $17.55 at the trial start). So the NAV measure trips after a trading loss of about $3.55, not $6. That is tighter, never looser; recorded for the risk reviewer.
- **2026-10-04 · Tests.** `worker/test/kill-latch.test.ts`, through the real worker: a NAV breach with nothing held latches R10, which holds after the recovery and across a restart; a booked weekly loss latches R9 on the next valuation with no candidate; a NAV above the line latches nothing. The first two fail on the base (3f14e0f). Mutants killed: no latch from the valuation, no weekly latch, no save to `control.json`. One equivalent: re-stamping a set latch, since risk stops reporting a trip once it is latched. Review fix tests, failing on 23fc039: (a) a held position with no mark whose stand-in crosses the weekly line latches neither R9 nor R10; (c) an exit judged on an unmarked account logs `weekly_loss` among its tripped codes and emits no `trip` line. Passing on both, as guards: (b) the same position with a fresh mark showing a real fall latches R9 from the valuation (fails on 3f14e0f, before RISK-LATCH); a stale SOL price that puts NAV under the kill line latches nothing, and the same price fresh latches R10; `marks.test.ts` `latchable` at each bound (no, stale or future SOL price; no, undated, stale or future mark; exactly `maxQuoteAgeMs` old passes). Mutants killed: drop the mark condition, drop the SOL freshness, drop the SOL null check and freshness, drop the mark freshness, the valuation or the exit latching on an unmarked account, never latching, `latchable` always false.

## Risk faults told apart from a clean account (RISK-FAULT, `packages/core/src/risk/evaluate.ts` `evaluateExit`, `packages/worker/src/engine/strategy.ts`)

- **2026-10-04 · Why.** `evaluateExit` never throws, so an exit is never blocked. But on an internal failure it returned no tripped controls and no trips: the same result as a clean account. A caller could not tell "nothing tripped" from "could not evaluate".
- **2026-10-04 · The fix.** `ExitDecision` gains `fault: string | null`. Null means the account was evaluated. Otherwise it gives why not, `tripped` holds one reason with the new code `risk_fault` (control R1, the account's valuation), and `trips` stays empty. The exit is still allowed, as before. The fault text is read from an `Error`'s message only, inside its own guard, so a hostile thrown value cannot make `evaluateExit` throw. Entries fail closed: `evaluateEntry` keeps its contract (it throws on a malformed clock), and the strategy now catches a throw from it and refuses the candidate as `risk fault: <why>` (gate R1, code `risk_fault`), logged on the reject line, and the step goes on. Before this, the throw left the strategy's step. On an exit the decision lists `risk_fault` among its `risk tripped` codes and adds a `risk fault <why>` line; no `trip` line is written, so nothing is latched from it. No limit, guard or test was loosened.
- **2026-10-04 · The account valuation (after RISK-LATCH, #124).** `#markAccount` runs `evaluateExit` on every valuation and uses its trips to latch. A fault there has no trips, so it latches nothing, and it is no longer silent: the worker logs `Risk could not value the account: <why>` once when an episode starts and `Risk can value the account again.` once when it clears. The state is held in memory, so a restart during a fault logs it again. Tests (`worker/test/risk-fault.test.ts`): a healthy valuation logs nothing; a broken one logs one start over many steps; a recovery logs one clear; a second break logs a second start; and nothing is latched. It fails with no log. Mutants killed: no log; a log on every valuation; no clear line; the state never updated; the state never cleared.
- **2026-10-04 · Tests.** `core/test/risk/controls.test.ts`: a clean account gives `fault: null`. A broken clock, a broken account, a thrown non-`Error`, and an `Error` whose message getter throws each give a fault, the single `risk_fault` reason and no trips. `worker/test/risk-fault.test.ts`, through the real worker with the `markedHistory` seam making the account unreadable to risk: the entry is refused with `risk fault: `, with no `Engine step failed`, and enters once risk can evaluate again. The stop's exit still closes the position, with `risk_fault` as its only tripped code, a `risk fault` reason, no `trip` line and no latch. All of these fail on the base (the entry test with the uncaught `TypeError`). Mutants killed: the catch returning the old clean result; the `risk_fault` reason dropped; `fault` null on the catch; a non-null `fault` on the clean path; the guard around the fault text removed; the message ignored; the strategy's entry catch removed; the exit's fault line dropped.

## Live/replay parity (TEST-1, `packages/worker/src/run/parity.ts`, `packages/worker/scripts/parity.ts`)

- **What is compared.** Each recorded boot of a worker state folder is replayed in its recorded release order through the backtest's engine path: core's `Engine` on `replayRecorded`, core's `FactFeed` (`engineFeed`) and the same `LiveStrategy`, seeded with the boot's journaled seed. Every engine record is turned into a journal line by the same function the live desk uses (`journalFields`, moved out of `Desk` unchanged), encoded as the journal encodes (`jsonText`, `redact`). The live journal's `decision` lines of that boot, with `seq`, `ts` and `boot` dropped, must equal the replay's lines byte for byte. Each boot is replayed N times (10 by default); every replay must equal the first. The first differing line is reported with its index, its event and both texts. The session's ledger is replayed by core's `replayLedgerFile`, handed in by the caller (the CLI, the tests), since worker source never imports ledger internals (core's import guard); a missing or failing ledger fails the session, and so does a folder with no recorded boot.
- **Lines no replay can make** are counted, not compared: `command_refused` (an API command) and `ledger_refused` (a ledger write refusing an event the engine applied). A ledger refusal fails the session: it is a live engine/ledger divergence (review of cd187c0). A refused command does not.
- **Every deciding boot must be replayable (review of cd187c0):** a start line with decisions but no recording or no seed is a boot entry `missing: 'no recording' | 'no seed'` and fails the session; a boot that decided nothing (a reconcile-only run) needs neither.
- **Recorder redaction.** The recorder redacts credential values in recorded frames too and lists each file in its manifest's `coverage_gaps`; a replay cannot match where a value was redacted. The report carries each boot's redaction count, and any redaction fails the session even when the decisions still match: altered inputs are not parity evidence (review of cd187c0). Credentials never belong in market data, so a real session shows 0.
- **One command:** `node --no-warnings packages/worker/scripts/parity.ts <state dir> [--replays N]` rebuilds the session and strategy as `main.ts` does from the journal's start line (policy hash, strategy version, paper edge, entry rule and S0 salt), refuses with exit 2 when they differ from this code or between boots, prints the report as JSON and exits 0 (parity), 1 (a divergence, a nondeterministic replay or a bad ledger). The qualifying dry run's state folder is its real input.
- **Evidence:** `packages/worker/test/parity.test.ts`: a session with an entry and an exit replays ten times identically and its ledger replays; a planted journal change is reported at its exact index and event; a recording missing releases is reported where it runs short; a nondeterministic replay is caught even when its first run matches; two boots across a kill each replay; a corrupted or missing ledger fails; a refused command is excluded; a killed session's plain files replay from whole lines (a torn last line is skipped); no recorded boot fails; redaction parity; recorder redaction counted; the CLI exits 0 and then 1 on a planted change. Mutants of each check (normalising, divergence, replay count, ledger, missing ledger, exclusions, torn line, empty session, boot filter, redaction) fail a test.

## Saved state across restarts (PERSIST-1, `packages/worker/src/persist/state.ts`, `DeployerIndex.snapshot/restore`, `RugLabeller.snapshot/restore`)

- **2026-10-04 · Why.** Without saved state, every boot re-runs the creates backfill. The rehearsal measured 14 boots × a 14-day create backfill ≈ 4,150 credits each, projecting 11.9M Helius credits a month. Saving the deployer index, the labeller's tables and the coverage facts makes the RPC fill run once per host; each later restart tops up only the downtime. The data is public chain data and our own labels only (supervisor approval under the stored-data ruling).
- **2026-10-04 · File.** One JSON file: `{ version, sha256, payload }`. The payload holds the as-of moment, `DeployerIndex.snapshot(asOf, retainFromMs?)`, `RugLabeller.snapshot()` and every released `coverage:*` fact, with bigints written as `{ "$bigint": "…" }`. It is written atomically (a temporary file, fsync, rename), so a crash leaves the old file whole. `saveState` refuses a fact after the moment, or an index taken at another moment.
- **2026-10-04 · As-of honesty.** Nothing in the file may be after its moment: not in event order, and not by receipt time either (a fact with an earlier slot but a later receipt is refused). The index snapshot refuses a moment before its last event. The restore applies the same rule everywhere: the index's first and last moments, its entries and the labeller's launches (`createdAtMs`) may not be after the saved moment, by event order or by receipt time. Pruning entries older than `retainFromMs` moves the index's own start up to that time, so the restored index never claims to have watched what it no longer holds.
- **2026-10-04 · Restore.** `loadState(path, rugConfig, continuing?)` restores the index and the labeller. A labeller saved under another rug config version is refused, because its running sums were judged by other rules. It returns the saved coverage plus, for every started via that was covered at the save, an open `restart` gap at the saved moment. The only exception is the seed's backfill via, which no live stream continues. This holds whether or not the restart continues that watch, so a stream nothing restarts stays not covered (#71 review: excluding it had let it read as covered across the downtime). `continuing(stream, via)` only filters the fill plan. A crash leaves no gap of its own, so without this the downtime would read as covered. It also returns a fill plan: one `RestartFill` per open gap (`stream`, `via`, `fromSlot`, `at`, `synthesized`). A gap already open at the save is planned as it was, not doubled.
- **2026-10-04 · Top-up.** For creates: `buildSeed({ days: [], fill: { fromSlot: asOf.slot, fromMs: asOf.receivedAt, close: { via, fromSlot, at }, liveStart }, … })`, then `index.fill(creates, now)`. For trades: `fillTradeGaps` with the same close. The fill reads only from the saved moment, or an older open gap, to the live start; the test fails if anything before the save is fetched. One fill closes the restart gap, and coverage stays continuous (tested end to end).
- **2026-10-04 · The rug half after a restart.** The `coverage:rugs` restart gap is closed only by something that re-establishes rug coverage over the downtime. The labeller missed the downtime's trades, and no fill reconstructs them for every mint. The named closer is RUG-1c's on-demand per-deployer rug check. Until it exists, the rug half of H14 stays not covered after any restart (fail safe). That makes RUG-1c a dependency of the qualifying dry run with restart drills (supervisor, 2026-10-04).
- **2026-10-04 · Discard.** A missing, corrupt (checksum), truncated, unparsable, version-mismatched or malformed file, or one claiming anything after its moment, is discarded whole. The caller then starts as a fresh process, unseeded and not covered, never "clean".
- **2026-10-04 · Daily budget.** `DailyBudget` keeps the fill's credits per UTC day in its own small file, so it survives a discarded state. It saves on every spend. A missing file is a first boot (the full budget). An unreadable one counts today as spent, because a corrupt budget must not let a reboot loop spend again.
- **2026-10-04 · Evidence.** Tests: a restart restores without re-fetching (index and labeller equal, top-up from the saved moment only, coverage continuous after it), as-of honesty (not covered without the top-up; nothing after the save), an open gap carried as it was, pruning honesty, discards (corrupt, truncated, missing, version, rug config, late fact, late mint, moved moment, bad launch), and the budget. All 21 mutations of the guards fail the tests, the #71 review round included. Since RUG-1c (#74) each label carries its kind (the rule that made it, or null) and each launch its peak venue state. Both are saved and restored exactly, round-trip tested, and a malformed one discards the file. `peakState` must be present. A venue state with a zero peak is refused, because the labeller sets it only when a level beats the peak. A peak with a null state is kept: the labeller produces it for a curve trade without virtual reserves or a pool trade without base reserves (supervisor approval: it adds only fields of public labels the index already holds).

## Live dry run trades on paper (WORKER-1e, `run/sim-read.ts`, core `S0DiagnosticPart`)

- **2026-10-04 · Why.** The audit found the live dry run could not make a paper trade: H15 had no simulation fact, the regime needs exec-health (green only with owner limits) and 28 published days of curve volume, and H14 needs 14 days of creates coverage. Supervisor rulings (a)–(d), 2026-10-04.
- **2026-10-04 · H15 live.** A candidate whose last evaluation names the `sim` input (H16 evidence) gets one simulation from LiveFacts, at the spend that evaluation sized (`Candidate.spend`), at most once a minute per mint. `simReader` quotes the exact local round trip on the pool the worker prices from, reads pool, mint, GlobalConfig and Rent at P2 at or after the feed head, and calls SIM-1's `RoundTripSimulator` (P2, the first `ZEROED_STANDINS` address as fee payer, `sigVerify` off, never sent). The answer goes onto the feed as `read:sim:<mint>`, so a replay rebuilds the fact. At most `SIM_READS_PER_HOUR` (120) start in any rolling hour, about 3 Helius credits each. Every attempt is journaled as `h15_sim` (SIM-1's `SimRecord`, or `not-run` with its reason); `h15_sim` is not item 4's `simulation`. Without a stand-in nothing is simulated and H15 rejects, as before.
- **2026-10-04 · S0 diagnostic set (`ZEROED_S0_DIAGNOSTIC=on`).** Exactly one set with four parts (the fourth, `regime-survival`, by supervisor ruling the same day); each relaxes only its own check:
  - `regime-volume`: the curve-volume condition is computed and logged, not judged.
  - `regime-survival`: the graduate-survival condition likewise (it needs 15 days of graduates; PERSIST-2 keeps them across restarts); SOL change is judged as before.
  - `exec-health`: execution health is measured from paper's own attempts (`PaperWorld.execStats`: settled attempts sent in the last 24 h, restart losses left out, landing delay from the paper height at send, quote error of each fill) and published every 10 s as `read:exec-health`; the regime logs it, never judges it.
  - `h14-creates-coverage`: when the 14-day look-back is not covered, H14 judges the deployer over the unbroken creates coverage up to now (an open gap still rejects) and notes it; the serial-deployer count, the rug half (labeller coverage or RUG-1c's on-demand check) and everything else stay real.
  - Each part is named on every decision line that relied on it (`s0_diagnostic`), on the `start` line, in `/health` and in the runner's journal report (counts per part); a reject line is written again when only the parts it relied on change. `REPORT.md` prints "H15 simulations: N run of M, C Helius credits" under the quota table. Refused unless `ZEROED_STRATEGY=S0`, refused in any release that names a qualifying run (whatever the run id), and the runner's qualifying guard fails a boot that carries it.
- **2026-10-04 · Exit flow.** EXIT-1's negative-flow trigger reads one-minute net SOL flow on a held mint's pool from released swaps (BuyEvent `quoteAmountIn` − SellEvent `quoteAmountOut`), deduplicated by signature, user and amounts. Kept in memory: after a restart the run of negative minutes starts again, so the trigger can fire later, never earlier. The still-open minute is left out twice: by the strategy (`closedFlow`) and again by EXIT-1's `negativeRun`, so a refactor of either alone cannot let it count (tests: `closedFlow` passes only finished minutes; exits.test's "not finished one millisecond earlier"; end to end, a large sale early in the fifth minute does not fire until that minute ends).
- **2026-10-04 · Known gaps.** `sellRoute` stays null, so `no_route` cannot fire; card EXIT-ROUTE wires it before the qualifying run, after the Jupiter P0 budget is measured. Survival needs 15 days of graduates (`survivalMedianDays` 14 plus the last 24 h), built live from migrations and survival reads and not saved across restarts, so on a fresh host, and after each restart, the regime would stay off for about 15 days; the shakedown logs it through `regime-survival`, and card PERSIST-2 saves and seeds the series for the qualifying run.
- **2026-10-04 · Open owner item.** Exec-health limits (`ExecHealthLimits`: minimum attempts, failure share, landing delay, quote error) are live risk limits and the owner's. Before the qualifying run the supervisor brings a proposal with the shakedown's measured figures.
- **2026-10-04 · Creates coverage across restarts.** The qualifying run needs 14 days of continuous creates coverage on the host (about 18 Oct from a start tonight). In code a restart keeps it continuous only when the downtime fill completes within 90 s (worker-flow test "a restart reloads the saved coverage and fills the downtime"); a fill that fails or times out leaves a lossy gap, which restarts the 14-day count. Not yet confirmed on the real host: check each restart's `Deployer index: fill` log line.
- **2026-10-04 · Evidence.** `packages/core/test/gates/s0-diagnostic.test.ts` (each part relaxes only its check, reports itself, and nothing changes without the set); `packages/worker/test/worker-1e.test.ts` (on live-like facts, no curve volume, no graduates, no exec-health, 2-day creates coverage, H15 simulated by the fact source: no entry without the set; with it an entry, each entry simulated, the parts named on the lines, start, `/health` and report; the qualifying guard and config refuse it; negative flow fires on net selling, not on net buying, a twice-released swap counts once); `sim-read.test.ts` (spend, slot, stand-in, hourly cap, every skip recorded); `facts-source.test.ts` (sim read at the judged spend; exec stats published only when wired). Mutants checked: no diagnostic in the strategy, survival judged under the set, no spend on the candidate, no `sim` read kind, flow off, no swap dedupe.

## Worker faults that crashed it (WORKER-CRASH, `providers/canonical.ts`, `run/worker.ts`, `run/delay-probe.ts`, `main.ts`)

- **2026-10-04 · A transaction DEC-1 cannot decode is a fact gap, never a crash.** `eventsOfFrame` turns it into one `tx:undecodable` event (id `txerr:<signature>`), the way the logs path already makes `logs:undecodable`. It makes no `ev:` event, so a cut creates log it was fetched for stays a hole in the deployer index. `fetchTx` reads it as not found, so a cut trade log still becomes a `coverage:rugs:gap`. Before, the DecodeError went from `LiveFeed.advance` through the step to the loop's crash path.
- **2026-10-04 · A loop crash ends the process with exit code 1.** The worker exposes `stopping` and `stopped`. `main.ts` exits with the stop's code on every stop (a signal, a refused start, a loop crash), with the 25 s hard exit from the moment a stop begins. Before, `main.ts` never exited after a loop crash: the process exited only once every handle closed, and a crash during the seed made `start` report `stopped during the start` with code 0. `stop` called again returns the first stop's code, and a stop that throws half way ends as a crash.
- **2026-10-04 · A recorder that cannot write (ENOSPC) halts entries, never the process.** Every recorder call goes through `#record`. The first throw, at boot or in any write (including inside a socket handler's ingest), sets the fault: recording stops for the rest of the process, entries halt with `recorder failed (<code>): recording stopped, entries off until a restart`, `/health` shows `recorder: off` and the reason in `critical` (the watchdog's `worker_critical` alert), and the journal gets one `alert` line `recorder_failed`. From the moment the fault is found, the desk refuses every entry at its reservation (`entriesBlocked`, a journaled `entry_refused` line and a reject), ahead of the halt fact, which reaches the engine only at a later advance. So no entry decided at or after the fault proceeds, whether the fault comes inside a step or at an ingest between steps (#148 review B1; exits never reserve, so they are not affected). Exits and the rest of the worker go on (test: a held position exits on its stop after the fault). The boot's open files stay plain; the next start seals them, as after a kill. No automatic resume: a recording with a silent hole would break the parity replay, so a restart starts a new boot folder. Out of scope here: a full disk also fails the journal and the ledger. Those still crash, and systemd's StartLimitBurst (10 in 600 s) caps that loop, after which the missing heartbeat alerts.
- **2026-10-04 · The delay probe survives a sample it cannot record.** `record` runs in a try/finally: a throw drops that sample (reported through `onError`), and the next tick samples again. Before, `#inFlight` stayed true for good and, live, the rejected `sample()` reached `unhandledRejection`, which exits 1.
- **2026-10-04 · Refused API commands are bounded in the journal** (card 5). At most `COMMAND_LINES_PER_MINUTE` (10) `command_refused` lines a minute. The rest are counted on one line when the next minute's first refusal comes, or at the first heartbeat after the minute when the flood has stopped (review N2). A command name is cut to 32 characters. `worker-api.test.ts` now checks the position endpoint while the trade is open: its `if (pos !== null)` never ran, because risk refuses the second entry after the loss.
- **Evidence.** `packages/worker/test/worker-crash.test.ts` (17 tests) and `worker-api.test.ts` (flood test). The first 11 fail on the base source. The B1 entry tests (a fault in the entering step, at the ingest before it, in the step before it) fail with the gate off. The B2 tests pin a stop that throws (exits 1) and a refused start of the real entry process (exits 1, a child process with the health port taken). Hand mutants M1–M18 and the review's W9, M2, plus N2 and B1 mutants are killed, except M12, which is equivalent (the catch already resets `#inFlight`).

## Paper settlement equals the backtest's (PAPER-1, `packages/core/src/fills/settle.ts`, `packages/worker/src/run/account.ts`, `run/paper-world.ts`, `run/api.ts`)

From the external audit of d92b73e (items M4, M5 and M8's rent part). One settlement module in core; the backtest's world, trade records and report and the paper world, account and app API all call it, so a paper trade settles exactly as the historical backtest scores it.

- **2026-10-04 · Fees per signature, failed ones included (M4).** Every attempt's fee comes from its outcome through `feeParts` (a landed failure pays base and priority, a fill base, priority and tip, anything else nothing; the audit's 500,000 + 5,000 case is 505,000). A paper trade's net is recomputed from its signature-keyed attempts in paper.json and the wallet moves by the change, so a replayed or duplicate signature never charges twice. The account settles when a book event lands, when an attempt lands failed, at the end of the start reconcile and at the first SOL price.
- **2026-10-04 · Fees of an entry that never filled are an account cost (M4).** They are the backtest's `StrayCost`: risk's new `AccountCost` kind `failed_entry` (it lowers equity and counts toward the day's and week's loss, never as a trade, so R8, R11, R15 and statistics do not see it; risk review required). Stored in account.json `strayFees` (by signature: send time, lamports, micro-dollars at the booking SOL price, rounded up) and `strayFolded`. Supervisor-approved under the stored-data ruling (the bot's own fees, no personal data). Bounded: records from before the current Melbourne week fold into one total dated at the latest folded record (moving those costs later can only lower equity in between, the safe side; the day and week windows never reach them). Every attempt sent at or before that date is in the total, so a signature seen again is not charged; an entry still unresolved holds the fold back to before its first attempt, and so does an ended one whose fee is not booked yet (no SOL price, as in a new process before its first price; risk review of #133): before, the fold could pass it and its fee was then never charged. A stray cost's dollars round up.
- **2026-10-04 · Dollars at each flow's own SOL price (M5).** Paper uses `tradeUsd`, the backtest report's rule: the entry, the entry leg's costs and the rent paid at the entry's SOL price; the proceeds, the exit leg's costs and the rent returned at the close's. The audit's case (buy 0.02 SOL at $100, sell for 0.024 SOL at $80) books −$0.08, not +$0.32. The app's trade record and statistics report the SOL result (`netSol`) and split the dollar result exactly into `tradingUsd` (the SOL result at the close's price) and `solMoveUsd` (SOL's own move over the trade). Same approximation as the report: every attempt of a leg at that leg's price. A trade with an unknown SOL price still books its notional as lost. Every term rounds against us (AUDIT-RM1 F5, 5 Oct): what we pay (the entry, every cost, the rent paid) up, what we receive (the proceeds, the rent returned) and the results down, so a trade's dollar result is at or below its exact value, never above; `toUsd` takes the direction. The backtest report's stray costs round up too.
- **2026-10-04 · Rent follows the account-close outcome in paper too (M8, RENT-1).** `TokenAccounts` (moved from the backtest world) keeps one account per mint: a sell of the whole balance closes it in the same transaction unless it is sell-only; a failed close fails that attempt (fee paid) and makes the account sell-only; a new account may pick up dust. `tradeRent` charges the rent to the entry's first trade and returns it only when one of its sells closed the account. The paper world draws dust and the close from seeds of the signature alone, so a restart under a new boot rebuilds the same state from paper.json (`restore`: a filled attempt is applied again without a new draw, a recorded close failure makes the account sell-only); the dry-run simulation leg closes the account only when the account would close. The engine still plans a clean account (`strategy.ts`); where the close happens decides.
- **Before live: late landings and ambiguous results after a crash.** Paper attempts die with their process; a real transaction can land after a restart, or its result can be unknown. Recovering its fee, fill and account state from the chain belongs to the signer work and is out of PAPER-1's scope.
- **2026-10-04 · Two settlement gaps (fixed by PAPER-2, below).** (a) A failed exit attempt that lands after its trade closed did not reach the wallet. (b) A main trade closed before a sibling's sell closes the shared account never booked its returned rent.
- **2026-10-04 · A late-landing sell closes its paper trade (risk review of #133).** The desk hands the account a sell booked by `orphan_fill` after its exit ended, as it does a reconcile's fills, so the trade closes and risk sees its result. Before, the wallet took the sell at the next settle but the trade stayed open, its loss outside the closed-trade history. A late buy's own position (`<position>.o<n>`) still opens no paper trade; it is LEDGER-1b's live case and goes with the late landings above.
- **2026-10-04 · A late sell keeps its stop and closes its trade (run/CI review of #133, B1 and B2).** The desk keeps each exit intent's trigger reasons when it is triggered and uses them for that exit's late sell, whose owner the abandon cleared (or handed to a second exit); so a late stop counts as a stop (R8's 24 h re-entry block). A late sell that leaves the position at quantity 0 closes the trade whatever the position's status (supervisor ruling: the desk, not the core lifecycle): a second exit that owns the position then can only end unfilled, and the trade must not stay open at quantity 0. PAPER-2 (below) fixed what was left: a late sell of a trade already closed now updates its result, and the kept reasons survive a restart.
- **2026-10-04 · A late buy stops entries until paper settles it (risk ruling on #133).** A buy booked by `orphan_fill` after its entry ended opens its own position (`<position>.o<n>`), which paper does not settle: its cost reaches the wallet only when the parent trade is next settled (never if the parent already closed), and its sale books no trade. Until the late-landing card settles it, the desk hands such a fill to `lateBuy` (never to the trade records), the worker journals one critical `late_buy` alert, and entries halt with the reason "late buy not settled by paper; entries off" while the book holds a late-buy position, so a restart keeps the halt.
